# Apologetics II: The Catholic Claims · Reference Card

> Open book. This card is meant to be open while you work — including during
> quizzes. Nothing here is something you're expected to recall cold.

This course argues the Catholic side of the disputes among Christians (Scripture and authority, justification, the papacy and orders, the Orthodox questions, the contested distinctives, the hard history) on ground every party already shares: the creeds, the Trinity and the Incarnation, Scripture as inspired. Its machinery is four habits. **State the other side first**, as its own confession states it, article and edition named, and get that statement right before any reply: a Lutheran, Reformed, Anglican, Zwinglian, Baptist or Orthodox position is never "the Protestant view". **Name the concession** the Catholic case owes before making the case. **Mark every Catholic claim on the ladder**, no higher and no lower: find the act, read canons as worded and chapters at least rung 3, and remember that whether a named confession falls under a canon is rung 5 unless an act has said so. **Mark every opposing text's weight inside its own tradition**, never with a Catholic rung: the Book of Concord is a *norma normata* under Scripture, Westminster a subordinate standard, the Articles formularies whose standing varies across the Anglican Communion, Mogila and Dositheus of limited authority. A few facts the lessons verified recur everywhere: no act condemns *sola scriptura* by name; Trent condemns propositions and names no confession; Florence defined the filioque addition lawful; no act names Palamas; *Apostolicae Curae* is an apostolic letter, not a bull; the 1616 decree and the 1633 sentence defined nothing. The card holds the ladder and every level the lessons mark, every other tradition's text with its standing at home, how to read the citations and their numbering traps, the confessions themselves, the vocabulary, the positions and distinctions, the argument skeletons (Boss parts at outline only), the concessions, the thinkers, what is assumed from sibling courses, and the traps. The ladder belongs to [`fundamental-theology` 4.4](../fundamental-theology/lessons/04-04-the-ladder-of-doctrinal-authority.md); where that course and this card differ, that course wins.

## Authority and citations

### Levels of authority

**The ladder, as this course uses it** ([`fundamental-theology` 4.4](../fundamental-theology/lessons/04-04-the-ladder-of-doctrinal-authority.md)). **Rung 1**: proposed as divinely revealed, by solemn judgment (a canon with its anathema, a *definimus* or *diffinimus*) or by the ordinary universal magisterium; owed faith, its denial heresy (can. 751). **Rung 2**: definitive, to be held, not revealed: truths connected with revelation, including [dogmatic facts](#dogmatic-fact); its denial sets one against definitive teaching (can. 750 §2) but is not heresy. **Rung 3**: authentic ordinary magisterium, owed religious submission of intellect and will. Then **the seam**: rungs 4 and 5 are not lower grades of a magisterial act but theologians' notes on claims no act settles. **Rung 4**: theologically certain, the schools' common teaching. **Rung 5**: freely disputed among Catholics. Off the ladder: **no magisterial level** (Fathers, Doctors and theologians writing as authors, commissions, dialogue texts); **no Catholic level** (every other tradition's text, whose weight is stated inside that tradition: see [Weight inside other traditions](#weight-inside-other-traditions)); **law, discipline or liturgical law, no rung** (binding and changeable, not doctrine).

**The procedure.** Find the **act**, not the book you read the sentence in. Ask what the act proposes the claim **as**. Read off the assent. If the level is not manifest, **default down**: can. 749 §3, nothing is understood as infallibly defined unless that is manifestly established. Genre is evidence, not verdict ([`fundamental-theology` 4.5](../fundamental-theology/lessons/04-05-reading-a-documents-weight.md)); the Catechism **locates and fixes no level**, each paragraph keeping its source's weight. Overstating and understating are both errors, graded alike: "Trent condemned Westminster" and "only the canons count, so ch. 9 binds no one" fail together [3.2](lessons/03-02-trent-session-vi-against-the-confessions.md).

**Three rules this course leans on.**

- **Canons and chapters.** A canon is **rung 1 as worded**, in the sense it states; a chapter clause no canon repeats is **at least rung 3**, and rung 1 only where another act carries the same content. Trent VI's fence binds Catholics to its chapters' doctrine without making every sentence a definition ([chapters and canons](#chapters-and-canons)) [3.2](lessons/03-02-trent-session-vi-against-the-confessions.md).
- **Application is a second judgment.** Whether a named text asserts what a canon condemns is **rung 5** unless an act has applied the canon; no act applies Trent's canons to Westminster, the Formula, Article VI, Chemnitz or Calvin, and the *Joint Declaration* §41 judges only the Lutheran teaching it presents ([application of a condemnation](#application-of-a-condemnation)) [2.1](lessons/02-01-sola-scriptura-at-its-best.md), [2.3](lessons/02-03-tradition-and-what-sufficiency-means.md), [2.4](lessons/02-04-perspicuity-tested.md), [3.1](lessons/03-01-justification-as-the-confessions-state-it.md), [3.2](lessons/03-02-trent-session-vi-against-the-confessions.md), [4.4](lessons/04-04-the-visible-church-and-what-replaced-the-papacy.md), [6.4](lessons/06-04-the-real-presence.md).
- **Global censures fix no note.** The contradictory of a proposition condemned *in globo* is **at least rung 3, no precise note**; calling its denial heresy overstates, calling the question open understates ([in globo censure](#in-globo-censure)) [4.4](lessons/04-04-the-visible-church-and-what-replaced-the-papacy.md), [7.1](lessons/07-01-the-inquisition-and-the-galileo-affair.md).

**Phrases this card uses**, exactly as the lessons mark them.

- *Rung 1 as worded*: a canon or definition read for what its words reach and no further: Trent XXIV can. 7, "whether it defined indissolubility as revealed being disputed" [5.5](lessons/05-05-marriage-divorce-and-the-state-of-the-dead.md); XXII can. 8 on private Masses [1.3](lessons/01-03-arguing-from-history-without-abusing-it.md), [6.5](lessons/06-05-the-sacrifice-of-the-mass.md); Trent IV's "equal ... reverence", equal in reverence, not extent [2.3](lessons/02-03-tradition-and-what-sufficiency-means.md). *Rung 1 in substance*: "nothing preceding justification merits it", carried by VI cans. 1–3 with Orange II while ch. 8's own wording stays at least rung 3 [3.3](lessons/03-03-paul-and-james.md). *Rung 1 for the change and the term, not for Aristotle*: transubstantiation [1.3](lessons/01-03-arguing-from-history-without-abusing-it.md), [6.4](lessons/06-04-the-real-presence.md). *Rung 1 by the ordinary universal magisterium*: perpetual virginity, with no single defining act [6.1](lessons/06-01-mary.md). *Rung 1 if ex cathedra, at least rung 3 regardless*: *Benedictus Deus*, whose standing is open [5.4](lessons/05-04-essence-and-energies.md).
- *Rung 2*: a defined judgment on something connected with the faith but not revealed: the lawfulness of the filioque addition [5.1](lessons/05-01-the-filioque-two-questions-kept-apart.md); *Apostolicae Curae*, as the CDF's 1998 *Doctrinal Commentary* (§11) classifies it [4.3](lessons/04-03-apostolic-succession-and-the-validity-of-orders.md); Vatican I's legitimacy as an ecumenical council [5.3](lessons/05-03-vatican-i-read-by-orthodox-theologians.md). *At least rung 2*: the indissolubility of a ratified and consummated marriage by any human power [5.5](lessons/05-05-marriage-divorce-and-the-state-of-the-dead.md).
- *At least rung 3*: chapter text no canon repeats; Vatican II's restatements, which define nothing new; the contradictory of a global censure; a floor under a disputed higher reading (the addition's lawfulness, "so it is not freely disputed" [5.1](lessons/05-01-the-filioque-two-questions-kept-apart.md)). *At least rung 3, not open to denial*: Trent XXII chs. 1–2, the once-for-all premise and the identity formula [6.5](lessons/06-05-the-sacrifice-of-the-mass.md).
- *Rung 3*: encyclicals, conciliar decrees' own teaching, dicastery acts with papal approval. Grades the lessons use: *rung 3 at its heaviest* (LG 22 on the college [4.1](lessons/04-01-the-petrine-texts-argued.md), [5.3](lessons/05-03-vatican-i-read-by-orthodox-theologians.md); *Mystici Corporis* 14 [4.4](lessons/04-04-the-visible-church-and-what-replaced-the-papacy.md)); *rung 3, light grade* (the 2007 *Doctrinal Note* [1.2](lessons/01-02-what-the-church-says-about-other-christians.md); *Communionis Notio* on priority [5.2](lessons/05-02-papal-primacy-as-the-east-reads-it.md); the 1998 commentary's classifications, "at most" [4.2](lessons/04-02-what-the-patristic-record-proves.md), [4.3](lessons/04-03-apostolic-succession-and-the-validity-of-orders.md)); *the lightest grade of rung 3* (the 1616 censure while in force; John Paul II's 1992 address for principles it restates [7.1](lessons/07-01-the-inquisition-and-the-galileo-affair.md)); *rung 3 at most* (the 1995 clarification, which "defines nothing (no rung; rung 3 at most)" [5.1](lessons/05-01-the-filioque-two-questions-kept-apart.md)). *Rung 3 by papal approval*: the German bishops' 1875 declaration [5.3](lessons/05-03-vatican-i-read-by-orthodox-theologians.md).
- *Rung 4*: a school's explanation, never defined: the Latin relational argument [5.1](lessons/05-01-the-filioque-two-questions-kept-apart.md); grace a "quality" [5.4](lessons/05-04-essence-and-energies.md); privation as original sin's essence [6.1](lessons/06-01-mary.md); *condign* and *congruous*, "words no act uses" [3.4](lessons/03-04-the-joint-declaration-merit-and-reward.md).
- *Rung 5*: freely disputed. It includes every **application** question (above), every account of a mechanism the Church leaves open (how the sacrifice is made present [6.5](lessons/06-05-the-sacrifice-of-the-mass.md); purgatory's fire, place and duration [5.5](lessons/05-05-marriage-divorce-and-the-state-of-the-dead.md), [6.3](lessons/06-03-penance-purgatory-and-indulgences.md)), and exegesis inside a defined fence (the rock [4.1](lessons/04-01-the-petrine-texts-argued.md); James 2:21–24 inside can. 24 [3.3](lessons/03-03-paul-and-james.md); *porneia* inside XXIV can. 7 [5.5](lessons/05-05-marriage-divorce-and-the-state-of-the-dead.md)).
- *Not taught*: no act teaches the claim at all: that Scripture is obscure [2.4](lessons/02-04-perspicuity-tested.md). *Not defined*: "Co-redemptrix" [6.1](lessons/06-01-mary.md); whether Mary died [6.1](lessons/06-01-mary.md); the eucharistic reading of John 6 (Trent XXI ch. 1) [6.4](lessons/06-04-the-real-presence.md). *Taught, not defined*: *Pastor Aeternus*'s histories, Philip's sentence included, and ch. 4's use of Luke 22:32 for the see [4.1](lessons/04-01-the-petrine-texts-argued.md), [4.2](lessons/04-02-what-the-patristic-record-proves.md). *Defines nothing of itself*: Michael VIII's profession at Lyons II [5.5](lessons/05-05-marriage-divorce-and-the-state-of-the-dead.md). *Not manifestly defined*: *Unam Sanctam*'s closing sentence [4.2](lessons/04-02-what-the-patristic-record-proves.md); Florence's 1442 list of books [2.2](lessons/02-02-the-canon-question.md).
- *Carries what it restates*: a text whose weight is its sources': the Catechism; the *Letter to the People of God*, whose judgment on clericalism is pastoral [7.3](lessons/07-03-the-abuse-crisis-and-the-argument-from-scandal.md). *A request, not a teaching*: *Ut Unum Sint*'s invitation [5.2](lessons/05-02-papal-primacy-as-the-east-reads-it.md), [5.3](lessons/05-03-vatican-i-read-by-orthodox-theologians.md); the 1998 Catholic Response's §7 [3.3](lessons/03-03-paul-and-james.md). *Official, no rung of its own*: the *Joint Declaration* and the Official Common Statement with its Annex, acts of the Church as a party [3.4](lessons/03-04-the-joint-declaration-merit-and-reward.md). *Below the seam*: *The Bishop of Rome* (2024) [5.2](lessons/05-02-papal-primacy-as-the-east-reads-it.md), which [5.3](lessons/05-03-vatican-i-read-by-orthodox-theologians.md) marks **no magisterial level**.
- *No rung*, *no level*, *not a level*: a historians' judgment (whether the record by itself proves jurisdiction [4.2](lessons/04-02-what-the-patristic-record-proves.md)); a historical fact (Honorius's anathema [4.2](lessons/04-02-what-the-patristic-record-proves.md)); a judicial act (the 1633 sentence [7.1](lessons/07-01-the-inquisition-and-the-galileo-affair.md)); unpublished advice (the 1616 consultors [7.1](lessons/07-01-the-inquisition-and-the-galileo-affair.md)); a label ("deuterocanonical", "no rank" [2.2](lessons/02-02-the-canon-question.md)); school words (*dulia*, *hyperdulia*, "relative *latria*" [6.1](lessons/06-01-mary.md), [6.2](lessons/06-02-the-saints-the-images-the-relics.md)). *Disputed, no level*: *Haec Sancta* [4.4](lessons/04-04-the-visible-church-and-what-replaced-the-papacy.md). *No authority*: the forgeries [4.2](lessons/04-02-what-the-patristic-record-proves.md).
- *Law, no rung*; *discipline*; *liturgical law*: the Vulgate decree [2.2](lessons/02-02-the-canon-question.md); communion in one kind and withholding the cup [1.3](lessons/01-03-arguing-from-history-without-abusing-it.md), [7.2](lessons/07-02-the-reformations-grievances.md); the 1607 formula [2.4](lessons/02-04-perspicuity-tested.md); the first-millennium canons (Nicaea c. 6, Sardica, Chalcedon cc. 9 and 28, Constantinople I c. 3, Apostolic Canon 34) [4.2](lessons/04-02-what-the-patristic-record-proves.md), [5.2](lessons/05-02-papal-primacy-as-the-east-reads-it.md); the Ephesian decree on the creed and reciting it without the clause [5.1](lessons/05-01-the-filioque-two-questions-kept-apart.md); Palamas in Byzantine Catholic calendars [5.4](lessons/05-04-essence-and-energies.md); nullity's grounds and procedure [5.5](lessons/05-05-marriage-divorce-and-the-state-of-the-dead.md); every Tridentine reform decree and indulgence norm [6.3](lessons/06-03-penance-purgatory-and-indulgences.md), [6.5](lessons/06-05-the-sacrifice-of-the-mass.md), [7.2](lessons/07-02-the-reformations-grievances.md); *Ad extirpanda* and the 1616 book bans [7.1](lessons/07-01-the-inquisition-and-the-galileo-affair.md); *Vos estis*, the 2019 rescript and Book VI [7.3](lessons/07-03-the-abuse-crisis-and-the-argument-from-scandal.md); the Code's canons throughout.

**The levels in this course, in one place.** Each lesson's own entry has the act; the links after each group name the lessons.

- **Rung 1.** The creedal core: the Son *homoousios*, one person in two natures (Nicaea, Constantinople, Chalcedon) [1.1](lessons/01-01-shared-ground-and-where-the-argument-is.md); *Theotokos* (Ephesus) [6.1](lessons/06-01-mary.md). Peter's primacy of true jurisdiction, not of honour only; perpetual successors, the Roman pontiff; full, supreme, ordinary and immediate jurisdiction, communion with Peter's successor a bond of unity; infallibility under ch. 4's conditions with *ex sese* (*Pastor Aeternus* chs. 1–4; Florence's primacy definition of 1439, its *quemadmodum* disputed; LG 22's restatement) [1.1](lessons/01-01-shared-ground-and-where-the-argument-is.md), [1.3](lessons/01-03-arguing-from-history-without-abusing-it.md), [4.1](lessons/04-01-the-petrine-texts-argued.md), [4.2](lessons/04-02-what-the-patristic-record-proves.md), [4.4](lessons/04-04-the-visible-church-and-what-replaced-the-papacy.md), [5.2](lessons/05-02-papal-primacy-as-the-east-reads-it.md), [5.3](lessons/05-03-vatican-i-read-by-orthodox-theologians.md), [7.1](lessons/07-01-the-inquisition-and-the-galileo-affair.md), [7.3](lessons/07-03-the-abuse-crisis-and-the-argument-from-scandal.md). - **Rung 2.** The filioque addition "lawfully and reasonably added", for declaring the truth and "because of a necessity pressing at that time" (Florence, DS 1302; lesson's translation) [5.1](lessons/05-01-the-filioque-two-questions-kept-apart.md); *Apostolicae Curae*, as the 1998 commentary classifies it, the commentary's force being the disputed point [4.3](lessons/04-03-apostolic-succession-and-the-validity-of-orders.md); Vatican I's legitimacy as an ecumenical council [5.3](lessons/05-03-vatican-i-read-by-orthodox-theologians.md). **At least rung 2**: no human power dissolves a ratified, consummated marriage (Rota, 2000, §8) [5.5](lessons/05-05-marriage-divorce-and-the-state-of-the-dead.md).

### Levels of authority used in this course

Every Catholic text the lessons cite, with the level the lessons mark, in date order. "Cited" means a lesson uses the text without assigning a rung; do not invent one. Joint and dialogue texts (the *Joint Declaration*, ARCIC, Ravenna, Chieti, Alexandria, *The Bishop of Rome*) and every other tradition's texts are in [Weight inside other traditions](#weight-inside-other-traditions); Fathers and theologians writing as authors have **no magisterial level** and are in Thinkers and texts. No Denzinger–Hünermann numbers are printed; where a lesson gives a DS number, it is from a DS-numbered edition and equals DH for these pre-1963 texts ([citations](#citations-and-how-to-read-them)).

| [Nicaea, canon 6](#nicaea-canon-6) (Percival, with the Latin versions he records) | 325 | conciliar canon | **law, no doctrinal rung** | [4.2](lessons/04-02-what-the-patristic-record-proves.md) |

### Weight inside other traditions

Every Lutheran, Reformed, Zwinglian, Anglican, Baptist, Orthodox and joint text the lessons cite, with its standing **inside its own tradition** as the lessons state it, grouped by tradition and dated. None of them has a Catholic level. Theologians of every tradition, writing as authors, are in Thinkers and texts.

| Baptist Confession of 1677/1689 (Second London; Philadelphia) | Baptist (Particular) | 1677/1689 | confession of associated Particular Baptist churches; no binding magisterium | [2.4](lessons/02-04-perspicuity-tested.md) |
| Photius, [*Mystagogy of the Holy Spirit*](#mystagogy-of-the-holy-spirit) | Orthodox | c. 886 | a saint's treatise, **not a conciliar act** | [5.1](lessons/05-01-the-filioque-two-questions-kept-apart.md) |
| [Council of Constantinople, 879–880](#council-of-879-880) | Orthodox | 879–880 | counted ecumenical by some modern Orthodox theologians, **not all** | [5.1](lessons/05-01-the-filioque-two-questions-kept-apart.md) |
| The Constantinopolitan councils of 1341–1351 and the Tome of 1351; the synod of 1368; the Synodikon of Orthodoxy | Orthodox | 1341–1368 | received local councils, binding by reception; the Synodikon read on the Sunday of Orthodoxy | [5.4](lessons/05-04-essence-and-energies.md), [6.1](lessons/06-01-mary.md) |
| [Mark of Ephesus](#mark-of-ephesus), the Ferrara texts on purgatory | Orthodox | 1438 | a saint's theological writings, **not Orthodox dogma** | [5.5](lessons/05-05-marriage-divorce-and-the-state-of-the-dead.md), [6.2](lessons/06-02-the-saints-the-images-the-relics.md) |
| Mogila, *Orthodox Confession*; Dositheus's confession ([seventeenth-century confessions](#orthodox-confessions-of-the-seventeenth-century)) | Orthodox | 1643; 1672 | of **limited authority** in modern Orthodoxy and partly Latinized; never "the Orthodox position" | [1.1](lessons/01-01-shared-ground-and-where-the-argument-is.md), [5.5](lessons/05-05-marriage-divorce-and-the-state-of-the-dead.md), [6.2](lessons/06-02-the-saints-the-images-the-relics.md) |
| [Encyclical of the Eastern Patriarchs](#encyclical-of-the-eastern-patriarchs-1848) | Orthodox | May 1848 | a synodal letter of four patriarchates with their synods: weighty, honoured, **not a council** | [1.1](lessons/01-01-shared-ground-and-where-the-argument-is.md), [1.3](lessons/01-03-arguing-from-history-without-abusing-it.md), [5.3](lessons/05-03-vatican-i-read-by-orthodox-theologians.md) |
| Philaret, *Longer Catechism* | Orthodox (Russian) | 1839 | a nineteenth-century Russian catechism: a catechism's weight, **not an Orthodox definition** (the Orthodox act on images is Nicaea II) | [6.2](lessons/06-02-the-saints-the-images-the-relics.md) |
| Anthimos VII, [Patriarchal and Synodical Encyclical](#patriarchal-encyclical-of-1895) | Orthodox (Constantinople) | Aug 1895 | one patriarchate's official synodal letter, polemical and widely cited, **not a definition binding all Orthodoxy** | [5.1](lessons/05-01-the-filioque-two-questions-kept-apart.md), [5.2](lessons/05-02-papal-primacy-as-the-east-reads-it.md), [5.3](lessons/05-03-vatican-i-read-by-orthodox-theologians.md), [5.5](lessons/05-05-marriage-divorce-and-the-state-of-the-dead.md), [6.1](lessons/06-01-mary.md), [6.6](lessons/06-06-development-or-corruption.md), [7.1](lessons/07-01-the-inquisition-and-the-galileo-affair.md), [7.3](lessons/07-03-the-abuse-crisis-and-the-argument-from-scandal.md) |
| Moscow, [*Basic Principles of Attitude to the Non-Orthodox*](#basic-principles-2000); [*Basis of the Social Concept*](#basis-of-the-social-concept) | Orthodox (Russian) | Aug 2000 | synodal texts of one autocephalous church; bind the Russian Orthodox Church only | [1.2](lessons/01-02-what-the-church-says-about-other-christians.md), [5.5](lessons/05-05-marriage-divorce-and-the-state-of-the-dead.md) |
| Moscow Holy Synod, *Position* on primacy | Orthodox (Russian) | 2013 | one church's synodal act: universal primacy in the first millennium "of honour", of human institution | [5.2](lessons/05-02-papal-primacy-as-the-east-reads-it.md) |
| [Holy and Great Council](#holy-and-great-council-2016), Crete | Orthodox | Jun 2016 | a council of ten autocephalous churches; Antioch, Moscow, Bulgaria and Georgia did not attend; not received as pan-Orthodox by Moscow | [1.2](lessons/01-02-what-the-church-says-about-other-christians.md), [5.5](lessons/05-05-marriage-divorce-and-the-state-of-the-dead.md) |
| [*Joint Declaration on the Doctrine of Justification*](#joint-declaration); the [Official Common Statement and Annex](#official-common-statement-and-annex) | Lutheran (LWF) and Catholic | text 1997; OCS and Annex signed at Augsburg, 31 Oct 1999 | for Catholics an official act of the Church as a party with **no rung of its own**, defining and revoking nothing, §41's correctness **rung 5**; later associations by Methodist (2006), Anglican (2016, a resolution) and Reformed (2017) bodies are acts of those communions | [1.1](lessons/01-01-shared-ground-and-where-the-argument-is.md), [1.3](lessons/01-03-arguing-from-history-without-abusing-it.md), [3.1](lessons/03-01-justification-as-the-confessions-state-it.md), [3.2](lessons/03-02-trent-session-vi-against-the-confessions.md), [3.4](lessons/03-04-the-joint-declaration-merit-and-reward.md) |
| [*The Condemnations of the Reformation Era*](#condemnations-of-the-reformation-era) (*Lehrverurteilungen*, eds Lehmann and Pannenberg) | ecumenical (German) | 1986 | an ecumenical study, **no magisterial level** | [3.2](lessons/03-02-trent-session-vi-against-the-confessions.md) |
| [ARCIC](#arcic) I: *Ministry and Ordination* (1973), *Elucidation* (1979), *Authority in the Church II* (1981), *Final Report* (1982) | Anglican–Catholic | 1973–1982 | dialogue reports, **no level** in either church | [4.3](lessons/04-03-apostolic-succession-and-the-validity-of-orders.md), [6.6](lessons/06-06-development-or-corruption.md) |
| *The Challenge of Proselytism and the Calling to Common Witness* | Catholic–World Council of Churches | 1995 | a joint study, **no magisterial level** | [1.2](lessons/01-02-what-the-church-says-about-other-christians.md) |
| North American Orthodox–Catholic Theological Consultation, *The Filioque: A Church-Dividing Issue?* | Orthodox–Catholic | 25 Oct 2003 | **no level** | [5.1](lessons/05-01-the-filioque-two-questions-kept-apart.md) |
| [Ravenna](#ravenna-document) | Orthodox–Catholic Joint International Commission | 13 Oct 2007 | by its own preface no official declaration of teaching; **no magisterial level**, binds no church | [5.2](lessons/05-02-papal-primacy-as-the-east-reads-it.md), [5.4](lessons/05-04-essence-and-energies.md) |
| *From Conflict to Communion* | Lutheran–Catholic Commission on Unity | 2013 | a commission report: binds no church, **amends no Lutheran confession** | [1.2](lessons/01-02-what-the-church-says-about-other-christians.md) |
| [Chieti; Alexandria](#chieti-and-alexandria) | Orthodox–Catholic Joint International Commission | 21 Sep 2016; 7 Jun 2023 | dialogue texts, **no magisterial level**, binding no church | [5.2](lessons/05-02-papal-primacy-as-the-east-reads-it.md), [5.3](lessons/05-03-vatican-i-read-by-orthodox-theologians.md), [5.4](lessons/05-04-essence-and-energies.md) |
| Leuenberg Agreement | Lutheran, Reformed and United (Europe) | 16 Mar 1973 | an act of those churches | [4.4](lessons/04-04-the-visible-church-and-what-replaced-the-papacy.md) |
| [*The Bishop of Rome*](#bishop-of-rome-2024) | Catholic dicastery study document on the dialogues | 2024 | **below the seam** [5.2](lessons/05-02-papal-primacy-as-the-east-reads-it.md), **no magisterial level** [5.3](lessons/05-03-vatican-i-read-by-orthodox-theologians.md): by its own statement no summary of the magisterium; binds no church | [5.2](lessons/05-02-papal-primacy-as-the-east-reads-it.md), [5.3](lessons/05-03-vatican-i-read-by-orthodox-theologians.md) |
| Justinian, Novel 117 | Roman (Byzantine) civil law | 18 Dec 542 | Roman law on grounds of divorce | [5.5](lessons/05-05-marriage-divorce-and-the-state-of-the-dead.md) |

**The rule.** Other traditions' texts have **no Catholic level**: the ladder grades Catholic acts, and putting a Lutheran or Orthodox text on it is a category error. Inside its own tradition each has a weight, and the lessons grade that weight in both directions. Calling the Book of Concord a Lutheran magisterium or its articles "Lutheran dogma defined" [2.1](lessons/02-01-sola-scriptura-at-its-best.md), [3.2](lessons/03-02-trent-session-vi-against-the-confessions.md), the Smalcald Articles' Antichrist sentence Lutheran dogma binding as a magisterium's definition [1.2](lessons/01-02-what-the-church-says-about-other-christians.md), or the 1895 encyclical, the Moscow *Position* or Mogila "the Orthodox position" [5.2](lessons/05-02-papal-primacy-as-the-east-reads-it.md), [5.5](lessons/05-05-marriage-divorce-and-the-state-of-the-dead.md) overstates; calling Westminster "a museum piece" [2.1](lessons/02-01-sola-scriptura-at-its-best.md), or treating the 2013 commission report as having amended a Lutheran confession [1.2](lessons/01-02-what-the-church-says-about-other-christians.md), understates. Overstating another tradition's text is the same error as overstating a Catholic one. A theologian of any tradition (Luther, Calvin, Chemnitz, Lossky, Ware) is weighed by arguments, never as his tradition's confession; and "Protestant" names no text and no tradition.

### Citations and how to read them

| Form | Points at, and the traps |
|---|---|
| Trent, Session (Roman numeral), ch. *n*, can. *n* | a decree of the Council of Trent in Waterworth's English (1848), read in the Hanover College transcription and checked against Schaff, *Creeds of Christendom* vol. 2, which prints Waterworth with the Latin. A chapter expounds; a canon defines ([chapters and canons](#chapters-and-canons)). Session V has no chapters; Session VI has a proem, sixteen chapters and thirty-three canons; Session XXV's decrees have no canons |
| VII, *On Baptism*, can. *n*; VII can. 12; "reform ch. *n*" | Session VII prints separate canon series (on the sacraments in general; on baptism); "reform" or "ref." is a session's decree on reformation, which is **law, no rung** |
| Hanover's Trent text | sound except: XXI can. 1 is garbled (use Schaff's English); V can. 5's closing anathema is missing (Schaff has it); VI can. 28 is cited from Schaff because the OCR garbles it. Waterworth himself leaves XXI can. 2's *aut in eo errasse* untranslated |
| Waterworth's word choices | XIII ch. 4 "suitably and properly called" but can. 2 "most aptly calls" Transubstantiation; VII can. 8 "through the act performed", not "by the work worked"; XXII's *poenis* is "punishments" in ch. 2 and "pains" in can. 3; XXIV cans. 3–4 render *dirimere* (impediments that make a marriage void) as "dissolve", while cans. 5 and 7's *dissolvi* is the bond itself [5.5](lessons/05-05-marriage-divorce-and-the-state-of-the-dead.md), [6.4](lessons/06-04-the-real-presence.md), [6.5](lessons/06-05-the-sacrifice-of-the-mass.md) |
| Calvin's *Antidote* on Trent VI | Calvin's printed decree counts the proem as head I, so his heads run **one higher** than Trent's chapters (Trent ch. 7 = his "VIII"); his canon numbers match |
| *Dei Filius* ch. *n*; can. II.4; "canons on revelation, 4" | Vatican I's constitution (1870), four chapters each with its own series of canons: can. II.4 is the fourth canon on revelation, IV.3 the third on faith and reason. Quoted in Schaff vol. 2 |
| *Pastor Aeternus* ch. *n*, canon | Vatican I's constitution on the Church (1870), four chapters, a canon closing chs. 1–3 and the definition in ch. 4; Schaff vol. 2 ("of themselves, and not from the consent of the Church") |
| Triglot | the *Concordia Triglotta* (1921), German, Latin and English, English by F. Bente and W. H. T. Dau: the course's text of the Book of Concord. Paragraph numbers are read from the Latin column's margin. Its front matter is Bente's *Historical Introductions*, an editor's work, not confession. Never splice Schaff's English of a Lutheran text with the Triglot's |
| Augsburg (CA) art. *n* | Part I, arts. I–XXI, doctrine; Part II, arts. XXII–XXVIII, abuses corrected. **Schaff vol. 3 renumbers the abuse articles I–VII**, so CA XXIV is Schaff's "Art. III" and CA XXV is not "XXV" in Schaff: cite XXII–XXVIII from the Triglot. The *Variata* (1540) is a different text from the Unaltered (1530) [6.3](lessons/06-03-penance-purgatory-and-indulgences.md), [6.4](lessons/06-04-the-real-presence.md), [6.5](lessons/06-05-the-sacrifice-of-the-mass.md), [7.2](lessons/07-02-the-reformations-grievances.md) |
| Apology, Art. *n*, §*n* | Melanchthon's *Apology* by the **Triglot's** article and paragraph numbers. The Triglot's headings carry both numberings ("Article IV (II)", "Article XXIV (XII)"), and its **Art. III** (on love and the fulfilling of the law) is folded into Art. IV in other editions: the merit passage (§73, §§195–203), the reply on James 2:24 (§§123–132) and the forensic sentences (§§183–187) are all Art. III in the Triglot. Where a part restarts its paragraph numbers (the Apology's part on confession and satisfaction), [6.3](lessons/06-03-penance-purgatory-and-indulgences.md) cites article and part [3.1](lessons/03-01-justification-as-the-confessions-state-it.md), [3.3](lessons/03-03-paul-and-james.md), [3.4](lessons/03-04-the-joint-declaration-merit-and-reward.md), [6.3](lessons/06-03-penance-purgatory-and-indulgences.md) |
| Smalcald Articles, Part.Art. | Luther's articles by part and article: II.1 the chief article; II.2 the Mass; II.4 the papacy; III.3 repentance; III.6 the Sacrament; III.8 confession |
| *Treatise*, §*n* | Melanchthon's *Treatise on the Power and Primacy of the Pope* by the Triglot's Latin margin numbers: the historical section is §§12–21 in the Latin numbering, which the Triglot's English heads VI–XI, so give both; §§63–67 on bishops and ordination [4.2](lessons/04-02-what-the-patristic-record-proves.md), [4.3](lessons/04-03-apostolic-succession-and-the-validity-of-orders.md) |
| Formula, Ep. or SD, art., aff./neg. | the Formula of Concord's Epitome or Solid Declaration (the Triglot heads the latter "Thorough Declaration"), by article and affirmative or negative thesis. "Rule and Norm" (Ep., "Of the Summary Content, Rule, and Standard"): the Triglot says "the sole rule and standard", Schaff "the only rule and norm" (Latin *unicam regulam et normam*); neither *sola scriptura* nor *norma normans* is in the text. The Formula never names Osiander or Stancarus ("the one side ... the other"). Schaff vol. 3 prints the Epitome only [2.1](lessons/02-01-sola-scriptura-at-its-best.md), [3.1](lessons/03-01-justification-as-the-confessions-state-it.md), [6.4](lessons/06-04-the-real-presence.md) |
| Thirty-Nine Articles, Art. *n* | cite from the Articles' **own section** in Schaff vol. 3, never from the **Methodist Articles** printed later in the volume, which renumber them (Article XXII appears there as XIV). Schaff prints three columns: the Latin of 1563, the English of 1571 (old spelling) and the **American revision of 1801** (modern spelling); a modern-spelling quotation is the 1801 column, safe only where its words match 1571. The 1563 Latin lacks Art. XXIX, so Schaff's Latin column labels **XXX–XXXIX one lower** (XXXI headed "XXX.", XXXVI labelled XXXV, XXXVII "XXXVI. [XXXVII.]"). The American revision omits XXI, rewrites XXXVII and drops the Athanasian Creed from VIII. Article XXXI: the 1571 English has "the Priestes" (plural), the Latin and the 1801 text "the Priest"; quote it in full or not at all [6.5](lessons/06-05-the-sacrifice-of-the-mass.md). A modern-spelling "may err, and sometimes have erred" elsewhere in Schaff vol. 3 is the Irish Articles of 1615, not Article XXI [5.3](lessons/05-03-vatican-i-read-by-orthodox-theologians.md) |
| Westminster ch.section | the Westminster Confession by chapter and section in the **1647 numbering** Schaff prints, with the American numbering in brackets: **XXXI.3 is American XXXI.2, XXXI.4 is American XXXI.3** (printed "IV. [III.]"); the American revision rewrote chiefly chs. XXIII and XXXI. XXV.6 is the 1647 text (the PCUSA rewrote it in 1903; the OPC's edition omits the Antichrist clause). Proof texts are Schaff's footnotes. Shorter Catechism by question [2.1](lessons/02-01-sola-scriptura-at-its-best.md), [4.4](lessons/04-04-the-visible-church-and-what-replaced-the-papacy.md), [5.3](lessons/05-03-vatican-i-read-by-orthodox-theologians.md), [7.1](lessons/07-01-the-inquisition-and-the-galileo-affair.md) |
| Heidelberg Q.*n*; Belgic art. *n*; Second Helvetic ch.section; Dort | Schaff vol. 3. Heidelberg Q.80 is **not in the first edition** (Schaff's note), its bracketed clauses added later. The Second Helvetic is printed in Latin, with the English in a third-edition appendix that has no section numbers; the lessons cite sections by the Latin numbering (XIV.8, XVIII.13–17). Dort's Sentence on the Remonstrants is in Latin (lesson's translation) [4.1](lessons/04-01-the-petrine-texts-argued.md), [4.4](lessons/04-04-the-visible-church-and-what-replaced-the-papacy.md), [6.4](lessons/06-04-the-real-presence.md) |
| Calvin, *Institutes* book.chapter.section | Beveridge's translation (1845), public domain, by book, chapter and section (III.11.1). Beveridge says "a twofold benefit", not "double grace", and "carrying its own evidence along with it", not "self-authenticating": those are later translators' phrases, and Battles's (1960) English is copyrighted. In the CCEL file IV.18's heading prints "CHAPTER [653]", and IV.6.3 misprints John 20:23 as "John 10:23". The *Short Treatise* is headed 1540 (usually dated 1541); the *Consensus Tigurinus* is printed in its 1554 edition [2.1](lessons/02-01-sola-scriptura-at-its-best.md), [3.1](lessons/03-01-justification-as-the-confessions-state-it.md), [4.1](lessons/04-01-the-petrine-texts-argued.md), [6.4](lessons/06-04-the-real-presence.md), [6.5](lessons/06-05-the-sacrifice-of-the-mass.md) |
| Luther, *Bondage of the Will*, Sect. *n*; the 1522 prefaces | Henry Cole's translation (1823), by section; the common archive.org 1823 text is **E. T. Vaughan's**, a different translation. The September Testament prefaces (1522) have no public-domain English located: lesson's translation of the German [2.2](lessons/02-02-the-canon-question.md), [2.4](lessons/02-04-perspicuity-tested.md), [3.3](lessons/03-03-paul-and-james.md) |
| Percival | *The Seven Ecumenical Councils*, ed. Percival (NPNF second series, vol. 14): canons, acts and excursuses. Nicaea's canon 6 with the Latin versions he records ([4.2](lessons/04-02-what-the-patristic-record-proves.md)); Ephesus's decree on the creed is "the decree carried as canon 7" ([5.1](lessons/05-01-the-filioque-two-questions-kept-apart.md)); for Nicaea II, quote only what Percival prints ("the honour paid to the image passes to its prototype" is Basil's phrase, which the council cites) [6.2](lessons/06-02-the-saints-the-images-the-relics.md) |
| ANF; NPNF1; NPNF2 | Schaff and Wace's *Ante-Nicene* and *Nicene and Post-Nicene Fathers*, public domain; their numbering can differ from modern editions (Cyprian's Ep. 29 in ANF is Oxford Ep. 36; Ep. 63.17 in Hartel is ANF 62.17); Gregory the Great by Register book and letter [1.3](lessons/01-03-arguing-from-history-without-abusing-it.md), [6.2](lessons/06-02-the-saints-the-images-the-relics.md), [6.5](lessons/06-05-the-sacrifice-of-the-mass.md) |
| DR; [DR *n*:*n*] | the Douay–Rheims (Challoner), quoted for the verse only, never Challoner's polemical notes. Numbering: 2 Thess 2:15 = DR 2:14 (the pair 2:14–15 = DR 2:13–14); John 6:54–58 = DR 6:55–59, 6:63 = DR 6:64; Ps 51:4 = DR 50:6; Ps 19:12 = DR 18:13; 2 Sam 12:14 = DR 2 Kings 12:14; 2 Macc 15:38 = DR 15:39; Malachi, Isaiah, Revelation = DR Malachias, Isaias, Apocalypse. Renderings that follow the Vulgate: 1 Cor 11:2's *paradoseis* as "ordinances"; 2 Tim 3:16 attributively; Matt 11:19 "children" where the Greek has "works". Greek: Tischendorf, 8th edition [2.3](lessons/02-03-tradition-and-what-sufficiency-means.md), [2.4](lessons/02-04-perspicuity-tested.md), [3.3](lessons/03-03-paul-and-james.md), [6.3](lessons/06-03-penance-purgatory-and-indulgences.md), [6.4](lessons/06-04-the-real-presence.md) |
| Newman | the *Essay on Development* in the **1878** edition, by chapter and section (the notes are ch. 5 §§1–7); the *Lectures on Justification* in the 1874 third edition, whose bracketed notes are the Catholic Newman's; the *Letter to Pusey* dated 7 December 1865, published 1866; the *Letter to the Duke of Norfolk* (1875) by section [1.3](lessons/01-03-arguing-from-history-without-abusing-it.md), [3.4](lessons/03-04-the-joint-declaration-merit-and-reward.md), [5.3](lessons/05-03-vatican-i-read-by-orthodox-theologians.md), [6.6](lessons/06-06-development-or-corruption.md) |
| de Sales, *The Catholic Controversy* II.III.12 | Mackey's translation (1886; third edition 1909), by part, article and chapter. The "when did Rome change?" challenge there is **Edmund Campion's** (*Decem Rationes* 7), quoted by de Sales [1.3](lessons/01-03-arguing-from-history-without-abusing-it.md) |
| *Apostolicae Curae* §*n* | Leo XIII's apostolic letter by the common §§1–41 numbering, quoted from the 1896 Art and Book Company English pamphlet (whose own paragraphs run 1–10). Two 1896 English versions circulated (*Saepius Officio* quotes "nude commemoration" where the pamphlet has "bare"): attribute wording to a version. *Saepius Officio* by its Roman-numbered sections [4.3](lessons/04-03-apostolic-succession-and-the-validity-of-orders.md), [6.5](lessons/06-05-the-sacrifice-of-the-mass.md) |
| 1848 Encyclical §*n*; 1895 Encyclical §*n* | the Encyclical of the Eastern Patriarchs has no English with a recorded translator: paraphrased. Anthimos VII's encyclical in Metallinos's English (Manchester, 1896), by Roman-numbered section [1.1](lessons/01-01-shared-ground-and-where-the-argument-is.md), [5.2](lessons/05-02-papal-primacy-as-the-east-reads-it.md) |
| Kidd No. 126; Gebler | the *Consilium de emendanda ecclesia* in Kidd's *Documents Illustrative of the Continental Reformation* (1911), lesson's translation of the Latin [7.2](lessons/07-02-the-reformations-grievances.md); the Galileo documents in Gebler (tr. Sturge, 1879) [7.1](lessons/07-01-the-inquisition-and-the-galileo-affair.md) |
| DS *n* | a number from a DS-numbered Denzinger, printed only where a lesson verified it there; for these pre-1963 texts DS equals DH. Never the 1911 Denzinger's "old D" numbers. A Denzinger number is an index, not a weight, and its headings are its editors' |
| CCC *n* | the *Catechism of the Catholic Church*: locates, keeps its source's weight, fixes no level |
| can. *n* | the 1983 Code of Canon Law: law, no rung. Can. 749 §3 the default-down rule; can. 750 §2 definitive teaching; can. 751 heresy |
| LG, UR, OE, DV, DH, SC *n* | Vatican II's documents by article (vatican.va), paraphrased or quoted in short phrases (copyrighted English) |
| JD §*n*; OCS; Annex 2C, 2E | the *Joint Declaration* by paragraph; the Official Common Statement and its Annex (1999); paraphrased |
| ST I-II q.*n* a.*n* | Aquinas, *Summa Theologiae*, English Dominican Province translation |
| (lesson's translation) | the lesson's own English of a text with no public-domain English: Florence, *Benedictus Deus*, the 1875 declaration's Latin version, *Exsurge*, Dort's Sentence, Mark of Ephesus (Petit), Zwingli's and Luther's German, Lehmann's address, Kidd's *Consilium* |
| (paraphrased) | a modern text under copyright (vatican.va English, the Catechism, the dialogue texts, modern theologians), given in paraphrase or short phrases |
| `course-id` *M.N* | a sibling lesson, linked by its course id (the ladder is `fundamental-theology` 4.4; the doctrines are `ecclesiology-and-mariology`, `sacraments-and-liturgy`, `creation-grace-last-things`, `trinity-and-god`, `theology-of-debt`; the record is `church-history`) |

*From* [1.1](lessons/01-01-shared-ground-and-where-the-argument-is.md), [2.1](lessons/02-01-sola-scriptura-at-its-best.md), [3.1](lessons/03-01-justification-as-the-confessions-state-it.md), [3.2](lessons/03-02-trent-session-vi-against-the-confessions.md), [4.2](lessons/04-02-what-the-patristic-record-proves.md), [6.5](lessons/06-05-the-sacrifice-of-the-mass.md); the citation conventions are [`fundamental-theology` 4.5](../fundamental-theology/lessons/04-05-reading-a-documents-weight.md)'s

### Chapters and canons

A council defines in its canons; its chapters expound. A chapter clause no canon repeats is **at least rung 3**, and rung 1 only where another act carries the same content.

**At Trent.** A canon condemns a proposition in the sense it states, so its contradictory is **rung 1 as worded**; the anathemas attach to the canons. Session VI's chapters are fenced unusually strongly: the proem forbids teaching otherwise than the decree defines and declares, and ch. 16 closes by calling the chapters doctrine "which whoso receiveth not faithfully and firmly cannot be justified". The fence binds Catholics to the chapters' **doctrine** without making every sentence, proof texts and reasons included, a definition; can. 749 §3 defaults down. So ch. 7's "alone", ch. 8's wording, ch. 9's denial of a certainty of faith, ch. 12 and ch. 16's grounds of merit are **at least rung 3**, while cans. 13–14 and 16 are **rung 1**, and ch. 15 only restates what cans. 23 and 27 define [3.2](lessons/03-02-trent-session-vi-against-the-confessions.md), [3.1](lessons/03-01-justification-as-the-confessions-state-it.md), [3.3](lessons/03-03-paul-and-james.md), [3.4](lessons/03-04-the-joint-declaration-merit-and-reward.md).

- **The same rule elsewhere.** *Dei Filius* ch. 2's "not because ... approved by her authority" is chapter text, **at least rung 3**, its substance following from can. II.4's rung-1 inspiration [2.1](lessons/02-01-sola-scriptura-at-its-best.md), [2.2](lessons/02-02-the-canon-question.md); ch. 3's Church as motive of credibility **at least rung 3**, its can. III.3 **rung 1** [2.3](lessons/02-03-tradition-and-what-sufficiency-means.md), [7.3](lessons/07-03-the-abuse-crisis-and-the-argument-from-scandal.md); *Pastor Aeternus* ch. 1's reading of Matthew 16 and John 21 **at least rung 3** [4.1](lessons/04-01-the-petrine-texts-argued.md); Trent XIII ch. 1 [6.4](lessons/06-04-the-real-presence.md), XIV ch. 8 [6.3](lessons/06-03-penance-purgatory-and-indulgences.md) and XXII ch. 3 [6.1](lessons/06-01-mary.md), [6.2](lessons/06-02-the-saints-the-images-the-relics.md) **at least rung 3**; XXII chs. 1–2 **at least rung 3 and not open to denial**, since can. 4 rests on them [6.5](lessons/06-05-the-sacrifice-of-the-mass.md).
- **Decrees without canons.** Trent XXV teaches invocation and relics in a decree with no canon (those who deny invocation "think impiously"): **at least rung 3**. But its images section closes with an anathema on those who teach contrary to its decrees (DS 1824), restating Nicaea II's definition, so images stay **rung 1** [6.2](lessons/06-02-the-saints-the-images-the-relics.md).

**Traps.** "The fence makes ch. 9 defined, so Westminster XVIII.2 falls under Trent's anathema" is wrong twice: ch. 9 is at least rung 3, and cans. 13–14 condemn making certainty *necessary*, which Westminster XVIII.3 also denies [3.2](lessons/03-02-trent-session-vi-against-the-confessions.md). The opposite error, "only the canons count, so ch. 9 binds no one", is the one [`creation-grace-last-things` 5.1](../creation-grace-last-things/lessons/05-01-trent-session-vi-justification.md) corrects. Saying Trent *defined* what a chapter only expounds overstates.

*Lessons:* [3.2](lessons/03-02-trent-session-vi-against-the-confessions.md); applied in [2.1](lessons/02-01-sola-scriptura-at-its-best.md), [2.2](lessons/02-02-the-canon-question.md), [2.3](lessons/02-03-tradition-and-what-sufficiency-means.md), [3.1](lessons/03-01-justification-as-the-confessions-state-it.md), [3.3](lessons/03-03-paul-and-james.md), [3.4](lessons/03-04-the-joint-declaration-merit-and-reward.md), [4.1](lessons/04-01-the-petrine-texts-argued.md), [6.2](lessons/06-02-the-saints-the-images-the-relics.md), [6.4](lessons/06-04-the-real-presence.md), [6.5](lessons/06-05-the-sacrifice-of-the-mass.md), [7.3](lessons/07-03-the-abuse-crisis-and-the-argument-from-scandal.md)

### Application of a condemnation

Whether a given text asserts what a canon condemns, in the canon's sense, is a second judgment, separate from the canon's truth.

The canon's content is defined, **rung 1 as worded**. Its application to a named text is a reading no magisterial act has made, so it is **rung 5**, however plain, unless an act applies it. No act applies Trent's canons to Westminster or the Formula: Westminster (completed 1646) postdates Trent and is named by no act [3.2](lessons/03-02-trent-session-vi-against-the-confessions.md). The *Joint Declaration* §41 judges only the Lutheran teaching "as presented" in it, an official judgment of the Church as a party with **no rung of its own**; whether it is correct is **rung 5** [3.1](lessons/03-01-justification-as-the-confessions-state-it.md), [3.2](lessons/03-02-trent-session-vi-against-the-confessions.md), [3.4](lessons/03-04-the-joint-declaration-merit-and-reward.md); the method is [`creation-grace-last-things` 5.2](../creation-grace-last-things/lessons/05-02-the-1999-joint-declaration.md)'s.

- **Plain is not applied.** Westminster XVII.1 against VI can. 23 is plain; Westminster XVIII against can. 16 is genuinely open (XVIII.4's assurance "shaken, diminished, and intermitted"); both are **rung 5** [3.2](lessons/03-02-trent-session-vi-against-the-confessions.md).
- **The lessons' application questions**, all **rung 5**: Trent IV's anathema and Westminster's or Mathison's formulation [2.1](lessons/02-01-sola-scriptura-at-its-best.md); Westminster I.6, Article VI, Chemnitz [2.3](lessons/02-03-tradition-and-what-sufficiency-means.md); Westminster I.7 [2.4](lessons/02-04-perspicuity-tested.md); a confession under a VI canon [3.1](lessons/03-01-justification-as-the-confessions-state-it.md); Hus's article 1 and Westminster XXV.1, which unlike Hus (art. 3) counts reprobate professors in the visible Church [4.4](lessons/04-04-the-visible-church-and-what-replaced-the-papacy.md); XIII can. 8's "spiritually only" and Calvin's "true and substantial communication" [6.4](lessons/06-04-the-real-presence.md); simplicity's acts and Palamas, where can. 749 §3 forbids inferring a condemnation [5.4](lessons/05-04-essence-and-energies.md).
- **A sense, not a word.** VI can. 9 condemns *sola fide* only "in such wise as to mean" nothing else co-operates; Trent condemns *sole* imputation (can. 11), not every use of the word [3.1](lessons/03-01-justification-as-the-confessions-state-it.md).

**Traps.** "Trent condemned Westminster" [3.2](lessons/03-02-trent-session-vi-against-the-confessions.md); "the Declaration revoked the canons" or "the Annex repealed can. 32": it has no rung and revokes nothing [1.1](lessons/01-01-shared-ground-and-where-the-argument-is.md), [3.4](lessons/03-04-the-joint-declaration-merit-and-reward.md).

*Lessons:* [3.2](lessons/03-02-trent-session-vi-against-the-confessions.md); applied in [2.1](lessons/02-01-sola-scriptura-at-its-best.md), [2.3](lessons/02-03-tradition-and-what-sufficiency-means.md), [2.4](lessons/02-04-perspicuity-tested.md), [3.1](lessons/03-01-justification-as-the-confessions-state-it.md), [3.4](lessons/03-04-the-joint-declaration-merit-and-reward.md), [4.4](lessons/04-04-the-visible-church-and-what-replaced-the-papacy.md), [5.4](lessons/05-04-essence-and-energies.md), [6.4](lessons/06-04-the-real-presence.md)

### In globo censure

A condemnation of a list of propositions under a list of notes applied "respectively" (*respective*), without saying which note fits which proposition.

Such an act binds Catholics to reject each proposition but fixes **no precise note** on any one, so the contradictory of each is **at least rung 3, no precise note**. Calling its denial heresy overstates; calling the question open understates.

- **Leo X, *Exsurge Domine*** (15 June 1520): forty-one of Luther's propositions condemned "respectively" heretical, scandalous, false, offensive or misleading (DS 1492). Prop. 33, "that heretics be burned is against the will of the Spirit" (DS 1483; lesson's translation): its contradictory **at least rung 3 as a 1520 act, no precise note**; not a definition and not heresy to deny; whether *Dignitatis Humanae* developed or reversed it is **rung 5** [7.1](lessons/07-01-the-inquisition-and-the-galileo-affair.md). Prop. 36 (free will after sin "a thing with only a name") is repeated nearly word for word by VI can. 5 [3.2](lessons/03-02-trent-session-vi-against-the-confessions.md).
- **Constance, session XV** (6 July 1415): Hus's article 1, that the universal Church is "the totality of the predestined" (DS 1201), condemned under a global censure, as Martin V's *Inter cunctas* (1418) shows (DS 1251): contradictory **at least rung 3, no precise note**, carrying with LG 8 and *Mystici Corporis* the visible-Church claim; its application to Westminster XXV.1 **rung 5** [4.4](lessons/04-04-the-visible-church-and-what-replaced-the-papacy.md).

**The critic's reply.** That a congregation's censure or an *in globo* condemnation is not the Church teaching in the promise's sense is the Catholic distinction; the [retreat objection](#retreat-objection) answers that the conditions were not fixed independently of the failures. That is part of 7.1's crux [7.1](lessons/07-01-the-inquisition-and-the-galileo-affair.md).

*Lessons:* [7.1](lessons/07-01-the-inquisition-and-the-galileo-affair.md); also [3.2](lessons/03-02-trent-session-vi-against-the-confessions.md), [4.4](lessons/04-04-the-visible-church-and-what-replaced-the-papacy.md)

### Dogmatic fact

A fact not itself revealed but so tied to revealed doctrine that the Church can judge it definitively: **rung 2**.

The CDF's 1998 *Doctrinal Commentary* on the *Professio fidei* (§11) lists examples of truths to be held definitively "without any intention of completeness or exhaustiveness": among those connected with revelation by historical necessity, the legitimacy of a papal election or of the celebration of an ecumenical council, canonizations (which it calls dogmatic facts), and Leo XIII's declaration in *Apostolicae Curae*. Denying a rung-2 truth is not heresy (can. 751) but sets one against definitive teaching (can. 750 §2). The commentary's own classifications weigh **rung 3, light grade, at most** [4.2](lessons/04-02-what-the-patristic-record-proves.md), [4.3](lessons/04-03-apostolic-succession-and-the-validity-of-orders.md), [5.3](lessons/05-03-vatican-i-read-by-orthodox-theologians.md).

- **Vatican I's legitimacy** as an ecumenical council: **rung 2**, under the commentary's class; no act names Vatican I [5.3](lessons/05-03-vatican-i-read-by-orthodox-theologians.md).
- ***Apostolicae Curae***: **rung 2, as the commentary classifies it**, the commentary's force being disputed. Its label of dogmatic facts goes with the canonizations and does not name the letter, so never write "*Apostolicae Curae* is a dogmatic fact" as the commentary's words; older writers (the 1907 *Catholic Encyclopedia*) held Leo's act an infallible judgment on a dogmatic fact [4.3](lessons/04-03-apostolic-succession-and-the-validity-of-orders.md).

**Traps.** Calling a rung-2 truth rung 1 (it is not revealed); calling *Apostolicae Curae* "open" (the floor is a papal judgment given "by Our authority, of Our own motion and certain knowledge"). The general theory of dogmatic facts is [`fundamental-theology` 4.2](../fundamental-theology/lessons/04-02-infallibility-and-its-conditions.md)'s; the textbook case, Jansen's *Augustinus*, is [`creation-grace-last-things` 5.6](../creation-grace-last-things/lessons/05-06-jansenism.md).

*Lessons:* [4.3](lessons/04-03-apostolic-succession-and-the-validity-of-orders.md); also [5.3](lessons/05-03-vatican-i-read-by-orthodox-theologians.md)

### Norma normans and norma normata

The ruling rule and the ruled rule: Scripture norms everything; the confessions are normed by it and bind only as agreeing with it.

The terms belong to later Lutheran dogmatics, **not to the Formula of Concord's text**, which calls Scripture "the sole rule and standard" and "the only judge", and the creeds and other writings witnesses, "a testimony and declaration of the faith" (Triglot); in the Triglot the Latin terms occur only in Bente's *Historical Introductions* (1921) [2.1](lessons/02-01-sola-scriptura-at-its-best.md). Applied: the [Book of Concord](#book-of-concord) is a *norma normata* under Scripture, the *norma normans* [2.1](lessons/02-01-sola-scriptura-at-its-best.md), [2.3](lessons/02-03-tradition-and-what-sufficiency-means.md), [4.4](lessons/04-04-the-visible-church-and-what-replaced-the-papacy.md).

- **Binding, not infallible.** A *norma normata* binds those who subscribe it, and it is neither a magisterium nor infallible on its own terms: 2.1 grades both errors ("a Lutheran magisterium"; Westminster "a museum piece") [2.1](lessons/02-01-sola-scriptura-at-its-best.md). Lutheran churches differ in how much of the Book of Concord binds them [2.1](lessons/02-01-sola-scriptura-at-its-best.md).
- **Where authority reappears.** Bente reports about 8,000 signatures to the Formula, with Electoral Saxony demanding subscription and refusers suspended in Mecklenburg: authority returned as subscription [4.4](lessons/04-04-the-visible-church-and-what-replaced-the-papacy.md).
- The Presbyterian counterpart is a **subordinate standard** (Westminster) [2.1](lessons/02-01-sola-scriptura-at-its-best.md).

*Lessons:* [2.1](lessons/02-01-sola-scriptura-at-its-best.md), [2.3](lessons/02-03-tradition-and-what-sufficiency-means.md), [4.4](lessons/04-04-the-visible-church-and-what-replaced-the-papacy.md)

### Book of Concord

The 1580 collection of Lutheran confessions: the three creeds, the [Augsburg Confession](#augsburg-confession) (1530) and its [Apology](#apology-of-the-augsburg-confession) (1531), the [Smalcald Articles](#smalcald-articles) (1537), Melanchthon's [*Treatise on the Power and Primacy of the Pope*](#treatise-on-the-power-and-primacy-of-the-pope) (1537), Luther's Small and Large Catechisms (1529), and the [Formula of Concord](#formula-of-concord) (1577).

**Standing.** The confessional standard Lutheran pastors subscribe, a [*norma normata*](#norma-normans-and-norma-normata) under Scripture; not a magisterium, not infallible on its own terms; Lutheran churches differ in how much of it binds them. **No Catholic level.** Cited from the *Concordia Triglotta* (1921) ([citations](#citations-and-how-to-read-them)).

- **What it contains and what it does not.** It contains **no canon list**: the Formula names its rule without listing the books, and the Apology (XXI (IX)) cites 2 Maccabees without settling its rank [2.2](lessons/02-02-the-canon-question.md). Luther's 1522 rankings, the Magdeburg *Centuries*, Chemnitz's *Examen* and *Bondage of the Will* are not in it, though the Formula (SD II.44) commends *Bondage* on free will [1.3](lessons/01-03-arguing-from-history-without-abusing-it.md), [2.2](lessons/02-02-the-canon-question.md), [2.4](lessons/02-04-perspicuity-tested.md), [3.2](lessons/03-02-trent-session-vi-against-the-confessions.md), [3.3](lessons/03-03-paul-and-james.md).
- **Its Preface on condemnations.** They are not aimed at "those men who err from a certain simplicity of mind", nor at whole churches: a doctrine can divide confessions without being declared necessary to salvation [2.4](lessons/02-04-perspicuity-tested.md).

**Traps.** A Lutheran magisterium, a Lutheran "infallible authority", "Lutheran dogma defined": all overstate [2.1](lessons/02-01-sola-scriptura-at-its-best.md), [3.1](lessons/03-01-justification-as-the-confessions-state-it.md), [3.2](lessons/03-02-trent-session-vi-against-the-confessions.md); treating a subscribed standard as a museum piece understates [2.1](lessons/02-01-sola-scriptura-at-its-best.md).

*Lessons:* [1.1](lessons/01-01-shared-ground-and-where-the-argument-is.md), [1.2](lessons/01-02-what-the-church-says-about-other-christians.md), [1.3](lessons/01-03-arguing-from-history-without-abusing-it.md), [2.1](lessons/02-01-sola-scriptura-at-its-best.md), [2.2](lessons/02-02-the-canon-question.md), [2.4](lessons/02-04-perspicuity-tested.md), [3.1](lessons/03-01-justification-as-the-confessions-state-it.md), [3.2](lessons/03-02-trent-session-vi-against-the-confessions.md), [3.3](lessons/03-03-paul-and-james.md), [3.4](lessons/03-04-the-joint-declaration-merit-and-reward.md), [4.1](lessons/04-01-the-petrine-texts-argued.md), [4.2](lessons/04-02-what-the-patristic-record-proves.md), [4.3](lessons/04-03-apostolic-succession-and-the-validity-of-orders.md), [4.4](lessons/04-04-the-visible-church-and-what-replaced-the-papacy.md), [6.2](lessons/06-02-the-saints-the-images-the-relics.md), [6.3](lessons/06-03-penance-purgatory-and-indulgences.md), [6.4](lessons/06-04-the-real-presence.md), [6.5](lessons/06-05-the-sacrifice-of-the-mass.md), [6.6](lessons/06-06-development-or-corruption.md), [7.2](lessons/07-02-the-reformations-grievances.md)

### Augsburg Confession

The Lutheran confession presented by the Lutheran estates at the Diet of Augsburg in 1530, written by Melanchthon: the first document of the [Book of Concord](#book-of-concord). Part I (arts. I–XXI) states doctrine; Part II (arts. XXII–XXVIII) the abuses corrected.

**Standing.** Lutheran confessional standard, *norma normata* under Scripture; the Formula's Rule and Standard names it, with the Apology, the Smalcald Articles and Luther's Catechisms, among the witnesses Scripture judges [2.1](lessons/02-01-sola-scriptura-at-its-best.md). Melanchthon's *Variata* (1540) rewrote art. X ("with the bread and wine are truly offered") and dropped its rejection of contrary teachers; Calvin signed it [6.4](lessons/06-04-the-real-presence.md). No Catholic level.

**Articles the lessons use** (Triglot): I, Nicaea's decree "true and to be believed without any doubting" [1.1](lessons/01-01-shared-ground-and-where-the-argument-is.md); IV, justification (its "This faith God imputes for righteousness" is set beside the Formula in 3.1 and Boss 3(a)) [3.1](lessons/03-01-justification-as-the-confessions-state-it.md); V and XIV, the ministry and the call [4.3](lessons/04-03-apostolic-succession-and-the-validity-of-orders.md); VI, new obedience, works necessary "because of God's will" and not to merit [3.1](lessons/03-01-justification-as-the-confessions-state-it.md), [3.2](lessons/03-02-trent-session-vi-against-the-confessions.md); VII, the Church and its marks, the [*satis est*](#satis-est) (Boss 4(c) close-reads it) [1.2](lessons/01-02-what-the-church-says-about-other-christians.md), [4.4](lessons/04-04-the-visible-church-and-what-replaced-the-papacy.md); VIII, hypocrites mingled in the Church, sacraments valid from evil ministers, the Donatists condemned [7.3](lessons/07-03-the-abuse-crisis-and-the-argument-from-scandal.md); IX, baptism necessary, infants baptized [2.4](lessons/02-04-perspicuity-tested.md); X, the Supper [6.4](lessons/06-04-the-real-presence.md); XI, XII and XXV, absolution retained, enumeration not necessary, repentance of "two parts" (contrition and faith) [6.3](lessons/06-03-penance-purgatory-and-indulgences.md); XII also condemns those who deny the justified can lose the Spirit [3.1](lessons/03-01-justification-as-the-confessions-state-it.md), [3.2](lessons/03-02-trent-session-vi-against-the-confessions.md); XV and XVIII, observances and free will [3.2](lessons/03-02-trent-session-vi-against-the-confessions.md); XX, faith not "merely the knowledge of the history", the terrified conscience [3.1](lessons/03-01-justification-as-the-confessions-state-it.md); XXI, the saints remembered, not invoked [6.1](lessons/06-01-mary.md), [6.2](lessons/06-02-the-saints-the-images-the-relics.md); the close of Part I, claiming nothing in its doctrine varies from Scripture or the Church Catholic [6.6](lessons/06-06-development-or-corruption.md); Part II's preface and XXII–XXVIII, the abuses (XXIV: the Mass "retained among us, and celebrated with the highest reverence") [6.5](lessons/06-05-the-sacrifice-of-the-mass.md), [7.2](lessons/07-02-the-reformations-grievances.md).

**Traps.** Schaff vol. 3 numbers the abuse articles I–VII (CA XXIV = Schaff "Art. III"): cite from the Triglot, and do not splice Schaff's English with the Triglot's [6.5](lessons/06-05-the-sacrifice-of-the-mass.md), [7.2](lessons/07-02-the-reformations-grievances.md). "Alien righteousness" is not in art. IV [3.1](lessons/03-01-justification-as-the-confessions-state-it.md). Art. VII's Church is not "invisible": its marks are public [1.2](lessons/01-02-what-the-church-says-about-other-christians.md).

*Lessons:* [1.2](lessons/01-02-what-the-church-says-about-other-christians.md), [2.1](lessons/02-01-sola-scriptura-at-its-best.md), [2.4](lessons/02-04-perspicuity-tested.md), [3.1](lessons/03-01-justification-as-the-confessions-state-it.md), [3.2](lessons/03-02-trent-session-vi-against-the-confessions.md), [4.4](lessons/04-04-the-visible-church-and-what-replaced-the-papacy.md), [6.2](lessons/06-02-the-saints-the-images-the-relics.md), [6.3](lessons/06-03-penance-purgatory-and-indulgences.md), [6.4](lessons/06-04-the-real-presence.md), [6.5](lessons/06-05-the-sacrifice-of-the-mass.md), [6.6](lessons/06-06-development-or-corruption.md), [7.2](lessons/07-02-the-reformations-grievances.md), [7.3](lessons/07-03-the-abuse-crisis-and-the-argument-from-scandal.md); also [1.1](lessons/01-01-shared-ground-and-where-the-argument-is.md), [4.3](lessons/04-03-apostolic-succession-and-the-validity-of-orders.md), [6.1](lessons/06-01-mary.md)

### Apology of the Augsburg Confession

Melanchthon's 1531 defence of the Augsburg Confession against the Catholic *Confutation*; in the [Book of Concord](#book-of-concord), subscribed at Smalcald in 1537.

**Standing.** Lutheran confessional standard, *norma normata*, not a magisterium; no Catholic level. Cited by the **Triglot's** article and paragraph numbers, in which **Art. III** is a separate article that other editions fold into Art. IV ([citations](#citations-and-how-to-read-them)).

**What the lessons use.** IV.20, the terrified conscience; IV, "What is justifying faith?"; IV.48, IV.72, IV.114–117 and IV.119 on faith, its object and certainty [3.1](lessons/03-01-justification-as-the-confessions-state-it.md), [3.2](lessons/03-02-trent-session-vi-against-the-confessions.md). Art. III §§183–187: "forensic usage", the righteousness "of another" (*aliena iustitia*), faith righteousness "imputatively" [3.1](lessons/03-01-justification-as-the-confessions-state-it.md). Art. III §73: good works merit "other rewards, bodily and spiritual", not remission, grace or justification; §§195–203 against condign merit [3.2](lessons/03-02-trent-session-vi-against-the-confessions.md), [3.4](lessons/03-04-the-joint-declaration-merit-and-reward.md), [4.2](lessons/04-02-what-the-patristic-record-proves.md). Art. III §§123–132: James speaks of the works of those already justified, and §131's "pronounced righteous in a forensic sense": the Lutheran confessional reading of James, not Luther's 1522 preface [3.3](lessons/03-03-paul-and-james.md), [4.1](lessons/04-01-the-petrine-texts-argued.md). VII–VIII: the Church "originally a fellowship of faith and of the Holy Ghost in hearts", "Neither, indeed, are we dreaming of a Platonic state" (§20) [4.4](lessons/04-04-the-visible-church-and-what-replaced-the-papacy.md). XI–XIII: absolution retained, enumeration not by divine law, absolution a sacrament, "not a legal process" [6.3](lessons/06-03-penance-purgatory-and-indulgences.md), [6.5](lessons/06-05-the-sacrifice-of-the-mass.md). XIII.9, 11: ministers "called priests, not in order to make any sacrifices for the people", and ordination a sacrament if it is understood of the ministry of the Word [5.1](lessons/05-01-the-filioque-two-questions-kept-apart.md). XIV, ordination [4.3](lessons/04-03-apostolic-succession-and-the-validity-of-orders.md). XXI (IX): the saints honoured three ways; no command, promise or example for invoking them; Mary's honour against her substitution for Christ [2.2](lessons/02-02-the-canon-question.md), [6.1](lessons/06-01-mary.md), [6.2](lessons/06-02-the-saints-the-images-the-relics.md). XXIV (XII): sacrament and sacrifice, the [two species of sacrifice](#two-species-of-sacrifice) [6.5](lessons/06-05-the-sacrifice-of-the-mass.md).

**Traps.** Citing its merit or James passages as "Apology IV" mixes numberings; "alien righteousness" is the Apology's and Luther's term, not Augsburg IV's or the Epitome's [3.1](lessons/03-01-justification-as-the-confessions-state-it.md), [3.3](lessons/03-03-paul-and-james.md), [3.4](lessons/03-04-the-joint-declaration-merit-and-reward.md).

*Lessons:* [3.1](lessons/03-01-justification-as-the-confessions-state-it.md), [3.2](lessons/03-02-trent-session-vi-against-the-confessions.md), [3.3](lessons/03-03-paul-and-james.md), [3.4](lessons/03-04-the-joint-declaration-merit-and-reward.md), [4.1](lessons/04-01-the-petrine-texts-argued.md), [4.2](lessons/04-02-what-the-patristic-record-proves.md), [4.4](lessons/04-04-the-visible-church-and-what-replaced-the-papacy.md), [6.1](lessons/06-01-mary.md), [6.2](lessons/06-02-the-saints-the-images-the-relics.md), [6.3](lessons/06-03-penance-purgatory-and-indulgences.md), [6.5](lessons/06-05-the-sacrifice-of-the-mass.md); also [4.3](lessons/04-03-apostolic-succession-and-the-validity-of-orders.md), [5.1](lessons/05-01-the-filioque-two-questions-kept-apart.md)

### Smalcald Articles

Luther's articles of 1537, prepared for a proposed council and later included in the [Book of Concord](#book-of-concord): the bluntest Lutheran confessional text on the Mass and the papacy.

**Standing.** Lutheran confessional standard; no Catholic level. Luther's, not Melanchthon's: Melanchthon signed them allowing that, if the pope would allow the Gospel, his superiority over the bishops might be conceded "by human right" [4.2](lessons/04-02-what-the-patristic-record-proves.md).

**What the lessons use** (Triglot). Part I, the Trinity and Incarnation, on which "there is no contention or dispute" [1.3](lessons/01-03-arguing-from-history-without-abusing-it.md); I.4, Christ born of the pure, holy Virgin, where the Triglot's English brackets "[and always]" for the Latin *semper virgine* [6.1](lessons/06-01-mary.md). II.1, justification "the first and chief article": the Lutheran objection that the gospel, not authority, is the root of the quarrel [1.1](lessons/01-01-shared-ground-and-where-the-argument-is.md), [1.3](lessons/01-03-arguing-from-history-without-abusing-it.md). II.2, the Mass ("this dragon's tail") and the invocation of saints as abuses against the chief article [6.2](lessons/06-02-the-saints-the-images-the-relics.md), [6.5](lessons/06-05-the-sacrifice-of-the-mass.md), [7.2](lessons/07-02-the-reformations-grievances.md). II.4, the pope "the very Antichrist" [1.2](lessons/01-02-what-the-church-says-about-other-christians.md). III.3, holy men who fall into manifest sins lose faith and the Spirit, against those who say such never had them: the Lutherans side with Trent VI can. 23 on losing grace [3.1](lessons/03-01-justification-as-the-confessions-state-it.md), [3.2](lessons/03-02-trent-session-vi-against-the-confessions.md). III.6, the Supper received by the wicked too [6.4](lessons/06-04-the-real-presence.md). III.8, confession retained, enumeration free [6.3](lessons/06-03-penance-purgatory-and-indulgences.md).

**Traps.** The Antichrist sentence is not Lutheran dogma binding as a magisterium's definition, nor was it amended away in 2013: *From Conflict to Communion* (§229) reports that Lutherans today reject Luther's identification, as a commission report binding no church and amending no confession [1.2](lessons/01-02-what-the-church-says-about-other-christians.md). Polemical wording: describe, quote short.

*Lessons:* [1.1](lessons/01-01-shared-ground-and-where-the-argument-is.md), [1.2](lessons/01-02-what-the-church-says-about-other-christians.md), [1.3](lessons/01-03-arguing-from-history-without-abusing-it.md), [3.1](lessons/03-01-justification-as-the-confessions-state-it.md), [3.2](lessons/03-02-trent-session-vi-against-the-confessions.md), [6.1](lessons/06-01-mary.md), [6.2](lessons/06-02-the-saints-the-images-the-relics.md), [6.3](lessons/06-03-penance-purgatory-and-indulgences.md), [6.5](lessons/06-05-the-sacrifice-of-the-mass.md), [7.2](lessons/07-02-the-reformations-grievances.md); also [4.2](lessons/04-02-what-the-patristic-record-proves.md), [6.4](lessons/06-04-the-real-presence.md)

### Formula of Concord

The Lutheran confession signed 29 May 1577 (Epitome and Solid Declaration) that settled the intra-Lutheran controversies after Luther's death: the last document of the [Book of Concord](#book-of-concord).

**Standing.** Lutheran confessional standard, subscribed as *norma normata* under Scripture (Bente reports about 8,000 signatures [4.4](lessons/04-04-the-visible-church-and-what-replaced-the-papacy.md)). Chemnitz was among its authors, which gives his own *Examen* no confessional standing [2.3](lessons/02-03-tradition-and-what-sufficiency-means.md), [3.2](lessons/03-02-trent-session-vi-against-the-confessions.md). No Catholic level.

**What the lessons use** (Triglot; Schaff prints the Epitome only).

- **Rule and Standard** (Epitome, "Of the Summary Content, Rule, and Standard"): Scripture "the sole rule and standard" and "the only judge"; other writings received only as witnesses; the three creeds pledged (§2); Augsburg, Apology, Smalcald Articles and Catechisms named (§3); no list of books [1.1](lessons/01-01-shared-ground-and-where-the-argument-is.md), [2.1](lessons/02-01-sola-scriptura-at-its-best.md), [2.2](lessons/02-02-the-canon-question.md), [2.3](lessons/02-03-tradition-and-what-sufficiency-means.md).
- **II, free will**: before conversion "only two efficient causes", the Spirit and the Word; SD II.88–89 on Luther's language of pure passivity; SD II.44 commends *Bondage of the Will* [2.4](lessons/02-04-perspicuity-tested.md), [3.2](lessons/03-02-trent-session-vi-against-the-confessions.md).
- **III, the righteousness of faith**: Epitome III's affirmative and negative theses and SD III.54–55, naming neither Osiander nor Stancarus. Their topics: what is imputed, what faith does ("means and instrument"), what "justify" means, faith never alone, and what is denied about infusion, indwelling and double justice. Boss 3(a) asks for the Lutheran statement from them; 3.1 teaches its parts [3.1](lessons/03-01-justification-as-the-confessions-state-it.md), [3.2](lessons/03-02-trent-session-vi-against-the-confessions.md). SD III.41–43 adopts the Apology's reading of James [3.3](lessons/03-03-paul-and-james.md).
- **IV, good works**: necessary as God's will; the phrase that good works are necessary to salvation rejected; faith lost by wilful sin; SD IV.38 promises a reward [3.1](lessons/03-01-justification-as-the-confessions-state-it.md), [3.4](lessons/03-04-the-joint-declaration-merit-and-reward.md).
- **VII and VIII, the Supper and Christ's person**: in, with and under; the sacramental union; the unworthy receive; nothing a sacrament apart from its use; against transubstantiation and the "Sacramentarians", gross and subtle (Ep. VII neg. 4 condemns reading the words of institution as obscure); Mary "truly is the mother of God" and a virgin [2.4](lessons/02-04-perspicuity-tested.md), [6.1](lessons/06-01-mary.md), [6.4](lessons/06-04-the-real-presence.md). XI, election [3.2](lessons/03-02-trent-session-vi-against-the-confessions.md).

**Traps.** "Alien righteousness" is not in the Epitome (the term is Luther's and the Apology's). The Lutheran confessions teach that the justified can fall (Ep. IV; Smalcald III.3): perseverance of the saints is Reformed (Westminster XVII) [3.1](lessons/03-01-justification-as-the-confessions-state-it.md), [3.2](lessons/03-02-trent-session-vi-against-the-confessions.md). Neither "sola scriptura" nor "norma normans" is in its text [2.1](lessons/02-01-sola-scriptura-at-its-best.md).

*Lessons:* [1.1](lessons/01-01-shared-ground-and-where-the-argument-is.md), [2.1](lessons/02-01-sola-scriptura-at-its-best.md), [2.3](lessons/02-03-tradition-and-what-sufficiency-means.md), [2.4](lessons/02-04-perspicuity-tested.md), [3.1](lessons/03-01-justification-as-the-confessions-state-it.md), [3.2](lessons/03-02-trent-session-vi-against-the-confessions.md), [3.3](lessons/03-03-paul-and-james.md), [3.4](lessons/03-04-the-joint-declaration-merit-and-reward.md), [6.1](lessons/06-01-mary.md), [6.4](lessons/06-04-the-real-presence.md); also [2.2](lessons/02-02-the-canon-question.md), [4.4](lessons/04-04-the-visible-church-and-what-replaced-the-papacy.md)

### Westminster Confession

The confession of the Westminster Assembly, completed December 1646 (Schaff dates it 1647 and prints the 1647 edition): the subordinate standard of Presbyterian churches.

**Standing.** Reformed (Presbyterian) **subordinate standard** under Scripture: officers vow to receive it, with strictness varying; amendable and amended (the American revision rewrote chiefly chs. XXIII and XXXI; the PCUSA rewrote XXV.6 in 1903; the OPC's edition omits the Antichrist clause). Not "the Protestant view", not Calvin, and not *solo scriptura*: XXXI.3 gives synods a real, ministerial authority [1.1](lessons/01-01-shared-ground-and-where-the-argument-is.md), [1.2](lessons/01-02-what-the-church-says-about-other-christians.md), [2.1](lessons/02-01-sola-scriptura-at-its-best.md), [4.4](lessons/04-04-the-visible-church-and-what-replaced-the-papacy.md). No Catholic level.

**Chapters the lessons use** (1647 numbering). **I**, Scripture: I.2–3 the sixty-six books and the Apocrypha "no part of the Canon"; I.4–5 authority from God, the Church's testimony and the Spirit's inward work; I.6 the whole counsel, "good and necessary consequence", nothing added by "traditions of men"; I.7 clarity under three qualifiers; I.9 Scripture interprets Scripture; I.10 the supreme judge, "the Holy Spirit speaking in the Scripture". Boss 2(a) close-reads I.4–5, I.7 and I.10 [1.1](lessons/01-01-shared-ground-and-where-the-argument-is.md), [2.1](lessons/02-01-sola-scriptura-at-its-best.md), [2.2](lessons/02-02-the-canon-question.md), [2.3](lessons/02-03-tradition-and-what-sufficiency-means.md), [2.4](lessons/02-04-perspicuity-tested.md), [6.1](lessons/06-01-mary.md), [6.6](lessons/06-06-development-or-corruption.md). **II.3** the Spirit "from the Father and the Son" [1.1](lessons/01-01-shared-ground-and-where-the-argument-is.md). **III, VIII** [1.1](lessons/01-01-shared-ground-and-where-the-argument-is.md), [3.2](lessons/03-02-trent-session-vi-against-the-confessions.md), [6.3](lessons/06-03-penance-purgatory-and-indulgences.md). **XI**, justification (XI.1 not by infusing, nor "imputing faith itself"; XI.2 faith "the alone instrument", yet "not alone"; XI.5 never fall, "fatherly displeasure") [3.1](lessons/03-01-justification-as-the-confessions-state-it.md), [3.2](lessons/03-02-trent-session-vi-against-the-confessions.md), [3.3](lessons/03-03-paul-and-james.md), [6.3](lessons/06-03-penance-purgatory-and-indulgences.md). **XIII, XV, XVI** (works the "fruits and evidences" of faith; no merit of pardon or eternal life) [3.1](lessons/03-01-justification-as-the-confessions-state-it.md), [3.3](lessons/03-03-paul-and-james.md), [3.4](lessons/03-04-the-joint-declaration-merit-and-reward.md), [4.2](lessons/04-02-what-the-patristic-record-proves.md), [6.3](lessons/06-03-penance-purgatory-and-indulgences.md). **XVII** perseverance and **XVIII** assurance [3.1](lessons/03-01-justification-as-the-confessions-state-it.md), [3.2](lessons/03-02-trent-session-vi-against-the-confessions.md). **XX.2, XXI** (worship to God alone, "not to angels, saints"; no prayer for the dead) [4.4](lessons/04-04-the-visible-church-and-what-replaced-the-papacy.md), [6.1](lessons/06-01-mary.md), [6.2](lessons/06-02-the-saints-the-images-the-relics.md), [6.3](lessons/06-03-penance-purgatory-and-indulgences.md). **XXIII.3** the 1647 magistrate [4.4](lessons/04-04-the-visible-church-and-what-replaced-the-papacy.md). **XXIV.5–6** divorce for adultery or desertion [5.5](lessons/05-05-marriage-divorce-and-the-state-of-the-dead.md). **XXV**, the Church (XXV.1 the invisible Church "the whole number of the elect"; XXV.2 the visible, outside which "no ordinary possibility of salvation"; XXV.5 the purity and error of particular churches; XXV.6 the pope) [1.2](lessons/01-02-what-the-church-says-about-other-christians.md), [4.1](lessons/04-01-the-petrine-texts-argued.md), [4.2](lessons/04-02-what-the-patristic-record-proves.md), [4.4](lessons/04-04-the-visible-church-and-what-replaced-the-papacy.md), [7.1](lessons/07-01-the-inquisition-and-the-galileo-affair.md), [7.3](lessons/07-03-the-abuse-crisis-and-the-argument-from-scandal.md). **XXVII, XXVIII** (sacraments; baptism) [2.4](lessons/02-04-perspicuity-tested.md), [7.2](lessons/07-02-the-reformations-grievances.md). **XXIX** (the Mass "most abominably injurious"; the Supper "spiritually") [6.4](lessons/06-04-the-real-presence.md), [6.5](lessons/06-05-the-sacrifice-of-the-mass.md). **XXX** censures and the keys [4.1](lessons/04-01-the-petrine-texts-argued.md), [4.4](lessons/04-04-the-visible-church-and-what-replaced-the-papacy.md), [7.3](lessons/07-03-the-abuse-crisis-and-the-argument-from-scandal.md). **XXXI.3** synods determine controversies "ministerially"; **XXXI.4** the fallibility of synods [1.1](lessons/01-01-shared-ground-and-where-the-argument-is.md), [2.1](lessons/02-01-sola-scriptura-at-its-best.md), [2.3](lessons/02-03-tradition-and-what-sufficiency-means.md), [4.4](lessons/04-04-the-visible-church-and-what-replaced-the-papacy.md), [5.3](lessons/05-03-vatican-i-read-by-orthodox-theologians.md), [6.2](lessons/06-02-the-saints-the-images-the-relics.md), [7.1](lessons/07-01-the-inquisition-and-the-galileo-affair.md). **XXXII.1** no third place [6.3](lessons/06-03-penance-purgatory-and-indulgences.md).
