Shorter Catechism Q.88–89, the ordinary means [2.4](lessons/02-04-perspicuity-tested.md). Boss 7(a) close-reads XXV.5 and XXXI.4.

**Traps.** XXXI.3 and XXXI.4 are XXXI.2 and XXXI.3 in the American numbering; XXV.6's text varies by church [1.2](lessons/01-02-what-the-church-says-about-other-christians.md), [4.4](lessons/04-04-the-visible-church-and-what-replaced-the-papacy.md). "Trent condemned Westminster" (it postdates Trent and no act names it) [3.2](lessons/03-02-trent-session-vi-against-the-confessions.md).

*Lessons:* [1.1](lessons/01-01-shared-ground-and-where-the-argument-is.md), [1.2](lessons/01-02-what-the-church-says-about-other-christians.md), [2.1](lessons/02-01-sola-scriptura-at-its-best.md), [2.2](lessons/02-02-the-canon-question.md), [2.3](lessons/02-03-tradition-and-what-sufficiency-means.md), [2.4](lessons/02-04-perspicuity-tested.md), [3.1](lessons/03-01-justification-as-the-confessions-state-it.md), [3.2](lessons/03-02-trent-session-vi-against-the-confessions.md), [3.3](lessons/03-03-paul-and-james.md), [3.4](lessons/03-04-the-joint-declaration-merit-and-reward.md), [4.1](lessons/04-01-the-petrine-texts-argued.md), [4.2](lessons/04-02-what-the-patristic-record-proves.md), [4.4](lessons/04-04-the-visible-church-and-what-replaced-the-papacy.md), [5.2](lessons/05-02-papal-primacy-as-the-east-reads-it.md), [5.3](lessons/05-03-vatican-i-read-by-orthodox-theologians.md), [5.5](lessons/05-05-marriage-divorce-and-the-state-of-the-dead.md), [6.1](lessons/06-01-mary.md), [6.2](lessons/06-02-the-saints-the-images-the-relics.md), [6.3](lessons/06-03-penance-purgatory-and-indulgences.md), [6.4](lessons/06-04-the-real-presence.md), [6.5](lessons/06-05-the-sacrifice-of-the-mass.md), [6.6](lessons/06-06-development-or-corruption.md), [7.1](lessons/07-01-the-inquisition-and-the-galileo-affair.md), [7.2](lessons/07-02-the-reformations-grievances.md), [7.3](lessons/07-03-the-abuse-crisis-and-the-argument-from-scandal.md)

### Heidelberg Catechism

The Reformed catechism of the Palatinate (1563): a confessional standard of the continental Reformed churches, approved at Dort.

**Standing.** Reformed confessional standard, not a magisterium; no Catholic level. Cited from Schaff vol. 3's English.

**Questions the lessons use.** Q.47–48, Christ's human nature "now not upon earth", his Godhead beyond the bounds of the manhood yet in it [6.4](lessons/06-04-the-real-presence.md). Q.60–61, righteous before God only by true faith in Christ, Christ's satisfaction and righteousness imputed, not the worthiness of faith [3.1](lessons/03-01-justification-as-the-confessions-state-it.md). Q.75–79, the Supper: the bread not changed, yet believers truly partake of his body and blood through the Spirit [6.4](lessons/06-04-the-real-presence.md). Q.83–85, the keys: preaching and church discipline [4.1](lessons/04-01-the-petrine-texts-argued.md). Q.94–98, invocation of saints forbidden under the first commandment; images [6.2](lessons/06-02-the-saints-the-images-the-relics.md).

**Trap.** Q.80 (the Mass an idolatry) is **not in the first edition**: Schaff's note says it was inserted later, a reply to Trent ([citations](#citations-and-how-to-read-them)).

*Lessons:* [3.1](lessons/03-01-justification-as-the-confessions-state-it.md), [4.1](lessons/04-01-the-petrine-texts-argued.md), [6.2](lessons/06-02-the-saints-the-images-the-relics.md), [6.4](lessons/06-04-the-real-presence.md)

### Calvin Institutes

Calvin's *Institutes of the Christian Religion* (final Latin edition 1559), cited in Beveridge's English (1845) by book, chapter and section: **a theologian's book, not a Reformed confession**.

**Standing.** Weighed by its arguments. Calvin is not "the Reformed position" (the Reformed confessions are Westminster, Heidelberg, Belgic and Second Helvetic) and not the Lutheran *Treatise*; "the Lutheran confessions and Calvin" are not one source [4.2](lessons/04-02-what-the-patristic-record-proves.md). No Catholic level.

**Sections the lessons use.** I.7.1–5 and I.8.12–13: Scripture's authority not from the Church, which "does not make that authentic which was otherwise doubtful"; Augustine's sentence read as the unbeliever's way in; "the secret testimony of the Spirit"; Scripture "carrying its own evidence along with it" [2.1](lessons/02-01-sola-scriptura-at-its-best.md), [2.2](lessons/02-02-the-canon-question.md), [2.3](lessons/02-03-tradition-and-what-sufficiency-means.md). I.11–12: images; *latria* and *dulia* [6.1](lessons/06-01-mary.md), [6.2](lessons/06-02-the-saints-the-images-the-relics.md). III.2: faith as firm and sure knowledge of God's favour [3.1](lessons/03-01-justification-as-the-confessions-state-it.md). III.4–5: confession, guilt and punishment, satisfaction, the treasury, purgatory, and the concession of "thirteen hundred years" of prayer for the dead [6.3](lessons/06-03-penance-purgatory-and-indulgences.md). III.11: justification, "a twofold benefit", against Osiander, the mystical union [3.1](lessons/03-01-justification-as-the-confessions-state-it.md). III.15: merit [3.4](lessons/03-04-the-joint-declaration-merit-and-reward.md). III.16.1 [3.1](lessons/03-01-justification-as-the-confessions-state-it.md). III.17.11–13: James, the "double paralogism" [3.3](lessons/03-03-paul-and-james.md). III.20: invocation [6.2](lessons/06-02-the-saints-the-images-the-relics.md). IV.1: the Church visible and invisible, the Church a mother to those whose Father is God, her marks, offence at her fruit not a reason to say "no church" [4.4](lessons/04-04-the-visible-church-and-what-replaced-the-papacy.md), [7.3](lessons/07-03-the-abuse-crisis-and-the-argument-from-scandal.md). IV.2 and IV.5 [7.2](lessons/07-02-the-reformations-grievances.md). IV.6–7: Peter and Rome, the "personal, or real, or mixed" privilege, Rome's "high honour" for three causes [4.1](lessons/04-01-the-petrine-texts-argued.md), [4.2](lessons/04-02-what-the-patristic-record-proves.md). IV.8.10 and 13: councils and the word [7.1](lessons/07-01-the-inquisition-and-the-galileo-affair.md). IV.12.1: discipline the Church's sinews [4.4](lessons/04-04-the-visible-church-and-what-replaced-the-papacy.md). IV.17: the Supper, ubiquity, "true and substantial communication" (IV.17.19, reserved for Boss 6(a)) [6.4](lessons/06-04-the-real-presence.md). IV.18: the Mass, five charges, and the concession that the Fathers leaned too far toward the law's shadows [6.5](lessons/06-05-the-sacrifice-of-the-mass.md).

**Traps.** Beveridge's wording, not Battles's ("twofold benefit", not "double grace"); Calvin's *Antidote* numbers Trent VI's chapters one higher; Calvin signed the *Variata* ([citations](#citations-and-how-to-read-them)) [2.1](lessons/02-01-sola-scriptura-at-its-best.md), [3.1](lessons/03-01-justification-as-the-confessions-state-it.md), [6.4](lessons/06-04-the-real-presence.md).

*Lessons:* [2.1](lessons/02-01-sola-scriptura-at-its-best.md), [2.2](lessons/02-02-the-canon-question.md), [2.3](lessons/02-03-tradition-and-what-sufficiency-means.md), [3.1](lessons/03-01-justification-as-the-confessions-state-it.md), [3.3](lessons/03-03-paul-and-james.md), [3.4](lessons/03-04-the-joint-declaration-merit-and-reward.md), [4.1](lessons/04-01-the-petrine-texts-argued.md), [4.2](lessons/04-02-what-the-patristic-record-proves.md), [4.3](lessons/04-03-apostolic-succession-and-the-validity-of-orders.md), [4.4](lessons/04-04-the-visible-church-and-what-replaced-the-papacy.md), [6.3](lessons/06-03-penance-purgatory-and-indulgences.md), [6.4](lessons/06-04-the-real-presence.md), [6.5](lessons/06-05-the-sacrifice-of-the-mass.md), [7.1](lessons/07-01-the-inquisition-and-the-galileo-affair.md), [7.3](lessons/07-03-the-abuse-crisis-and-the-argument-from-scandal.md); also [6.1](lessons/06-01-mary.md), [6.2](lessons/06-02-the-saints-the-images-the-relics.md)

### Thirty-Nine Articles

The Anglican Articles of Religion: Latin of 1563 (agreed in Convocation in 1562 by the English reckoning), English of 1571, American revision of 1801.

**Standing.** The Church of England's historic formularies, with the Prayer Book and the Ordinal (Canon A5); **subscription and authority vary across the Anglican Communion**. Not an Anglican magisterium binding every province alike, and Anglican, not "Protestant" or Reformed as a bloc, though Article XXVIII is Reformed in character [1.1](lessons/01-01-shared-ground-and-where-the-argument-is.md), [2.4](lessons/02-04-perspicuity-tested.md), [6.4](lessons/06-04-the-real-presence.md). No Catholic level.

**Articles the lessons use.** II, Mary named only here [6.1](lessons/06-01-mary.md). VI, Scripture "containeth all things necessary to salvation"; the canon, and the other books read for example of life and instruction of manners but not to establish doctrine [2.1](lessons/02-01-sola-scriptura-at-its-best.md), [2.2](lessons/02-02-the-canon-question.md), [2.3](lessons/02-03-tradition-and-what-sufficiency-means.md), [6.1](lessons/06-01-mary.md), [6.6](lessons/06-06-development-or-corruption.md). VII and VIII, the moral law and the three creeds (1801 drops the Athanasian) [1.1](lessons/01-01-shared-ground-and-where-the-argument-is.md). IX and XV [6.1](lessons/06-01-mary.md). XI and XII, justification "by Faith only, is a most wholesome Doctrine"; works "spring out necessarily" of faith [3.1](lessons/03-01-justification-as-the-confessions-state-it.md). XIX, the visible Church; Rome "hath erred ... in matters of Faith" [1.2](lessons/01-02-what-the-church-says-about-other-christians.md), [4.4](lessons/04-04-the-visible-church-and-what-replaced-the-papacy.md). XX, the Church's "authority in Controversies of Faith" under "God's Word written" [1.1](lessons/01-01-shared-ground-and-where-the-argument-is.md), [2.1](lessons/02-01-sola-scriptura-at-its-best.md), [2.3](lessons/02-03-tradition-and-what-sufficiency-means.md), [4.4](lessons/04-04-the-visible-church-and-what-replaced-the-papacy.md). XXI, of the authority of general councils (omitted in 1801; Boss 7(a) close-reads it) [4.4](lessons/04-04-the-visible-church-and-what-replaced-the-papacy.md), [5.3](lessons/05-03-vatican-i-read-by-orthodox-theologians.md), [6.2](lessons/06-02-the-saints-the-images-the-relics.md), [7.1](lessons/07-01-the-inquisition-and-the-galileo-affair.md), [7.3](lessons/07-03-the-abuse-crisis-and-the-argument-from-scandal.md). XXII, "the Romish Doctrine concerning Purgatory, Pardons", images, relics and invocation [6.1](lessons/06-01-mary.md), [6.2](lessons/06-02-the-saints-the-images-the-relics.md), [6.3](lessons/06-03-penance-purgatory-and-indulgences.md). XXIII and XXXVI, lawful calling and the Ordinal's sufficiency [4.3](lessons/04-03-apostolic-succession-and-the-validity-of-orders.md). XXV, penance not a sacrament of the Gospel [6.3](lessons/06-03-penance-purgatory-and-indulgences.md). [XXVI](#article-xxvi), the unworthiness of ministers [7.3](lessons/07-03-the-abuse-crisis-and-the-argument-from-scandal.md). XXVIII–XXIX, the Supper "after an heavenly and spiritual manner"; transubstantiation rejected [6.4](lessons/06-04-the-real-presence.md). XXXI, the one oblation and "the sacrifices of Masses" (Boss 6(b) reads it with Trent XXII and Tract 90; quote it in full or not at all) [4.3](lessons/04-03-apostolic-succession-and-the-validity-of-orders.md), [6.5](lessons/06-05-the-sacrifice-of-the-mass.md). XXXVII, the bishop of Rome without jurisdiction in England (1571; rewritten in 1801) [4.2](lessons/04-02-what-the-patristic-record-proves.md), [4.4](lessons/04-04-the-visible-church-and-what-replaced-the-papacy.md).

**Traps.** Cite from the Articles' own section of Schaff vol. 3, not the Methodist Articles; the Latin column labels XXX–XXXIX one lower; the modern-spelling column is the 1801 revision ([citations](#citations-and-how-to-read-them)). "Councils may err" is Article XXI, not XX [1.1](lessons/01-01-shared-ground-and-where-the-argument-is.md). Article XXII names no Marian doctrine, so reading it as a condemnation of prayer to Mary misreads it [6.1](lessons/06-01-mary.md); it says nothing of prayer for the dead (Westminster XXI.4 does) [6.3](lessons/06-03-penance-purgatory-and-indulgences.md); agreed in 1562, it cannot be aimed at Trent XXV (December 1563) [6.2](lessons/06-02-the-saints-the-images-the-relics.md). Article XX is not *solo scriptura* [1.1](lessons/01-01-shared-ground-and-where-the-argument-is.md).

*Lessons:* [1.1](lessons/01-01-shared-ground-and-where-the-argument-is.md), [1.2](lessons/01-02-what-the-church-says-about-other-christians.md), [1.3](lessons/01-03-arguing-from-history-without-abusing-it.md), [2.1](lessons/02-01-sola-scriptura-at-its-best.md), [2.2](lessons/02-02-the-canon-question.md), [2.3](lessons/02-03-tradition-and-what-sufficiency-means.md), [2.4](lessons/02-04-perspicuity-tested.md), [3.1](lessons/03-01-justification-as-the-confessions-state-it.md), [4.2](lessons/04-02-what-the-patristic-record-proves.md), [4.3](lessons/04-03-apostolic-succession-and-the-validity-of-orders.md), [4.4](lessons/04-04-the-visible-church-and-what-replaced-the-papacy.md), [5.3](lessons/05-03-vatican-i-read-by-orthodox-theologians.md), [6.1](lessons/06-01-mary.md), [6.2](lessons/06-02-the-saints-the-images-the-relics.md), [6.3](lessons/06-03-penance-purgatory-and-indulgences.md), [6.4](lessons/06-04-the-real-presence.md), [6.5](lessons/06-05-the-sacrifice-of-the-mass.md), [6.6](lessons/06-06-development-or-corruption.md), [7.1](lessons/07-01-the-inquisition-and-the-galileo-affair.md), [7.3](lessons/07-03-the-abuse-crisis-and-the-argument-from-scandal.md)

### Orthodox confessions of the seventeenth century

Peter Mogila's *Orthodox Confession* (1643) and the confession of Dositheus (1672): Orthodox replies to Reformation and Latin controversy.

**Standing.** Of **limited authority** among modern Orthodox, and partly Latinized; they do not settle Orthodox positions, and Orthodoxy has no magisterium issuing definitions the Western way [1.1](lessons/01-01-shared-ground-and-where-the-argument-is.md). No Catholic level. Other Orthodox texts the lessons weigh: the 1848 Encyclical of four patriarchates, Anthimos VII's 1895 encyclical (one patriarchate) and the Crete council of 2016 ([Weight inside other traditions](#weight-inside-other-traditions)).

- **Mogila on the dead** (qq. 64, 66): refuses purgatory and any cleansing punishment after death while praying for the dead [5.5](lessons/05-05-marriage-divorce-and-the-state-of-the-dead.md). Stating "the Orthodox view" from Mogila alone is the error; 5.5 sets it beside Mark of Ephesus, who wrote as a theologian, and the 1895 encyclical, one patriarchate's letter [5.5](lessons/05-05-marriage-divorce-and-the-state-of-the-dead.md), [6.2](lessons/06-02-the-saints-the-images-the-relics.md).
- 4.3 reports *Saepius Officio* (1897) appealing to the Eastern churches, whose catechisms (the Orthodox Confession of about 1640; the Russian Longer Catechism, 1839) name no power of sacrifice [4.3](lessons/04-03-apostolic-succession-and-the-validity-of-orders.md).

*Lessons:* [1.1](lessons/01-01-shared-ground-and-where-the-argument-is.md), [5.5](lessons/05-05-marriage-divorce-and-the-state-of-the-dead.md); also [4.3](lessons/04-03-apostolic-succession-and-the-validity-of-orders.md), [6.2](lessons/06-02-the-saints-the-images-the-relics.md)

## Terms

Technical vocabulary in first-appearance order across the course (the first lesson whose teaching uses the term). Greek is transliterated; Latin glosses follow the lessons' translations. A linked term has a full entry on this card.

| Term | Means | First used |
|---|---|---|
| [common inheritance](#common-inheritance) | what every tradition in the course already holds: the creeds, the Trinity and Incarnation, Scripture as inspired, Trinitarian baptism, the moral law | [1.1](lessons/01-01-shared-ground-and-where-the-argument-is.md) |
| [two axes](#two-axes) | the two questions under every intra-Christian dispute: the seat of authority (axis 1) and the rule of interpretation (axis 2) | [1.1](lessons/01-01-shared-ground-and-where-the-argument-is.md) |
| seat of authority | where final authority lies: Scripture the sole infallible rule (Lutheran, Reformed, Anglican, Baptist); Scripture and Tradition one deposit under a living magisterium (Catholic); Scripture within Tradition, received by the whole Church (Orthodox) | [1.1](lessons/01-01-shared-ground-and-where-the-argument-is.md) |
| [rule of interpretation](#rule-of-interpretation) | who settles a disputed reading of Scripture, and how; for Catholics, Trent IV's rule (the Church's sense, the Fathers' unanimous consent), **at least rung 3** | [1.1](lessons/01-01-shared-ground-and-where-the-argument-is.md) |
| leak | a point inside the shared ground that is itself disputed: the creed's text (filioque), the canon's extent, baptism's subjects, remarriage | [1.1](lessons/01-01-shared-ground-and-where-the-argument-is.md) |
| reduction thesis | disputes unresolved by exegesis and history end in a side's answer on an axis: a diagnostic, not a licence to skip exegesis | [1.1](lessons/01-01-shared-ground-and-where-the-argument-is.md) |
| [burden of the Catholic claim](#burden-of-the-catholic-claim) | the Catholic answer on both axes is the larger claim, so it carries the larger burden of proof | [1.1](lessons/01-01-shared-ground-and-where-the-argument-is.md) |
| [solo scriptura](#solo-scriptura) | the individual with a Bible apart from creeds and Church ("Tradition 0"): a position every confession rejects, often attacked under the name *sola scriptura* | [1.1](lessons/01-01-shared-ground-and-where-the-argument-is.md) |
| [reception](#reception) | (Orthodox) a council is known to be ecumenical when the whole Church recognizes its own faith in it: not a vote, not juridical ratification | [1.1](lessons/01-01-shared-ground-and-where-the-argument-is.md) |
| [conciliarity](#conciliarity) (*sobornost*) | the Orthodox ideal of catholicity as communion, truth kept by the whole Church in the unity of love (Khomiakov) | [1.1](lessons/01-01-shared-ground-and-where-the-argument-is.md) |
| noetic effects of sin | how far sin damaged reason: a real Catholic–Reformed difference ([`apologetics-foundations` 1.2](../apologetics-foundations/lessons/01-02-three-strategies.md)), not argued here | [1.1](lessons/01-01-shared-ground-and-where-the-argument-is.md) |
| [*subsistit in*](#subsistit-in) | LG 8: the one Church of Christ "subsists in" the Catholic Church, with elements of it found beyond her visible boundaries | [1.2](lessons/01-02-what-the-church-says-about-other-christians.md) |
| [elements of sanctification and truth](#elements-of-sanctification-and-truth) | gifts belonging to the Church that are found outside her visible boundaries (LG 8; UR 3) | [1.2](lessons/01-02-what-the-church-says-about-other-christians.md) |
| [imperfect communion](#imperfect-communion) | the real but incomplete communion of the baptized with the Catholic Church (UR 3) | [1.2](lessons/01-02-what-the-church-says-about-other-christians.md) |
| [Churches and ecclesial communities](#churches-and-ecclesial-communities) | the classification of *Dominus Iesus* 17 and the 2007 *Responses*: bodies keeping apostolic succession and the Eucharist are Churches; the others ecclesial communities (UR 22's *defectus ordinis*) | [1.2](lessons/01-02-what-the-church-says-about-other-christians.md) |
| [separated brethren](#separated-brethren) | UR's name for baptized Christians outside the Catholic Church, who have a right to be called Christian | [1.2](lessons/01-02-what-the-church-says-about-other-christians.md) |
| [false irenicism](#false-irenicism) | obscuring Catholic doctrine to reach agreement, which UR 11 forbids (**rung 3**) | [1.2](lessons/01-02-what-the-church-says-about-other-christians.md) |
| [hierarchy of truths](#hierarchy-of-truths) | UR 11: truths differ in their connection with the foundation of faith; an axis of centrality, **no rung** | [1.2](lessons/01-02-what-the-church-says-about-other-christians.md) |
| truth and fairness | UR 4's rule for describing other Christians | [1.2](lessons/01-02-what-the-church-says-about-other-christians.md) |
| *par cum pari* | "equal footing" (UR 9): governs the conduct of dialogue, not a verdict of equal ecclesial status | [1.2](lessons/01-02-what-the-church-says-about-other-christians.md) |
| *quandoque* | "at times": UR 3's separation came "not without fault" on both sides; the vatican.va English has "often enough" | [1.2](lessons/01-02-what-the-church-says-about-other-christians.md) |
| [reciprocity](#reciprocity) | the frame John Paul II named: "reciprocity is required" (*Ut Unum Sint* 29) | [1.2](lessons/01-02-what-the-church-says-about-other-christians.md) |
| [proselytism and witness](#proselytism-and-witness) | proselytism in its negative sense (improper pressure) against witness and proposal (*Doctrinal Note*, 2007, §12, n. 49) | [1.2](lessons/01-02-what-the-church-says-about-other-christians.md) |
| [*satis est*](#satis-est) | "it is enough": Augsburg VII's phrase on the Church's true unity (Boss 4(c) close-reads it) | [1.2](lessons/01-02-what-the-church-says-about-other-christians.md) |
| [*norma normans*, *norma normata*](#norma-normans-and-norma-normata) | the ruling rule (Scripture) and the ruled rule (the confessions): later Lutheran dogmatics' terms | [1.2](lessons/01-02-what-the-church-says-about-other-christians.md) |
| [*in necessariis unitas*](#in-necessariis-unitas) | *in necessariis unitatem ... in omnibus vero caritatem*: Meiderlin's tag (1626), often credited to Augustine | [1.2](lessons/01-02-what-the-church-says-about-other-christians.md) |
| "historical name" | what Crete (2016, §6) accepts for non-Orthodox Churches and Confessions: wording that names them without settling what they are | [1.2](lessons/01-02-what-the-church-says-about-other-christians.md) |
| rites of reception | how Orthodox churches receive Western converts: by baptism, chrismation or repentance (Moscow 2000, §1.17); practice varies | [1.2](lessons/01-02-what-the-church-says-about-other-christians.md) |
| [argument from silence](#argument-from-silence) | the two-sided rule: unattested in 150 is not thereby denied in 150, nor attested; silence counts only where a record was expected and would survive | [1.3](lessons/01-03-arguing-from-history-without-abusing-it.md) |
| expected record | the condition under which silence is evidence | [1.3](lessons/01-03-arguing-from-history-without-abusing-it.md) |
| silence as attestation | the inference that because nobody objected all held it: circular, since protest is expected only if the practice was already widespread | [1.3](lessons/01-03-arguing-from-history-without-abusing-it.md) |
| [anachronism](#anachronism); [cherry-picked Father](#cherry-picked-father); [manufactured consensus](#manufactured-consensus) | three of the abuses of history 1.3 names, with the argument from silence | [1.3](lessons/01-03-arguing-from-history-without-abusing-it.md) |
| [locus](#loci-and-historical-premises) | ([`fundamental-theology` 5.5](../fundamental-theology/lessons/05-05-the-loci-theologici.md)) the source a premise draws on; a historical premise caps the argument's note, not the claim's | [1.3](lessons/01-03-arguing-from-history-without-abusing-it.md) |
| *papa* | in the third century a title of bishops generally (Cyprian, Heraclas) | [1.3](lessons/01-03-arguing-from-history-without-abusing-it.md) |
| confessional history | history written to win a dispute (the Magdeburg *Centuries*, Baronius, Cochlaeus, Coxe; Maria Monk on the atrocity side) | [1.3](lessons/01-03-arguing-from-history-without-abusing-it.md) |
| Tertullian's prescription | the argument against novelty from priority | [1.3](lessons/01-03-arguing-from-history-without-abusing-it.md) |
| [Vincentian canon](#vincentian-canon) | what has been believed everywhere, always and by all (Vincent of Lérins, c. 434): **no magisterial level** | [1.3](lessons/01-03-arguing-from-history-without-abusing-it.md) |
| [Newman's notes](#newman-notes) | Newman's seven notes of a genuine development: **no magisterial level** | [1.3](lessons/01-03-arguing-from-history-without-abusing-it.md) |
| [development of doctrine](#development-of-doctrine) | growth in understanding of the same doctrine, in the same sense (*Dei Filius* ch. 4), as against corruption | [1.3](lessons/01-03-arguing-from-history-without-abusing-it.md) |
| [*sola scriptura*](#sola-scriptura) | Scripture the sole infallible rule, read in the Church under creeds and confessions as real but fallible authorities (Tradition I) | [2.1](lessons/02-01-sola-scriptura-at-its-best.md) |
| [Tradition 0, I, II, III](#tradition-i-and-tradition-ii) | Oberman's single-source Tradition I (tradition as the Church's handing on and reading of Scripture) and two-source Tradition II, extended by Mathison: Tradition 0 the individualist *solo scriptura*; Tradition III the magisterium as itself the norm | [2.1](lessons/02-01-sola-scriptura-at-its-best.md) |
| [rule and standard](#rule-and-norm) | the Formula's terms: Scripture alone judges; creeds and confessions are witnesses, "not judges" | [2.1](lessons/02-01-sola-scriptura-at-its-best.md) |
| subscription | binding oneself to a confession as a ruled rule under Scripture; churches differ in how much of the Book of Concord binds them | [2.1](lessons/02-01-sola-scriptura-at-its-best.md) |
| subordinate standard | the Presbyterian term for Westminster's standing under Scripture | [2.1](lessons/02-01-sola-scriptura-at-its-best.md) |
| [inward testimony of the Spirit](#inward-testimony-of-the-spirit) | the Spirit's witness that gives full persuasion of Scripture's authority (Westminster I.5; Calvin's "secret testimony") | [2.1](lessons/02-01-sola-scriptura-at-its-best.md) |
| *autopistos* | Calvin's word, "credible of itself"; Beveridge, "carrying its own evidence along with it" | [2.1](lessons/02-01-sola-scriptura-at-its-best.md) |
| good and necessary consequence | Westminster I.6's admission of doctrine deduced from Scripture | [2.1](lessons/02-01-sola-scriptura-at-its-best.md) |
| [ministerial authority](#ministerial-authority) | synods determine controversies "ministerially" (Westminster XXXI.3): real authority, under Scripture | [2.1](lessons/02-01-sola-scriptura-at-its-best.md) |
| [material and formal sufficiency](#material-and-formal-sufficiency) | material: every revealed truth is in Scripture, at least implicitly; formal: Scripture is also its own sufficient rule of interpretation | [2.1](lessons/02-01-sola-scriptura-at-its-best.md) |
| internal and external critique | arguing from the opponent's premises or from one's own ([`philosophical-method` 4.4](../philosophical-method/lessons/04-04-arguing-within-a-tradition-the-disputed-question.md)); "Trent settled it" is external | [2.1](lessons/02-01-sola-scriptura-at-its-best.md) |
| [canon](#canon-of-scripture) | the list of inspired books; on both sides the Church recognizes it, not confers it | [2.2](lessons/02-02-the-canon-question.md) |
| edge of the canon | the disputed books (the deuterocanon; Hebrews, James, Jude, 2 Peter, 2–3 John, Revelation; Esther) against the core all received | [2.2](lessons/02-02-the-canon-question.md) |
| [deuterocanonical, protocanonical](#deuterocanon) | post-Tridentine labels for slower and earlier reception: a historians' label, **no rank**; the "Apocrypha" of Protestant usage | [2.2](lessons/02-02-the-canon-question.md) |
| corporate reception | one of the three attributes of canonicity in Kruger's model; for Catholics, the act of a Church that cannot fail when she defines | [2.2](lessons/02-02-the-canon-question.md) |
| [self-authenticating canon](#self-authenticating-canon) | Kruger's model of the canon (*Canon Revisited*, 2012), taught in 2.2; Boss 2(b) asks for it as its defenders state it | [2.2](lessons/02-02-the-canon-question.md) |
| ecclesiastical books (*libri ecclesiastici*) | Rufinus's term, as Calvin reports it, for books read to the people but not used to establish doctrine | [2.2](lessons/02-02-the-canon-question.md) |
| [Luther's 1522 prefaces](#luthers-1522-prefaces) | the September Testament prefaces, setting Hebrews, James, Jude and Revelation apart, unnumbered: a theologian's, not confessional | [2.2](lessons/02-02-the-canon-question.md) |
| [*partim–partim*](#partim-partim) | "partly ... partly": the draft wording Trent replaced with "and", which left material sufficiency open (**rung 5**) | [2.3](lessons/02-03-tradition-and-what-sufficiency-means.md) |

## Positions and distinctions

**Arguing among Christians (Module 1)**

### Two axes

The two questions every dispute between Christians in this course ends on: where final authority lies, and who settles a disputed reading of it.

**Axis 1, the seat of authority; axis 2, the rule of interpretation** (1.1). The analogy: states sharing one constitution, quarrelling over what counts as law (the text alone, or the text with precedent) and over who settles a disputed reading. On 1.1's map (approximate; each tradition holds a range) the Lutheran, Reformed, Anglican and Baptist answers share one point on axis 1, scripture the sole infallible rule, and spread out on axis 2: the Book of Concord subscribed under scripture; Westminster's synods that determine controversies "ministerially" (XXXI.3); Article XX's Church with "authority in Controversies of Faith" under "God's Word written"; Baptist confessions adopted by associations. The Catholic answer: scripture and Tradition one deposit, the living magisterium its authentic interpreter ([Dei Verbum](#dei-verbum) 10). The Orthodox: scripture within Tradition, a council known by [reception](#reception).

**The reduction (1.1 P3).** A downstream dispute is argued on its own terms, but when exegesis and history do not settle it, each side's last appeal is its answer on an axis. The canon and the papacy are axis 1; justification becomes axis 1 once someone asks whether Trent's canons bind; the [filioque](#filioque) is axis 2 as doctrine and axis 1 as the lawfulness of an addition to a council's creed. **Rule:** locate a dispute on its axis before arguing it, and never argue the [common inheritance](#common-inheritance). In 1.2 the Catholic-Orthodox quarrel over where the one visible Church is, and whether communion with Rome is internal to it, is an axis-1 dispute between parties who both hold the Church one and visible.

**Limits.** The reduction says where an unresolved dispute will end, not that it has no content of its own: the [Joint Declaration](#joint-declaration) moved the material question on justification while its own §43 left the word of God and church doctrine, and ecclesial authority, still to be clarified (1.1 Ex. 2). Using the map to skip exegesis ("Trent settled it") gives an external reply to an internal critique ([`philosophical-method` 4.4](../philosophical-method/lessons/04-04-arguing-within-a-tradition-the-disputed-question.md)).

**Two objections to the frame itself (1.1).** *Lutheran:* the Smalcald Articles (II.1) make justification "the first and chief article", on which "all things depend which we teach and practise in opposition to the Pope" (Triglot), so the authority quarrel grew out of the gospel, not the reverse. *Orthodox:* Khomiakov calls "authority" itself a Western category ([conciliarity](#conciliarity)). Patristic ancestors: Tertullian's question of whose the scriptures are (*Prescription* 19) for axis 1; Vincent's one scripture read in many senses (*Commonitorium* 2) for axis 2.

*Lessons:* [1.1](lessons/01-01-shared-ground-and-where-the-argument-is.md), [1.2](lessons/01-02-what-the-church-says-about-other-christians.md), [1.3](lessons/01-03-arguing-from-history-without-abusing-it.md)

### Common inheritance

What every tradition in the course already holds, much of it in the same words: the creeds, the Trinity and the Incarnation, scripture as inspired, Trinitarian baptism, the moral law, the resurrection and the judgment.

**Checked per confession (1.1).** *Lutheran:* Augsburg I holds Nicaea's decree on the one essence and three persons "true and to be believed without any doubting" (Triglot); the Formula, Epitome, Rule and Norm §2, pledges the Apostles', Nicene and Athanasian creeds; Solid Declaration VIII cites Chalcedon by name. *Anglican:* Article VIII receives the three creeds; Article VII binds every Christian to the commandments "which are called Moral". *Reformed:* Belgic IX receives the three creeds; the Second Helvetic (XI) professes what the creeds and the first four councils "defined out of the Holy Scriptures". **Westminster has no creed article:** it restates the Trinity (II.3) and the two natures "without conversion, composition, or confusion" (VIII.2), and the oldest editions of its Shorter Catechism annex the Apostles' Creed as "a brief sum of the Christian faith, agreeable to the Word of God", not as scripture. *Orthodox:* the Nicene-Constantinopolitan creed is shared; the Apostles' Creed is a Western baptismal creed with no place in Orthodox worship.

**The leaks**, where the shared ground is itself disputed: the creed's *text* (every Western confession carries the [filioque](#filioque), Westminster II.3 included; 5.1); scripture's *extent* ([deuterocanon](#deuterocanon); 2.2); baptism's *subjects*, disputed with Baptists and not argued in this course ([`sacraments-and-liturgy` 2.2](../sacraments-and-liturgy/lessons/02-02-necessity-infants-and-baptism-of-desire-and-blood.md)); Orthodox practice in receiving Western converts, which varies (1.2); remarriage (5.5).

**Levels (1.1).** The creedal core (the Son *homoousios*; one person in two natures): **rung 1** (Nicaea, Constantinople, Chalcedon). Scripture inspired, God its author: **rung 1** (Trent IV; *Dei Filius*, canons on revelation, 4). Baptism given by heretics in the Trinitarian name, with the intention of doing what the Church does, is true baptism: **rung 1** (Trent VII, Canons on Baptism, 4); *Unitatis Redintegratio* 22's sacramental bond of unity: **rung 3**; CCC 818 and 1271 locate and fix nothing.

**Traps.** Saying every Reformation confession has a creed article (Westminster has none; the American revision of Article VIII, 1801, drops the Athanasian Creed). Treating baptism as wholly shared. Arguing God's existence, the resurrection or scripture's reliability with a fellow Christian: that is [`apologetics-foundations`](../apologetics-foundations/syllabus.md)' work against non-Christians, and here it loses the thread. The noetic effects of sin are a real Catholic-Reformed difference about reason, but about method, and not argued here ([`apologetics-foundations` 1.2](../apologetics-foundations/lessons/01-02-three-strategies.md)). On the inheritance the other traditions are not "less Christian" (1.1's concession).

*Lessons:* [1.1](lessons/01-01-shared-ground-and-where-the-argument-is.md), [1.3](lessons/01-03-arguing-from-history-without-abusing-it.md)

### Burden of the Catholic claim

The Catholic answer is the larger claim on both axes, so the Catholic carries the larger burden of proof, though never the only one.

**Why larger (1.1 P4).** Scripture and Tradition one deposit whose authentic interpreter is the living magisterium (DV 10); papal definitions irreformable "of themselves, and not from the consent of the Church" and a universal ordinary jurisdiction (*Pastor Aeternus* chs. 4 and 3, Schaff); Marian definitions (1854, 1950) that only Rome has made. **What conceding it costs:** on [`philosophical-method` 4.2](../philosophical-method/lessons/04-02-steelmanning-and-the-burden-of-proof.md)'s grounds of presumption, possession would favour the older institution and "what both sides grant" favours scripture; a Catholic who concedes the larger burden gives up the possession ground.

**Larger is not only.** Whether the "alone" of *sola scriptura* is a further thesis with a burden of its own, or the bare residue left when the Catholic addition fails, is 1.1 P3's crux; presumption is procedural and only orders the debate.

**Where the burden bites later.** At the canon: a warrant for an infallible recognizer that does not presuppose it ([motive of credibility](#motive-of-credibility); 2.2). At the Petrine texts: a principled way to tell the unrepeatable parts of an apostle's commission from the permanent ones (4.1). At development: showing that "development" is not an alibi that absorbs any evidence, tested on the thinnest-attested distinctive, the Assumption (6.6).

*Lessons:* [1.1](lessons/01-01-shared-ground-and-where-the-argument-is.md), [2.2](lessons/02-02-the-canon-question.md), [4.1](lessons/04-01-the-petrine-texts-argued.md), [6.6](lessons/06-06-development-or-corruption.md)

### Solo scriptura

The individual alone with a Bible, judging apart from creeds, confessions and church: a position the Reformation confessions reject, and the commonest caricature of *sola scriptura*.

**The label.** Keith Mathison's (*The Shape of Sola Scriptura*, 2001), his "Tradition 0": the view of the Radical Reformation and of many modern evangelicals, never the Reformers' ([Tradition I and Tradition II](#tradition-i-and-tradition-ii)). A useful label, not confessional language; no tradition's confession holds what it names.

**The confessions reject it.** The [Second Helvetic](#second-helvetic-confession) (II) refuses "all kinds of exposition" and reads only "with the rule of faith and charity"; Westminster I.10 subjects "private spirits" to the Spirit speaking in scripture, and XXXI.3 gives synods power to determine controversies ministerially; the Formula keeps the creeds as binding witnesses; Article XX gives the Church authority in controversies of faith; Baptist confessions are adopted by associations and congregations.

**The blur, in its three forms.** (1) "Me and my Bible" for *sola scriptura*: 2.1's concession is that Catholic polemic attacking it attacks a position the Formula, Westminster and the Second Helvetic themselves reject; it refutes those who practise it, not the confessions. (2) "Protestants reject tradition": they reject unwritten traditions as a second rule and keep creeds, Fathers and councils as witnesses and helps (2.3). (3) The clarity form, "every verse is plain to every reader" or "you need no pastor, creed or council": Westminster I.7 opens by denying that all things are "alike plain" (2.4). The blur also runs from inside: a Reformed speaker who calls her faith "the believer and the Bible, as Westminster says" caricatures her own confession (2.3 P3). A reply in Boss 2(d) that argues against *solo scriptura* fails.

*Lessons:* [1.1](lessons/01-01-shared-ground-and-where-the-argument-is.md), [2.1](lessons/02-01-sola-scriptura-at-its-best.md), [2.3](lessons/02-03-tradition-and-what-sufficiency-means.md), [2.4](lessons/02-04-perspicuity-tested.md), [3.1](lessons/03-01-justification-as-the-confessions-state-it.md)

### Reception

In Orthodox ecclesiology, a council is known to be ecumenical when the whole Church recognizes in it, over time, its own faith: not a vote and not a juridical ratification.

**The Orthodox seat of authority (1.1).** On Kallistos Ware's account (*The Orthodox Church*, a theologian's book), scripture is the chief expression of Tradition, read within the Church with the councils, the Fathers and the liturgy, not set over it; no single organ settles a reading. The root text is the [1848 Encyclical](#encyclical-of-the-eastern-patriarchs-1848) §17, paraphrased (its English has no recorded translator): neither patriarchs nor councils could bring in novelties, because the whole body of the Church, the people as well as the bishops, guards the faith. Standing: a synodal letter of four patriarchates, weighty, not a council; Orthodoxy has no magisterium issuing definitions the Western way.

**Uses.** *5.1:* the Orthodox do not receive Florence, whose union was rejected in the East, so citing its definition of the addition's lawfulness against them begs the question. *5.4 and 6.1:* the Palamite councils of 1341-1351 were local councils whose teaching binds across Orthodoxy by reception (the 1351 Tome, the *Synodikon of Orthodoxy*, the canonization of 1368), not by a magisterium's definition: the Tome is neither "Orthodoxy's papal definition" nor a mere local synod's opinion.

**Against Vatican I (5.3; Boss 5(b) asks for both accounts and the premise dividing them, so outline only here).** 5.3 supplies the Orthodox parts: Georges Florovsky's account ("The Authority of the Ancient Councils and the Tradition of the Fathers", 1967) and its precedents (Ephesus 449, the Robber Synod; Hieria 754; Florence). It supplies the Catholic parts: what makes a council ecumenical on the Catholic account (LG 22; the Code) and what reception adds, with Newman's postscript to the *Letter to the Duke of Norfolk*; their levels are in [Levels of authority used in this course](#levels-of-authority-used-in-this-course).

**Traps.** Reception as a plebiscite or majority vote (a caricature that fails 5.3's problems). Putting "guardian" in quotation marks as the 1848 text's word: paraphrase it. Treating Ware or the Encyclical as "the Orthodox position" the way a council would state it (1.1).

*Lessons:* [1.1](lessons/01-01-shared-ground-and-where-the-argument-is.md), [5.1](lessons/05-01-the-filioque-two-questions-kept-apart.md), [5.3](lessons/05-03-vatican-i-read-by-orthodox-theologians.md), [5.4](lessons/05-04-essence-and-energies.md), [6.1](lessons/06-01-mary.md)

### Conciliarity

Two senses in this course: Khomiakov's *sobornost*, the truth kept by the whole Church in the unity of love (1.1); and synodal structure, a first who acts with the many at every level (5.2).

**Sobornost (1.1).** Aleksei Khomiakov (1804-1860) argued that Rome and the Reformation are two forms of one error, external authority on one side and private judgment on the other, and that the truth is kept by the whole Church in the unity of love. 1.1 uses it as the Orthodox objection to framing axis 1 as "authority" at all ([two axes](#two-axes)). Paraphrased; a theologian, **no Catholic level**.

**Synodality (5.2).** Authority works in synod: a region's bishops do nothing weighty without their first, nor he without them ([Apostolic Canon 34](#apostolic-canon-34)); it sits beside [taxis](#taxis) and [pentarchy](#pentarchy). Stated honestly: emperors convened and enforced the ancient councils, and Rome never convened or personally presided over one (Ravenna §42). That weakens any claim that conciliar authority flowed from Rome, but is no Orthodox trump, since neither side now derives it from an emperor. Ravenna and Chieti: **no magisterial level**.

*Lessons:* [1.1](lessons/01-01-shared-ground-and-where-the-argument-is.md), [5.2](lessons/05-02-papal-primacy-as-the-east-reads-it.md)

### Dei Verbum

Vatican II's Dogmatic Constitution on Divine Revelation (18 November 1965): scripture and Tradition one deposit committed to the Church; authentic interpretation entrusted to the living teaching office, which serves the word. Copyrighted: paraphrase.

**What the lessons use.** *DV 8:* through Tradition the Church's full canon is known (2.2, **rung 3**); the understanding of what was handed on grows (6.6, **at least rung 3**). *DV 9:* the Church does not draw her certainty about everything revealed from scripture alone, which left material sufficiency open ([`fundamental-theology` 3.4](../fundamental-theology/lessons/03-04-tradition.md)). *DV 10:* one sacred deposit committed to the whole Church; the task of authentically interpreting the word, written or handed on, entrusted *exclusively* to the living teaching office, which is not above the word but serves it. *DV 11* restates [Dei Filius](#dei-filius) ch. 2. *DV 25* urges all the faithful to read scripture often (CCC 133).

**Levels.** DV 10's account of the magisterium: **at least rung 3** (1.1). The denial of formal sufficiency (DV 9-10, with Trent IV): **at least rung 3**. DV 10's "exclusively": **at least rung 3** (it restates; Vatican II defined nothing new).

**Traps.** "Exclusively" governs *authentic* interpretation, not who may read scripture; the deposit is committed to the whole Church, and "the Pope above the Bible" ignores that the office serves the word (2.3 P3). DV 10 is also the Catholic text that, like the Formula and Article XX, puts the interpreting authority under the word (1.1 P2; 2.1 P2).

*Lessons:* [1.1](lessons/01-01-shared-ground-and-where-the-argument-is.md), [2.1](lessons/02-01-sola-scriptura-at-its-best.md), [2.2](lessons/02-02-the-canon-question.md), [2.3](lessons/02-03-tradition-and-what-sufficiency-means.md), [2.4](lessons/02-04-perspicuity-tested.md)

### Filioque

The Latin addition "and the Son" to the creed's article on the Spirit's procession: the one clause that divides a creed every tradition here otherwise shares.

**Two questions, kept apart (5.1).** (i) Is it **true** that the Spirit proceeds from the Father and the Son? (ii) Was it **lawful** to insert that into the creed of 381? Four positions: true and lawful (the Catholic position since Florence defined both); true but not to be inserted (Leo III, 810); false and unlawful (Photius and most Orthodox writers); false but lawful (no one). Every Western confession carries the clause (Westminster II.3; the Athanasian Creed). On 1.1's map it is axis 2 as doctrine and axis 1 as lawfulness.

**Levels (5.1).** Procession from Father and Son as from one principle by one spiration: **rung 1** (Lyons II's constitution; Florence). The lawfulness of the addition (Florence, DS 1302): **rung 2**, floor **at least rung 3**, so not freely disputed: a Catholic may concede the manner was hard to defend and that nothing binds Greeks to recite the clause now, but may not call the addition unlawful ([lawfulness of the addition](#lawfulness-of-the-addition)). Reciting the creed without the clause is discipline.

**In 1.3.** The 1848 Encyclical says the filioque has no ground in the Fathers and calls the Latin witnesses adulterations (§5, paraphrased): an Orthodox [manufactured consensus](#manufactured-consensus). Lossky's charge, and the premise a Catholic must deny to hold both the doctrine and the lawfulness, are Boss 5(a)'s: see 5.1.

*Lessons:* [1.1](lessons/01-01-shared-ground-and-where-the-argument-is.md), [1.3](lessons/01-03-arguing-from-history-without-abusing-it.md), [5.1](lessons/05-01-the-filioque-two-questions-kept-apart.md)

### Joint Declaration

The Lutheran World Federation-Catholic declaration on the doctrine of justification (text finished 1997), confirmed at Augsburg on 31 October 1999 through the [Official Common Statement with its Annex](#official-common-statement-and-annex).

**What it claims** (copyrighted: paraphrase). A consensus in basic truths of the doctrine (§§40, 43), the remaining differences being of language, theological elaboration and emphasis (§40); the Lutheran teaching *as presented in it* does not fall under Trent's condemnations (§41); §43 leaves for clarification the relation of the word of God and church doctrine, ecclesiology, ecclesial authority, church unity, ministry and the sacraments. On merit, a common confession that God accepts us by grace, owing nothing to any merit of ours (§15) and a Catholic explication of "meritorious" as a reward in heaven promised to works that remain gifts (§38). "Differentiated consensus" is ecumenists' later name for the method, not a phrase in the 1999 texts ([differentiated consensus](#differentiated-consensus)).

**Level.** **No rung of its own**; it defines and revokes nothing ([`creation-grace-last-things` 5.2](../creation-grace-last-things/lessons/05-02-the-1999-joint-declaration.md)), yet it is an official act of the Church as a party, not a theologians' paper. Whether §41 is correct, and whether a given confession falls under a canon: **rung 5**.

**In 1.1** it is the strain case for the two axes: the material question moved while §43 left the axis questions open. **In 3.4** the open question is whether it is real agreement or a postponement, tested on merit (Trent VI can. 32, **rung 1**, against §38 and the Annex). **Trap:** "the Declaration was signed in 1999": the Official Common Statement and Annex were.

*Lessons:* [1.1](lessons/01-01-shared-ground-and-where-the-argument-is.md), [1.3](lessons/01-03-arguing-from-history-without-abusing-it.md), [3.1](lessons/03-01-justification-as-the-confessions-state-it.md), [3.2](lessons/03-02-trent-session-vi-against-the-confessions.md), [3.4](lessons/03-04-the-joint-declaration-merit-and-reward.md)

### Subsistit in

LG 8: the one Church of Christ subsists in, continues whole in, the Catholic Church, while elements of the Church are found beyond her visible structure.

1.2 reloads the doctrine from [`ecclesiology-and-mariology` 5.2](../ecclesiology-and-mariology/lessons/05-02-subsistit-in-and-the-status-of-other-churches.md) in one sentence and does not re-derive it; here it is a constraint on how the apologist argues, not a thesis argued. Levels as that course grades them: LG 8 as an act **at least rung 3**; Rome's one-subsistence reading **rung 3**; no act defines *subsistit*. With the [elements of sanctification and truth](#elements-of-sanctification-and-truth) and [imperfect communion](#imperfect-communion) it is why the other side is argued with as kin.

*Lessons:* [1.2](lessons/01-02-what-the-church-says-about-other-christians.md)

### Elements of sanctification and truth

Gifts proper to the Church of Christ (scripture, the life of grace, faith, hope and charity, baptism, in some bodies orders and the Eucharist) really present beyond the Catholic Church's visible boundaries.

LG 8 and UR 3: **rung 3**. UR 15: the Eastern churches have true sacraments (1.2 Ex. 1). In 1.2 they are part of the reason the other side is argued with as kin, not as outsiders; the doctrine is [`ecclesiology-and-mariology` 5.2](../ecclesiology-and-mariology/lessons/05-02-subsistit-in-and-the-status-of-other-churches.md)'s.

*Lessons:* [1.2](lessons/01-02-what-the-church-says-about-other-christians.md)

### Imperfect communion

Those who believe in Christ and are truly baptized are in real, though imperfect, communion with the Catholic Church.

**The doctrine (1.2 P1).** UR 3 (with DI 17 and LG 15): **rung 3**, resting on the **rung 1** truth that baptism given outside the Church in the Trinity's name, with the Church's intention, is true baptism (Trent VII, *On Baptism*, can. 4, 3 March 1547; [`sacraments-and-liturgy` 2.2](../sacraments-and-liturgy/lessons/02-02-necessity-infants-and-baptism-of-desire-and-blood.md)). In words: he is a brother in Christ. **The born-into clause (1.2 P2):** those born into the separated communities cannot be charged with the sin of separation, which came about *quandoque non sine hominum utriusque partis culpa* (UR 3, **rung 3**). The `ecclesiology-and-mariology` card calls the same doctrine full and partial communion.

**Traps.** Citing CCC 1271 or UR 22 as what makes the baptism teaching rung 1: Trent VII can. 4 does, and UR 22's bond stays rung 3 (1.1). Reading "both sides" as equal blame: it assigns no shares, and *quandoque* means "at times" (as `ecclesiology-and-mariology` 5.3 renders it; vatican.va's "often enough" stretches the word). The Christian you argue with is not guilty of the separation at all (1.2's concession).

*Lessons:* [1.2](lessons/01-02-what-the-church-says-about-other-christians.md)

### Churches and ecclesial communities

The Catholic classification: the separated Eastern bodies keep apostolic succession and a valid Eucharist and are true particular Churches; the Reformation bodies, lacking succession in the sacrament of Orders, are called ecclesial communities, not Churches in the proper sense.

**Level: rung 3, defining nothing.** UR 22 (*defectus ordinis*); *Dominus Iesus* 17 (2000); CDF, *Responses to Some Questions Regarding Certain Aspects of the Doctrine on the Church* (29 June 2007), Q4-5, ratified and confirmed by Benedict XVI. The accompanying commentary carries no approval of its own and admits the language caused no little distress in the communities concerned and among some Catholics. 1.2 **states** the criterion and does not argue it; 4.3 argues its key input, the validity of orders.

**What it does and does not do.** It grades structure, not faith or holiness. It does not touch brotherhood, which rests on baptism ([imperfect communion](#imperfect-communion)). *Responses* Q4 also admits that division leaves the Catholic Church's own universality not fully realised. UR 9's equal footing (*par cum pari*) governs the conduct of dialogue, not a prior verdict that the ecclesiologies are equally true (1.2 P3).

**The Lutheran ecclesiology, as its confessions state it (1.2).** Augsburg VII marks the Church by the Gospel rightly taught and the sacraments rightly administered: for true unity "it is enough to agree concerning the doctrine of the Gospel and the administration of the Sacraments" (Triglot; [satis est](#satis-est)). The [Treatise](#treatise-on-the-power-and-primacy-of-the-pope): bishop and pastor do not differ by divine right, so "ordination administered by a pastor in his own church is valid by divine law". On these premises succession is not constitutive and "ecclesial community" begs the question; Ishmael Noko (LWF general secretary, 2007) said Lutheran churches understand themselves to be fully church (an official response, no confessional standing). *Reformed:* Setri Nyomi for the World Alliance of Reformed Churches (2007): their place in the church is God's gift, not dependent on the Vatican's interpretation. Both responses via `ecclesiology-and-mariology` 5.2.

**Traps.** Treating the Lutheran objection as hurt feelings: it is an ecclesiology, so answer the criterion. Stating it as "Lutherans believe in an invisible church" or "reject ordination" (caricature). Saying the *Responses* defined the label (overstates rung 3).

*Lessons:* [1.2](lessons/01-02-what-the-church-says-about-other-christians.md)

### Hierarchy of truths

UR 11: Catholic truths vary in their relation to the foundation of the Christian faith; an order of exposition in dialogue, not a scale of authority.

**Level: no rung** (an axis of centrality, not of firmness: [`fundamental-theology` 4.5](../fundamental-theology/lessons/04-05-reading-a-documents-weight.md)). **Its fence,** in the same paragraph: the doctrine presented whole, with no [false irenicism](#false-irenicism) (**rung 3**); and *Mysterium Ecclesiae* 4 (CDF, 1973, ratified by Paul VI): some dogmas rest on others, but all, being revealed, are believed with the same divine faith (**rung 3**). 1.2 uses it only inside P3 (the doctrine whole); its exercises belong to `fundamental-theology` 4.5 and `ecclesiology-and-mariology` 5.3. **Trap:** reading distance from the foundation as permission to treat a defined doctrine as negotiable.

*Lessons:* [1.2](lessons/01-02-what-the-church-says-about-other-christians.md)

### False irenicism

Peace bought by clouding the genuine meaning of Catholic doctrine; UR 11 says nothing is so foreign to ecumenism.

**Rung 3** (UR 11's fence; Latin *falsus irenismus*). In 1.2 it is the opposite failure to polemic: "he is a brother, so keep quiet" denies P3 (the doctrine whole, UR 11; *Mysterium Ecclesiae* 4) and P5 (the right to propose the faith). The fault is against truth, not an excess of charity. A ground rule that no one may say their church is the true Church, or that another lacks anything, is false irenicism; it would also gag the other side's own texts (Article XIX's Rome "hath erred"), so it is not even neutral (1.2 P2). Every ecclesiology at the table makes claims about the others ([reciprocity](#reciprocity)).

*Lessons:* [1.2](lessons/01-02-what-the-church-says-about-other-christians.md), [2.1](lessons/02-01-sola-scriptura-at-its-best.md)

### Separated brethren

*Unitatis Redintegratio*'s name for baptized Christians not in full communion: brothers, with a right to be called Christian (UR 3), who are separated.

**Rung 3** (UR 3). 1.2's point: the phrase is a reason to argue as with kin, never a reason not to argue. UR 4 makes dialogue the place where each side explains its distinctive teaching in depth; UR 11 requires the doctrine whole; the 2007 *Doctrinal Note* §12 keeps the right to propose the faith ([proselytism and witness](#proselytism-and-witness)). **Trap:** "Vatican II forbids Catholics to argue for the papacy with separated brethren, so we will mostly listen" misreads UR 4 and 11 (1.2 P2).

*Lessons:* [1.2](lessons/01-02-what-the-church-says-about-other-christians.md)

### Unitatis Redintegratio

Vatican II's Decree on Ecumenism (21 November 1964): a conciliar decree that defines nothing; its own teaching **rung 3**.

**What the course uses (1.2).** *UR 3:* imperfect communion; fault "at times" on both sides (*quandoque*); those born into separated communities not charged with the separation; [separated brethren](#separated-brethren). *UR 4:* ecumenical work begins by avoiding words, judgments and actions that do not represent the separated with truth and fairness (*secundum aequitatem et veritatem*); an individual's reconciliation is distinct from ecumenical action but not opposed to it; its Latin echoes the [in necessariis](#in-necessariis-unitas) tag. *UR 9:* study the others' outlook; meet on an equal footing, *par cum pari*. *UR 10:* theology taught *non polemice*, not polemically. *UR 11:* the doctrine whole, no [false irenicism](#false-irenicism), terms the others can really understand, the [hierarchy of truths](#hierarchy-of-truths). *UR 15:* the Eastern churches' true sacraments. *UR 22:* baptism's sacramental bond of unity; *defectus ordinis* ([churches and ecclesial communities](#churches-and-ecclesial-communities)).

**Levels (1.2).** UR 3 and 22: **rung 3**. UR 4, 9 and 10, part of the decree's teaching that ecumenism is every Catholic's duty: **rung 3**; "truth and fairness" also applies the moral law against false witness, which binds on its own, so a misquoted confession is a moral failure. The hierarchy of truths: **no rung**.

**Traps.** Calling UR 4's truth and fairness etiquette (understated). Reading *par cum pari* as a verdict of equal ecclesial status (it governs the conduct of dialogue). Faulting an Anglican or Lutheran for not obeying UR: it binds Catholics; the question is what a Catholic can keep (1.2 P2).

*Lessons:* [1.2](lessons/01-02-what-the-church-says-about-other-christians.md), [2.1](lessons/02-01-sola-scriptura-at-its-best.md)

### Reciprocity

*Ut Unum Sint* 29 (1995): the duty to avoid misrepresenting the other side binds every party to dialogue; reciprocity is required.

**Rung 3** (an encyclical restating UR 4). **1.2's frame for "the other side":** nobody's ecclesiology is polite. Augsburg VII's test judges Rome too; the Smalcald Articles (II.4) call the pope "the very Antichrist"; Westminster XXV.6 in its 1646 text calls him "that Antichrist, that man of sin and son of perdition"; Article XIX: the Church of Rome "hath erred ... also in matters of Faith"; Crete 2016 §1 and Moscow 2000 §1.1 identify the Orthodox Church with the one Church.

**Their standing (1.2).** Smalcald stands in the Book of Concord, a *norma normata*, not a magisterium; *From Conflict to Communion* (2013) §229 reports that Lutherans today reject the Antichrist identification, a commission's report that amends no confession and binds no church. Westminster's text varies by church (the Orthodox Presbyterian Church's edition omits the clause). The Articles' authority varies across the Anglican Communion. All: **no Catholic level**. Traps both ways: "binding Lutheran dogma" and "repealed in 2013"; "every Presbyterian confesses XXV.6".

**The crux it answers (1.2).** The "keep quiet" view must deny that exclusive claims are compatible with brotherhood; reciprocity is the Catholic evidence against it, since every party at the table makes such claims. What would move it: evidence that exclusive claims, even fairly stated, poison partnership.

*Lessons:* [1.2](lessons/01-02-what-the-church-says-about-other-christians.md)

### Proselytism and witness

Proselytism in the negative sense is promoting a faith by means and motives contrary to the Gospel; proposing the faith in full to another Christian who freely wishes to receive it is not.

**The text.** CDF, *Doctrinal Note on Some Aspects of Evangelization* (3 December 2007), §12 with n. 49: ecumenism removes neither the right nor the responsibility to propose the Catholic faith in full to other Christians who freely wish to receive it; one who asks from conviction to be received is the Spirit's work, not proselytism in the negative sense; undue pressure is excluded. **Level:** **rung 3, light grade** (approved by Benedict XVI in ordinary form, not "ratified and confirmed"). The negative sense in n. 49 is borrowed from *The Challenge of Proselytism and the Calling to Common Witness* (1995, a joint Catholic-World Council of Churches study): **no magisterial level**.

**1.2 P5:** the line falls at means, not conclusions. **Where it stops (1.2 Ex. 2):** it regulates means and motives and forbids no outcome; no text ranks urging an individual's reception now against working for corporate reconciliation, so that is prudence, and claiming the documents decide it overstates them. **Traps:** reading the Note as forbidding attempts to convince a Lutheran (1.2 P1(e)); seeking a crowd's verdict (a show of hands) instead of free assent, which is a rhetorical win (1.2 P2).

*Lessons:* [1.2](lessons/01-02-what-the-church-says-about-other-christians.md)

### Treatise on the Power and Primacy of the Pope

Melanchthon's treatise, subscribed at Smalcald in 1537 and printed in the Book of Concord: the Lutheran confessional text on the papacy and on the power of bishops.

**Standing.** Part of the [Book of Concord](#book-of-concord), the confessional standard Lutheran pastors subscribe, a *norma normata* under scripture, not a magisterium; **no Catholic level**. The Lutheran confessional text on the papacy is this one, not Calvin's *Institutes*. Triglot paragraph numbers follow its Latin column.

**What the lessons use.** *On bishops (1.2; 4.3, §§63-67):* with Jerome, bishop and pastor are one grade by divine right, so "ordination administered by a pastor in his own church is valid by divine law" (§65), and "wherever there is a true church, the right to elect and ordain ministers necessarily exists": the Lutheran confessional ground for denying that succession is constitutive ([churches and ecclesial communities](#churches-and-ecclesial-communities)). *On the Petrine texts (4.1, §§22-30):* Peter "is the representative of the entire assembly of apostles" (§23); what Matthew 16:19 says to him in the singular, Matthew 18:18 and John 20:23 say to all; the [keys](#power-of-the-keys) belong principally and immediately to the Church. *The historical section (4.2, §§12-21 Latin; the Triglot's English VI-XI)* argues from the ancient Church's practice. §12, on Nicaea and the origin of Rome's authority, is stated in 4.2 and read against Nicaea's canon 6 in Boss 4(a): outline only here. What it denies is divine right, not every primacy: Melanchthon signed the Smalcald Articles allowing the pope superiority over bishops "by human right" if he would allow the Gospel (4.2).

*Lessons:* [1.2](lessons/01-02-what-the-church-says-about-other-christians.md), [4.1](lessons/04-01-the-petrine-texts-argued.md), [4.2](lessons/04-02-what-the-patristic-record-proves.md), [4.3](lessons/04-03-apostolic-succession-and-the-validity-of-orders.md)

### Holy and Great Council 2016

The Orthodox council held in Crete in June 2016, attended by ten autocephalous churches.

**The text (1.2).** *Relations of the Orthodox Church with the Rest of the Christian World* speaks of the Orthodox Church as the One, Holy, Catholic and Apostolic Church (§1), and accepts the historical name of non-Orthodox Churches and Confessions not in communion with her while asking that the ecclesiological question be clarified (§6): wording that names them without settling what they are.

**Standing.** Antioch, Russia, Bulgaria and Georgia did not attend, and the Russian Church has declined (2016, 2017) to count the council pan-Orthodox or binding on all. Weighty evidence of Orthodox ecclesiology, not a binding definition, which Orthodoxy does not issue the Western way; **no Catholic level**. **Traps:** "the Orthodox Church's binding answer" (overstated; 1.2 P1(d)); "one theologian's opinion" (understated); treating the Orthodox as the softer partner, when §1 mirrors the Catholic claim.

*Lessons:* [1.2](lessons/01-02-what-the-church-says-about-other-christians.md)

### Basic Principles 2000

The Russian Orthodox Church's *Basic Principles of Attitude to the Non-Orthodox* (Bishops' Council, 2000): one autocephalous church's synodal text on other Christians.

**What it says (1.2).** The Orthodox Church is the true Church of Christ (§1.1); separated communities keep a certain incomplete fellowship (§1.15); three rites of reception, by baptism, chrismation or repentance, show that Orthodoxy treats different confessions differently (§1.17). **Standing:** a synodal text of one autocephalous church; **no Catholic level**. Orthodox practice in receiving converts is not uniform, and the lessons claim no more about it than §1.17 says.

*Lessons:* [1.2](lessons/01-02-what-the-church-says-about-other-christians.md)

### In necessariis unitas

The tag "in essentials unity, in non-essentials liberty, in all things charity", often credited to Augustine.

It descends from the Lutheran irenicist Peter Meiderlin, writing as Rupertus Meldenius (*Paraenesis votiva pro pace ecclesiae*, 1626). UR 4's Latin echoes it (*in necessariis unitatem ... in omnibus vero caritatem*), on unity in essentials, freedom in rite and theology, and charity in all things. John XXIII quoted it as a saying attributed to various authors (*Ad Petri Cathedram* 72, 1959). **No magisterial level** for the tag. **Trap:** quoting it as Augustine's.

*Lessons:* [1.2](lessons/01-02-what-the-church-says-about-other-christians.md)

### Argument from silence

Inferring that something was denied, or that it was held, because the surviving sources do not mention it.

**The two-sided rule (1.3 P2).** A doctrine unattested in 150 is not thereby denied in 150, and not thereby attested either. Silence counts only where a record was expected and would survive, and even then it shows absence from the record, not denial: the dog that did not bark is a clue only because a dog was there to bark. Silence run as *attestation* ("nobody objected, so all held it") is circular, since a protest is expected only if the practice was already widespread, which is what had to be shown (1.3 P1).

**Abused on every side (1.3).** *Catholic first:* popular apologetics argues that because no church ever claimed Mary's bones, the first Christians knew she was assumed; the checkable record cuts the other way, since in the 370s Epiphanius says no one knows her end ([`ecclesiology-and-mariology` 6.4](../ecclesiology-and-mariology/lessons/06-04-the-assumption.md)). *Protestant:* 1 Clement's silence about a bishop at Corinth read as proof there was none ([`patristics` 1.2](../patristics/lessons/01-02-first-clement-and-the-didache.md)). **Used properly:** the [1848 Encyclical](#encyclical-of-the-eastern-patriarchs-1848) §13 notes that Chalcedon's canon 28 grounds Rome's rank in the city and is silent about Peter, exactly where a record would be expected; [Jewel's challenge](#jewel-challenge) at full strength is an argument from expected record.

**Applied later.** The New Testament's silence about a successor to Peter neither denies nor attests one (4.1). Early silence about jurisdiction neither denies nor attests it, while Irenaeus rebuking Victor and Cyprian defying Stephen are positive evidence, not silence (4.2). Calvin's argument that Ephesians 4 would have named a single head under Christ if one existed weighs only as far as the author had to speak (4.3). Silence proves less than either side wants, and for the Assumption the record is not silence but recorded ignorance, from the first writer who asked and had occasion to know (6.6). Newman's own explanation of early silence (*Essay*, 1878, ch. 3 §2 and ch. 4 §3) is Boss 6(c)'s to reconstruct: see 6.6.

**Trap:** calling every argument from silence fallacious.

*Lessons:* [1.3](lessons/01-03-arguing-from-history-without-abusing-it.md), [4.1](lessons/04-01-the-petrine-texts-argued.md), [4.2](lessons/04-02-what-the-patristic-record-proves.md), [4.3](lessons/04-03-apostolic-succession-and-the-validity-of-orders.md), [6.6](lessons/06-06-development-or-corruption.md)

### Anachronism

Reading a word, a question or a text in a later century's sense.

**1.3, Catholic first.** Ignatius's "Catholic Church" (*Smyrnaeans* 8) read as the Roman communion ([`patristics` 1.3](../patristics/lessons/01-03-ignatius-of-antioch.md)); "papa" read as the later Roman office, when in 250 the Roman clergy wrote to Cyprian as "Papa" (ANF Ep. 29, Oxford 36) and Dionysius of Alexandria calls his predecessor Heraclas "our blessed father", *papa* in the Greek (Eusebius, *Church History* 7.7.4, McGiffert): any bishop's title, proving nothing about Rome. *Protestant:* reading a bare symbol into second-century Eucharistic realism, the twin of reading Trent into it. *Orthodox, and popular on every side:* dating "the" schism to 1054 ([`church-history` 4.2](../church-history/lessons/04-02-east-and-west-the-long-estrangement.md)).

**Counter-anachronism.** Coxe, correcting "pope", calls the early Roman bishops "no more 'Popes' than Cincinnatus was an emperor", makes Nicholas I (858-867) the first pope "in the modern sense", and dates supremacy as dogma to Pius IX, though Florence had defined Roman primacy over the whole world in 1439: his word check right, his history confessional. Primacy is **rung 1** by Florence and *Pastor Aeternus*, not by a word. Jewel's article asking for "only the accidents and shews" demands a word Trent avoids ([transubstantiation](#transubstantiation)).

**The rule (1.3 P3):** a text is evidence for its own sense. **Applied later.** Tertullian says Christ "made it His own body" and calls the bread "the figure of my body" in one sentence (*Against Marcion* IV.40): reading Zwingli's sign or Trent's conversion into it is anachronism (6.4). The second-century sacrificial texts attest an offering, and Cyprian an offering of the passion, not Trent's propitiatory doctrine in Trent's terms (6.5); Justin's commanded offering of thanksgiving does not show that it is [propitiatory](#propitiatory) (7.1).

*Lessons:* [1.3](lessons/01-03-arguing-from-history-without-abusing-it.md), [2.2](lessons/02-02-the-canon-question.md), [6.4](lessons/06-04-the-real-presence.md), [6.5](lessons/06-05-the-sacrifice-of-the-mass.md), [7.1](lessons/07-01-the-inquisition-and-the-galileo-affair.md)

### Cherry-picked Father

Quoting one sentence of one Father as his view, and so as the Church's, against the rest of his writing or the spread of the others.

**1.3, Catholic first.** "Peter has spoken through Leo" quoted without Chalcedon's examination of Leo's letter against Nicaea and Cyril, as the 1848 patriarchs pressed (§15); the fake tag "Roma locuta, causa finita" ([`patristics` 4.5](../patristics/lessons/04-05-augustine-against-the-pelagians.md)). *Protestant and Orthodox:* Gregory the Great's refusal of "universal bishop" quoted without his approving report that Pelagius II annulled John's synod "by the authority of the holy apostle Peter" (Register V.43; [`patristics` 5.3](../patristics/lessons/05-03-gregory-the-great.md)). Pelagius II made the annulment; Gregory reports it with approval.

**Jerome both ways (1.3 P2; Fremantle, NPNF2-6).** Letter 15 to Damasus (376 or 377): "I communicate with none but your blessedness, that is with the chair of Peter", answering whom to commune with while three men claimed Antioch. Letter 146 to Evangelus: "the world outweighs its capital", and every bishop's "dignity is one and his priesthood is one", answering a claim for deacons backed by a Roman custom. Held together: all bishops are equal in priesthood, and communion with Peter's chair was Jerome's test in a disputed succession.

**Basil both ways (2.3 P3).** Quoting Letter 189.3, "let God-inspired Scripture decide between us" (also transmitted among Gregory of Nyssa's works, so its author is disputed), while omitting *On the Holy Spirit* 27's "the same force" ([unwritten tradition](#unwritten-tradition)). **The rule (1.3 P4):** one Father is a witness, read whole.

*Lessons:* [1.3](lessons/01-03-arguing-from-history-without-abusing-it.md), [2.2](lessons/02-02-the-canon-question.md), [2.3](lessons/02-03-tradition-and-what-sufficiency-means.md)

### Manufactured consensus

Asserting "the Fathers unanimously taught X" without defining unanimous or counting the spread.

**1.3, Catholic first:** the claimed consensus that the rock of Matthew 16 is Peter, which [`patristics`](../patristics/syllabus.md) Boss 5 tests against the spread. *Protestant:* Coxe calls his account of the early papacy what "all candid critics and historians" prove (ANF 5, Elucidation III to Hippolytus). *Orthodox:* the 1848 Encyclical (§5) says the [filioque](#filioque) has no ground in the Fathers and calls the Latin witnesses adulterations (paraphrased). **The rule (1.3 P4):** a consensus must be defined, then shown across the spread: count before you claim.

**Applied later.** "Every Father acknowledged the pope as ruler of the whole Church" fails against Irenaeus rebuking Victor, Cyprian defying Stephen, Gregory refusing "universal", and the Catholic historian Schatz's concession that no Christian of 100 or 200 would have called Rome's bishop head of all Christians (4.2 P1). "Until Luther nobody doubted Tobit or Maccabees" fails against Jerome, Rufinus as Calvin reports him, and Cardinal Cajetan (2.2 P2).

*Lessons:* [1.3](lessons/01-03-arguing-from-history-without-abusing-it.md), [2.2](lessons/02-02-the-canon-question.md), [4.2](lessons/04-02-what-the-patristic-record-proves.md)

### Development of doctrine

The growth in the Church's understanding and explicit statement of the one deposit, in the same doctrine and the same sense, as against corruption.

**What each looks like in the record (1.3).** A development leaves scattered anticipations, grows under new questions, and makes older teaching firmer. A corruption has a birthday with no antecedents, leans on a forged or misread witness, and drops what came before. The [Vincentian canon](#vincentian-canon) and [Newman's notes](#newman-notes) are tests of what the record must contain, and a test that cannot fail is no test.

**The limit the Church binds herself to.** *Dei Filius* ch. 4 with can. IV.3: the sense of dogmas once declared is perpetually retained, and understanding grows "in one and the same doctrine, one and the same sense, one and the same judgment" (Schaff): **rung 1**. DV 8 (understanding grows): **at least rung 3**. *Mysterium Ecclesiae* (1973): **rung 3**. Newman's notes and Vincent's threefold canon: **no magisterial level**.

**Can it fail? (6.6).** Newman supplies falsifiers: a teaching that does but "contradict and reverse" what went before is "certainly corrupt" (*Essay*, 1878, ch. 5 §6), and if the Church herself, through pope or council, "has ever contradicted her own enunciations", his hypothesis "is at once shattered" (ch. 3 §2). 6.6's conclusion: development is not unfalsifiable in principle, but its falsifiers are narrow (contradiction and reversal in doctrine), and excess is judged in devotion and practice (LG 67; Trent XXI's reform decree abolishing the alms-questors despite customs "even though immemorial"). Its crux: whether any evidence could count as corruption in a defined doctrine, or every candidate is reread until it is not one. Showing that "development" is not an alibi is part of the Catholic [burden](#burden-of-the-catholic-claim).

**Where other lessons hand it the question.** The patristic record carries the primacy "to the door of development, and no further" (4.2). "You invented a doctrine in 1950" mistakes the definition for the belief, which the Dormition liturgy carried for centuries (6.1). Whether *Dignitatis Humanae* develops or reverses the 1520 censure on burning heretics: **rung 5** (7.1). **Trap (1.3):** a weak argument for a defined doctrine does not weaken the doctrine, and a defined doctrine does not promote a historical argument for it.

*Lessons:* [1.3](lessons/01-03-arguing-from-history-without-abusing-it.md), [4.2](lessons/04-02-what-the-patristic-record-proves.md), [6.1](lessons/06-01-mary.md), [6.6](lessons/06-06-development-or-corruption.md), [7.1](lessons/07-01-the-inquisition-and-the-galileo-affair.md)

### Vincentian canon

Vincent of Lerins, *Commonitorium* ch. 2 (c. 434): hold what has been believed everywhere, always and by all (universality, antiquity, consent).

**A test, not a conclusion (1.3).** Run as a historical audit it failed the Immaculate Conception ([`fundamental-theology` 5.1](../fundamental-theology/lessons/05-01-vincent-of-lerins.md)). **Levels:** the threefold canon, **no magisterial level**; the limit on growth that Vincent supplied, no new sense, binds only through *Dei Filius* ch. 4 and its canon, **rung 1** (1.3, 6.6).

**Newman's critique (6.6; *Essay*, 1878, Introduction).** The Anglican divines' hypothesis keeps what has primitive sanction and cuts the rest away, with Vincent's "always, everywhere, by all" as the knife; Newman's objection is that the knife "strikes at Rome through England": narrowed to exclude the Creed of Pius IV, it excludes the Athanasian Creed too. **The other use (2.3 P4):** Vincent calls the canon of scripture "complete, and sufficient of itself for everything", yet, read in different senses, needing "the authority of the Church's interpretation" (Heurtley): materially sufficient, formally not ([material and formal sufficiency](#material-and-formal-sufficiency)).

*Lessons:* [1.3](lessons/01-03-arguing-from-history-without-abusing-it.md), [6.6](lessons/06-06-development-or-corruption.md)

### Newman notes

Newman's seven notes for telling a genuine development from a corruption (*Essay on the Development of Christian Doctrine*, 1878 edition, ch. 5): preservation of type, continuity of principles, power of assimilation, logical sequence, anticipation of its future, conservative action upon its past, chronic vigour.

**Level: no magisterial level.** Newman calls them notes "of varying cogency": marks to be weighed ([`fundamental-theology` 5.2](../fundamental-theology/lessons/05-02-newmans-theory-of-development.md)), which 1.3 treats as tests of what the record must contain. Cite the 1878 edition's chapters and sections (the online public-domain text).

**Run honestly on the Assumption, the module's thinnest-attested distinctive (6.6 Ex. 2).** Type satisfied (the New Eve; she is taken up, not rising by her own power); continuity of principles satisfied, on a principle Article VI disputes (the feast came before the arguments); assimilation contested (the *Transitus* narratives, one listed among apocrypha by the Gelasian Decree); logical sequence weak (fittingness, not entailment); anticipation weakest (recorded ignorance, not silence); conservative action and chronic vigour strong. Where it stops: the notes clear the Assumption of contradiction and reversal but cannot turn Epiphanius's ignorance into apostolic memory; the bull rests on the Church's judgment. Running the seven notes again is not Boss 6(c)'s task; its passage is Newman's argument from silence ([argument from silence](#argument-from-silence)).

*Lessons:* [1.3](lessons/01-03-arguing-from-history-without-abusing-it.md), [6.6](lessons/06-06-development-or-corruption.md)

### The Catholic Controversy
