| 1 | Blame for the separations lies with people on both sides (UR 3, *quandoque*, "at times"), in no equal shares; the Christian you argue with bears none of it; the CDF's 2007 commentary admits its language caused "no little distress". | [1.2](lessons/01-02-what-the-church-says-about-other-christians.md) |
| 1 | Catholic polemic has its own record of forgery and atrocity story: the [Pseudo-Isidorean decretals](#pseudo-isidorian-decretals) and the [Donation of Constantine](#donation-of-constantine) served Roman claims, and Cochlaeus's hostile life of Luther (1549) governed Catholic history-writing for centuries (Herte, 1943). | [1.3](lessons/01-03-arguing-from-history-without-abusing-it.md) |
| 2 | *Sola scriptura* as the confessions state it is not individualism; attacking "me and my Bible" attacks a position the Formula, Westminster and the Second Helvetic themselves reject. | [2.1](lessons/02-01-sola-scriptura-at-its-best.md) |
| 2 | The Reformers did not invent their Old Testament: the Reformed and Anglican list is the Hebrew canon Jerome defended; Jerome and Cajetan doubted the deuterocanon; the Catholic canon was not defined under anathema before 1546, and Florence listed it without one. | [2.2](lessons/02-02-the-canon-question.md) |
| 2 | Material sufficiency is a legitimate and widely held Catholic position (Congar; Geiselmann; Newman to Pusey); the tradition texts one at a time prove little; the confessions subordinate tradition, they do not reject it. | [2.3](lessons/02-03-tradition-and-what-sufficiency-means.md) |
| 2 | Westminster's clarity thesis is modest and carefully qualified; Catholic unity is achieved by authority, not clarity, so Protestant division alone is not a like-for-like comparison; Marburg agreed on far more than it divided over. | [2.4](lessons/02-04-perspicuity-tested.md) |
| 3 | The Reformation doctrine protects what late-medieval preaching and practice genuinely obscured: that grace is unowed and comes first, and that a terrified conscience may rest on Christ's promise; Augsburg XX described real preaching. | [3.1](lessons/03-01-justification-as-the-confessions-state-it.md) |
| 3 | Some canons and articles pass each other because the words differ; reading every canon as a hit on Luther is careless (cans. 19 and 21 condemn antinomian propositions the Lutherans also reject; can. 23's "never truly justified" is condemned in the Smalcald Articles too). | [3.2](lessons/03-02-trent-session-vi-against-the-confessions.md) |
| 3 | The vindicative sense of *dikaioo* is real, and Calvin's reading of James has genuine support in 2:18 and the order of Genesis; making James simply "refute" Paul flattens both writers. | [3.3](lessons/03-03-paul-and-james.md) |
| 3 | The Annex Rome signed concedes more than apologists admit: justification "by faith alone" with cooperation in the Spirit's power (2C), *simul iustus et peccator* in a stated sense (2A), justification the "measure or touchstone" (3), a reward "on which we have no claim" (2E); not a liberal slip (Ratzinger's part, as *Lutheran World Information* reported). | [3.4](lessons/03-04-the-joint-declaration-merit-and-reward.md) |
| 4 | Matthew 16 alone does not reach an office with successors: the exegesis gets you Peter, not the papacy; binding and loosing were given to all the apostles, and the keys are exercised first in preaching and discipline. | [4.1](lessons/04-01-the-petrine-texts-argued.md) |
| 4 | The *Treatise*'s facts are facts: no Eastern church sought ordination or confirmation of its bishops from Rome; councils met without Rome presiding; Gregory refused the universal title; Chalcedon built a hierarchy of sees on the empire, and the East kept it. | [4.2](lessons/04-02-what-the-patristic-record-proves.md) |
| 4 | The archbishops' argument from the ancient forms is serious: the earliest Roman ordination prayers they cite do not name the power to offer sacrifice, Rome holds them valid, so the Ordinal's silence about sacrifice cannot, taken alone, be its defect. | [4.3](lessons/04-03-apostolic-succession-and-the-validity-of-orders.md) |
| 4 | The confessions never taught a merely invisible Church (Westminster: "no ordinary possibility of salvation" outside the visible one; Calvin: the Church as mother); the West before 1417 had its own visible-unity failure, thirty-nine years without agreement on who the pope was. | [4.4](lessons/04-04-the-visible-church-and-what-replaced-the-papacy.md) |
| 5 | The manner of the addition is genuinely hard to defend (Spain, Frankish pressure, Rome's two-century refusal and its legates in 879-880, the 1014 liturgy, no council of East and West, councils the East did not receive); the 1995 clarification concedes the monarchy of the Father. | [5.1](lessons/05-01-the-filioque-two-questions-kept-apart.md) |
| 5 | Universal ordinary jurisdiction has cost the local church something real (UR 16: "not always indeed carried out in practice"; UUS 95); the Orthodox fear of absorption describes a history, not a phantom. | [5.2](lessons/05-02-papal-primacy-as-the-east-reads-it.md) |
| 5 | The Orthodox objection is usually about jurisdiction, not infallibility, and answering only infallibility has not heard it; jurisdiction's cost is real; no earthly tribunal enforces the 1875 protection for a single bishop. | [5.3](lessons/05-03-vatican-i-read-by-orthodox-theologians.md) |
| 5 | No magisterial Catholic act condemns Palamas or the distinction; Catholic assessments range from Fortescue and Jugie to Kuhlmann and Nichols; several Byzantine-rite Catholic churches keep his commemoration; both sides hold deification real. | [5.4](lessons/05-04-essence-and-energies.md) |
| 5 | The patristic and Byzantine record on remarriage is genuinely mixed (Origen's bishops, Basil's uneven custom, Jerome's plea for Fabiola, Justinian's Novel 117); the Catholic definitions of purgatory never mention fire, and the Latins at Ferrara left it out of the decree. | [5.5](lessons/05-05-marriage-divorce-and-the-state-of-the-dead.md) |
| 6 | Marian devotion has repeatedly gone past what the doctrine licenses (Newman to Pusey §5; "safer to seek Mary than her Son", the Apology's complaint); to the Orthodox, the debate behind 1854 was fought inside Augustine's doctrine of original sin, without the East. | [6.1](lessons/06-01-mary.md) |
| 6 | Relic traffic, mechanical devotion and images treated as if power lived in them were real and profitable; Trent's own decree legislates against them (DS 1825), and Vatican II still asks that abuses be corrected (LG 51). | [6.2](lessons/06-02-the-saints-the-images-the-relics.md) |
| 6 | The sale of indulgences was real and the abuse systemic: Trent calls the "evil gains" "a most prolific cause of abuses" and abolishes them (collectors abolished 1562; Pius V's 1567 revocation, as reported); the defence depends on the [disciplinary and doctrinal](#disciplinary-and-doctrinal) distinction holding. | [6.3](lessons/06-03-penance-purgatory-and-indulgences.md) |
| 6 | The definition is not committed to Aristotle, and apologists who make it sound Aristotelian concede ground the Church has not; the Reformed confess a real feeding on Christ's own body, so the quarrel is over where and how it is given, not whether. | [6.4](lessons/06-04-the-real-presence.md) |
| 6 | Masses were multiplied and sold (Augsburg XXIV), and the Reformers attacked the trade before Trent reformed it; "propitiatory" needs explaining, not asserting; "daily immolated", unexplained, says what Hebrews 10:11 denies. | [6.5](lessons/06-05-the-sacrifice-of-the-mass.md) |
| 6 | Mozley's objection has force: a theory under which every later doctrine passes the notes would be unfalsifiable, the notes have sometimes been used that way, and Newman's answer to excess is a denial, not a test. | [6.6](lessons/06-06-development-or-corruption.md) |
| 7 | The Galileo condemnation was an error really made by the Church's own tribunal with papal involvement; the inquisitions killed people for heresy with the Church's doctrinal approval; *Tertio Millennio Adveniente* 35's "acquiescence" is weaker than the record warrants; the distinction touches none of this. | [7.1](lessons/07-01-the-inquisition-and-the-galileo-affair.md) |
| 7 | Most disciplinary grievances were justified ("especially this Roman curia"); Trent legislated against nearly every item; the abuse reached papal acts (the 1517 campaign ran on a papal grant); reform came late and under pressure. The honest claim is only that a corrupt Church can still be the Church. | [7.2](lessons/07-02-the-reformations-grievances.md) |
| 7 | On the abuse crisis the Catholic case does not win: for decades bishops failed the standard of discipline Article XXVI and the Belgic Confession state, outsiders brought the facts out, and the sign Vatican I offered is visibly damaged. The argument from scandal is survived, not answered. | [7.3](lessons/07-03-the-abuse-crisis-and-the-argument-from-scandal.md) |

## Thinkers and texts

Every author, council and confession the lessons name, grouped by tradition so the other side is never further away than your own. Dates and theses are as the lessons give them; a work by a theologian, of any tradition, has no magisterial level and is not its tradition's confession. Each Catholic text's level is under [Levels of authority used in this course](#levels-of-authority-used-in-this-course), and each other tradition's text's standing under [Weight inside other traditions](#weight-inside-other-traditions).

| Who / what | When | Tradition | Key work or act | One-line thesis, as the lessons give it | Lessons |

| **Fathers, popes and councils of the first millennium** | | | | | |
| *Didache* | early church order | Patristic | ch. 14 | calls the Lord's-day breaking of bread a sacrifice, citing Malachi 1:11 | [6.5](lessons/06-05-the-sacrifice-of-the-mass.md) |
| Ignatius of Antioch | early 2nd c. | Patristic | *Smyrnaeans* 7; *Romans* (Rome "presides in love") | the Eucharist is "the flesh of our Saviour" | [1.3](lessons/01-03-arguing-from-history-without-abusing-it.md), [4.2](lessons/04-02-what-the-patristic-record-proves.md), [5.2](lessons/05-02-papal-primacy-as-the-east-reads-it.md), [6.4](lessons/06-04-the-real-presence.md) |
| *Martyrdom of Polycarp* | 2nd c. | Patristic | 17-18 | Smyrna gathered the martyr's bones as a treasure; Christ adored, the martyrs loved | [6.2](lessons/06-02-the-saints-the-images-the-relics.md) |
| Justin Martyr | d. c. 165 | Patristic | *1 Apology* 66; *Dialogue with Trypho* 41, 117 | the Eucharist received "not as common bread"; Malachi's pure offering is the Eucharist; prayers and thanksgivings the only perfect sacrifices | [6.4](lessons/06-04-the-real-presence.md), [6.5](lessons/06-05-the-sacrifice-of-the-mass.md), [7.1](lessons/07-01-the-inquisition-and-the-galileo-affair.md) |

## Assumed, not taught here

This course argues; it re-teaches no doctrine and retells no event. Every fact it uses without deriving has an owner, from the syllabus Scope table and each lesson's *Builds on* and Connections.

| Fact | Where it's taught |
|---|---|
| **Scope: what this course never argues** | |
| Everything that divides Christians from non-Christians: God, the resurrection as history, Judaism, Islam, pluralism, salvation outside the visible Church argued outward; what apologetics is and its rules of persuasion | [`apologetics-foundations`](../apologetics-foundations/syllabus.md) [1.1](../apologetics-foundations/lessons/01-01-what-apologetics-is-and-what-it-isnt.md), [8.2](../apologetics-foundations/lessons/08-02-salvation-outside-the-visible-church.md), [8.3](../apologetics-foundations/lessons/08-03-apologetics-in-a-secular-age.md) |
| Van Til's presuppositional method; the conflict thesis run on Galileo; *shirk* as the Muslim form of the worship question | `apologetics-foundations` [1.2](../apologetics-foundations/lessons/01-02-three-strategies.md), [4.1](../apologetics-foundations/lessons/04-01-the-conflict-thesis-tested-in-both-directions.md), [7.2](../apologetics-foundations/lessons/07-02-tawhid-and-the-trinity.md) |
| Moral disagreements among Christians (sexual ethics, conscience) and social teaching used apologetically: declined | [`moral-theology`](../moral-theology/syllabus.md); [`catholic-social-teaching`](../catholic-social-teaching/syllabus.md) |
| The churches that did not receive Chalcedon (miaphysite and Assyrian) | `christology` [6.3](../christology/lessons/06-03-the-churches-that-did-not-receive-chalcedon.md) |
| **Authority, revelation and development (fundamental-theology)** | |
| The ladder of doctrinal authority: rungs 1-5, the seam, can. 749 §3, "default down" | `fundamental-theology` [4.4](../fundamental-theology/lessons/04-04-the-ladder-of-doctrinal-authority.md) |

## Pitfalls

Every lesson's Watch out, with the writers' recorded traps, deduped and stated as the wrong belief and its correction.

### Traps with levels and weights

- **Overstating another tradition's text is the same error as overstating a Catholic one.** The Book of Concord is a *norma normata*, not a magisterium; Westminster a subordinate standard, neither infallible nor a museum piece; Crete (four churches absent), the 1848 and 1895 encyclicals and the Moscow texts are not "the Orthodox position"; Jewel's sermon is not the Church of England's; the seventeenth-century Orthodox confessions (Mogila) are of limited authority, not a magisterium. *([1.1](lessons/01-01-shared-ground-and-where-the-argument-is.md), [1.2](lessons/01-02-what-the-church-says-about-other-christians.md), [1.3](lessons/01-03-arguing-from-history-without-abusing-it.md), [2.1](lessons/02-01-sola-scriptura-at-its-best.md), [5.2](lessons/05-02-papal-primacy-as-the-east-reads-it.md), [5.5](lessons/05-05-marriage-divorce-and-the-state-of-the-dead.md), [6.1](lessons/06-01-mary.md))*
- **A theologian's book is not its tradition's confession**: Calvin's *Institutes* and *Antidote*, Chemnitz's *Examen*, Luther's prefaces and *Bondage of the Will*, Cullmann, Mozley, Tract 90, Newman's *Lectures on Justification*. *([2.2](lessons/02-02-the-canon-question.md), [2.3](lessons/02-03-tradition-and-what-sufficiency-means.md), [2.4](lessons/02-04-perspicuity-tested.md), [3.1](lessons/03-01-justification-as-the-confessions-state-it.md), [3.2](lessons/03-02-trent-session-vi-against-the-confessions.md), [3.3](lessons/03-03-paul-and-james.md), [3.4](lessons/03-04-the-joint-declaration-merit-and-reward.md), [4.1](lessons/04-01-the-petrine-texts-argued.md), [6.5](lessons/06-05-the-sacrifice-of-the-mass.md), [6.6](lessons/06-06-development-or-corruption.md), [7.3](lessons/07-03-the-abuse-crisis-and-the-argument-from-scandal.md))*
- **"Trent defined it" when a chapter only expounds.** Chapters are at least rung 3; canons are rung 1 as worded; the decree's fence binds Catholics to the chapters without making every sentence a definition. *([3.1](lessons/03-01-justification-as-the-confessions-state-it.md), [3.2](lessons/03-02-trent-session-vi-against-the-confessions.md), [3.3](lessons/03-03-paul-and-james.md), [6.3](lessons/06-03-penance-purgatory-and-indulgences.md), [6.4](lessons/06-04-the-real-presence.md), [6.5](lessons/06-05-the-sacrifice-of-the-mass.md))*
- **Applying a condemnation to a named text is rung 5.** No act applies Trent's anathemas to Westminster, Article VI or the Formula, Constance's censure to a confession, or *Firmiter* and Florence to Palamas (can. 749 §3); Trent never condemned "sola scriptura" by name. *([2.1](lessons/02-01-sola-scriptura-at-its-best.md), [2.3](lessons/02-03-tradition-and-what-sufficiency-means.md), [2.4](lessons/02-04-perspicuity-tested.md), [3.2](lessons/03-02-trent-session-vi-against-the-confessions.md), [4.4](lessons/04-04-the-visible-church-and-what-replaced-the-papacy.md), [5.4](lessons/05-04-essence-and-energies.md))*
- **Understating is the mirror error**: a rung-1 canon called opinion, visibility called "Bellarmine's opinion", temporal punishment "a scholastic theory", the Assumption "a pious opinion", ch. 3 of *Pastor Aeternus* "a courtesy title", UR 4's "truth and fairness" mere etiquette. *([1.2](lessons/01-02-what-the-church-says-about-other-christians.md), [3.3](lessons/03-03-paul-and-james.md), [4.4](lessons/04-04-the-visible-church-and-what-replaced-the-papacy.md), [5.3](lessons/05-03-vatican-i-read-by-orthodox-theologians.md), [6.3](lessons/06-03-penance-purgatory-and-indulgences.md), [6.6](lessons/06-06-development-or-corruption.md))*
- **The Catechism locates; it fixes no level.** CCC 1271 does not make the baptism teaching rung 1; Trent VII can. 4 does, and UR 22's "bond" stays rung 3. *([1.1](lessons/01-01-shared-ground-and-where-the-argument-is.md), [6.3](lessons/06-03-penance-purgatory-and-indulgences.md))*
- **Dialogue texts settle less than claimed and carry no magisterial level.** The Annex did not repeal can. 32; Ravenna settled nothing (§45); ARCIC did not reopen *Apostolicae Curae*; the 2007 *Responses* defined nothing. *([1.2](lessons/01-02-what-the-church-says-about-other-christians.md), [3.4](lessons/03-04-the-joint-declaration-merit-and-reward.md), [4.3](lessons/04-03-apostolic-succession-and-the-validity-of-orders.md), [5.2](lessons/05-02-papal-primacy-as-the-east-reads-it.md))*
- **A weak argument for a defined doctrine does not weaken the doctrine,** and a defined doctrine does not promote the historical argument offered for it: the cap on an argument is not a claim's level. *([1.3](lessons/01-03-arguing-from-history-without-abusing-it.md), [6.6](lessons/06-06-development-or-corruption.md))*
- **For rung-2 teaching the word "infallible" is not the error;** "dogma", "heresy" and "defined" are (rejecting it falls under can. 750 §2, not can. 751). *([4.3](lessons/04-03-apostolic-succession-and-the-validity-of-orders.md))*
- **School words have no rung**: *dulia*, *hyperdulia*, "relative *latria*", *condign* and *congruous* (rung 4), grace as a "quality" (rung 4). *([3.4](lessons/03-04-the-joint-declaration-merit-and-reward.md), [5.4](lessons/05-04-essence-and-energies.md), [6.1](lessons/06-01-mary.md), [6.2](lessons/06-02-the-saints-the-images-the-relics.md))*
- **Law has no rung, and still binds**: reform decrees, the 1607 *de auxiliis* formula, withholding the cup, *Vos estis*. "Discipline" means changeable, not minor or blameless. *([2.4](lessons/02-04-perspicuity-tested.md), [7.2](lessons/07-02-the-reformations-grievances.md), [7.3](lessons/07-03-the-abuse-crisis-and-the-argument-from-scandal.md))*

### Traps of fidelity (caricature and blur)

- **"Protestants" is not a position.** Lutheran, Reformed, Anglican and Baptist confessions divide on baptism, falling away, the Supper, the Church and the saints; name the confession. *([1.1](lessons/01-01-shared-ground-and-where-the-argument-is.md), [2.4](lessons/02-04-perspicuity-tested.md), [3.1](lessons/03-01-justification-as-the-confessions-state-it.md), [4.4](lessons/04-04-the-visible-church-and-what-replaced-the-papacy.md), [6.2](lessons/06-02-the-saints-the-images-the-relics.md))*
- ***Sola scriptura* is not "me and my Bible".** That is *solo scriptura*, which the Second Helvetic (II), Westminster (I.10) and the Formula reject; arguing against it refutes no confession. *([1.1](lessons/01-01-shared-ground-and-where-the-argument-is.md), [2.1](lessons/02-01-sola-scriptura-at-its-best.md), [2.3](lessons/02-03-tradition-and-what-sufficiency-means.md), [2.4](lessons/02-04-perspicuity-tested.md))*
- **"Protestants reject tradition" fails.** They reject unwritten traditions as a second rule and keep creeds, Fathers and councils as witnesses; Chemnitz accepts seven kinds; Jewel staked his case on six centuries. "Luther invented sola scriptura" fails too: Tradition I is medieval. *([1.3](lessons/01-03-arguing-from-history-without-abusing-it.md), [2.1](lessons/02-01-sola-scriptura-at-its-best.md), [2.3](lessons/02-03-tradition-and-what-sufficiency-means.md))*
- **Answering an argument no confession makes.** "A Church that errs is no Church" caricatures the Reformed and Orthodox case, which keeps a guarantee and relocates it (what Westminster XXV.5 affirms is Boss 7(a)'s to state); "wicked priests, so no church" (Augsburg VIII, Article XXVI, Westminster XXVII.3 and Calvin IV.1.13 reject the Donatist inference); "bad popes don't falsify doctrine" answers no one. *([7.1](lessons/07-01-the-inquisition-and-the-galileo-affair.md), [7.2](lessons/07-02-the-reformations-grievances.md), [7.3](lessons/07-03-the-abuse-crisis-and-the-argument-from-scandal.md))*
- **The Lutheran objection to "ecclesial community" is an ecclesiology, not hurt feelings** (Augsburg VII's *satis est*; the *Treatise*); answer the criterion. The Orthodox are not the softer partner: Crete §1 and the Moscow text identify their Church with the one Church. *([1.2](lessons/01-02-what-the-church-says-about-other-christians.md))*
- **Not every Reformation confession has a creed article**: Westminster has none, and the American revision of Article VIII (1801) drops the Athanasian Creed. Baptism is not wholly shared ground: its subjects are disputed with Baptists. *([1.1](lessons/01-01-shared-ground-and-where-the-argument-is.md))*
- **"Trent settled it" is an external critique,** and shared ground (God, the resurrection, Scripture's reliability) is not argued with fellow Christians. "The Catholic carries the larger burden" does not mean the other side carries none. *([1.1](lessons/01-01-shared-ground-and-where-the-argument-is.md), [2.1](lessons/02-01-sola-scriptura-at-its-best.md))*
- **Quotation traps.** "Self-authenticating" is not Calvin's word (*autopistos*; Beveridge: "carrying its own evidence along with it"), and Calvin gives the Church "the stamp of her authority" (I.7.2). "Faith alone justifies, but the faith that justifies is never alone" is worded in Calvin's *Antidote*; the Formula (SD III.41) credits the idea to Luther. The *simul* is Luther's, in no Book of Concord text; "alien righteousness" is Luther's and the Apology's, not Augsburg IV's. "Differentiated consensus" is not the 1999 texts' phrase ("consensus in basic truths"). *([2.1](lessons/02-01-sola-scriptura-at-its-best.md), [2.2](lessons/02-02-the-canon-question.md), [3.1](lessons/03-01-justification-as-the-confessions-state-it.md), [3.2](lessons/03-02-trent-session-vi-against-the-confessions.md), [3.4](lessons/03-04-the-joint-declaration-merit-and-reward.md))*
- **Quote the translation you read**: Percival's Nicaea II has the honour "passes on to that which the image represents" ("passes to the prototype" is Basil's phrase via John of Damascus); name which 1896 English of *Apostolicae Curae* you quote; Beveridge misprints Calvin's Maccabees reference in III.5.8 (it is 2 Macc 12:43). *([4.3](lessons/04-03-apostolic-succession-and-the-validity-of-orders.md), [6.2](lessons/06-02-the-saints-the-images-the-relics.md), [6.3](lessons/06-03-penance-purgatory-and-indulgences.md))*

### Traps on scripture, canon and tradition

- **"Protestants made up their own Old Testament."** The Reformed and Anglican list is the Hebrew canon Jerome defended; the traditions differ over the rest (Westminster I.3: no authority; Article VI: read for example of life; Luther: printed apart). "Nobody doubted the deuterocanon before Luther" fails too: Jerome, Rufinus as Calvin reports him, Cajetan. *([2.2](lessons/02-02-the-canon-question.md))*
- **"Luther cut four books from his Bible."** He printed Hebrews, James, Jude and Revelation last, with prefaces that rank them; a prefatory judgment, not an excision, and the Lutheran confessions never adopted his rankings. *([2.2](lessons/02-02-the-canon-question.md))*
- **"Florence defined the canon in 1442"** (no anathema, not even "canonical": at least rung 3), **or "deuterocanonical" means second-class** (Trent's one list is rung 1 for all seventy-three books). *([2.2](lessons/02-02-the-canon-question.md))*
- **"The Church gave the Bible its authority"** is a Catholic error: *Dei Filius* ch. 2 denies that approval makes the books sacred, as Calvin I.7.2 and Westminster I.4 do; the dispute is the edge of the list and the certainty of the recognition. *([2.1](lessons/02-01-sola-scriptura-at-its-best.md), [2.2](lessons/02-02-the-canon-question.md))*
- **Article VI uses two tests**: "never doubted" for the Old Testament, "commonly received" for the New; reading the switch as Anglican acceptance of the deuterocanon fails. *([2.2](lessons/02-02-the-canon-question.md))*
- **Trent's "equal affection of piety, and reverence" is equal reverence, not extent,** and Trent wrote "and" where its draft had "partly ... partly": it did not dogmatize Tradition II (extent is rung 5). The mirror overstatement: material sufficiency is an option, not *the* Catholic view. *([2.1](lessons/02-01-sola-scriptura-at-its-best.md), [2.3](lessons/02-03-tradition-and-what-sufficiency-means.md))*
- **Irenaeus and Tertullian do not refute the Reformers directly**: their opponents rejected the rule of faith; what transfers is the rule as a norm of reading, and Tertullian's prescription bars outsiders, not creed-confessing Christians. *([2.3](lessons/02-03-tradition-and-what-sufficiency-means.md))*
- **Over-reading the tradition texts either way**: 2 Thess 2:15's traditions may all have been written later; 2 Tim 2:2 promises no immunity from error and says "heard"; 2 Tim 3:16-17's "profitable" is not "alone", and 3:15's scriptures are the Old Testament; no reading may rest on Pauline authorship. *([2.3](lessons/02-03-tradition-and-what-sufficiency-means.md))*
- **DV 10's "exclusively" governs authentic interpretation,** not who may read Scripture; the office "is not above the word of God, but serves it". *([2.3](lessons/02-03-tradition-and-what-sufficiency-means.md))*
- **The clarity thesis does not say every verse is plain**: Westminster I.7 denies it and cites 2 Pet 3:16 for the denial; "ordinary means" includes preaching, sacraments and prayer; Luther's thesis (all the things of Scripture, in the public ministry) is not Westminster's (what is necessary). Acts 8:31 and 2 Pet 3:16 prove no infallible interpreter. *([2.4](lessons/02-04-perspicuity-tested.md))*
- **The Church does not teach that Scripture is obscure** (DV 25 urges reading it), and its rule of interpretation is at least rung 3 with penalties, not an anathema. *([2.4](lessons/02-04-perspicuity-tested.md))*
- **Marburg did not "agree on nothing"**: fourteen articles and most of the fifteenth were agreed; the split was whether the body and blood are corporally present. *([2.4](lessons/02-04-perspicuity-tested.md))*
- **The inward testimony is not the fideism *Dei Filius* condemns**: canons on faith 3 targets denying outward signs; Westminster I.5 and Calvin cite evidences. *([2.1](lessons/02-01-sola-scriptura-at-its-best.md))*
- **2.2's crux is not fundamental-theology 3.3's**: that asked whether "recognize" describes Trent's act; this asks how certain the recognition can be. *([2.2](lessons/02-02-the-canon-question.md))*

### Traps on justification

- **"The Reformation made justification a legal fiction."** The confessions require renewal (Calvin III.16.1; Westminster XIII.1; Epitome IV aff. 3); they deny only that it belongs to the ground of acceptance. *([3.1](lessons/03-01-justification-as-the-confessions-state-it.md))*
- **"The Reformation denied that the justified can fall."** Only the Reformed did (Westminster XVII.1, XI.5); Augsburg XII and Smalcald III.3 side with Trent can. 23. "Once saved, always saved" is false of the Book of Concord, and "live as you like" caricatures even Westminster (XVII.3; XVIII.3). Smalcald III.3's target was 1520s sectarists, not Westminster. *([3.1](lessons/03-01-justification-as-the-confessions-state-it.md), [3.2](lessons/03-02-trent-session-vi-against-the-confessions.md))*
- **Augsburg says God imputes faith; Westminster denies it in words** (mostly a difference of formulation). The Apology's "made righteous, or regenerated" is not Lutheran infusion; the Formula glosses it as absolution. *([3.1](lessons/03-01-justification-as-the-confessions-state-it.md))*
- **"Trent condemned faith alone, or imputation, outright."** Can. 9 condemns "faith alone" in one stated sense, can. 11 *sole* imputation, and ch. 8 says we are "justified by faith". *([3.1](lessons/03-01-justification-as-the-confessions-state-it.md))*
- **"Trent defined that no one can be sure he is in grace."** Ch. 9 is at least rung 3; cans. 13-14 condemn only requiring certainty and miss Westminster XVIII.3; only can. 16 might reach, and that application is rung 5. *([3.2](lessons/03-02-trent-session-vi-against-the-confessions.md))*
- **"Every canon was aimed at Luther."** Cans. 19 and 21 condemn antinomian propositions the Lutherans also reject; can. 23's "never truly justified" is condemned in the Smalcald Articles too. But can. 5 does repeat Luther's condemned words (*Exsurge* prop. 36). The 1986 study asked whether the canons still strike today's partner, not whether they missed Luther. *([3.2](lessons/03-02-trent-session-vi-against-the-confessions.md))*
- **The merit clash is narrow**: the Apology grants rewards here and hereafter (§73) and the Formula "a glorious reward" (SD IV.38); they deny meriting grace and eternal life. Crediting Westminster's disproportion argument to the Apology is the blur. *([3.2](lessons/03-02-trent-session-vi-against-the-confessions.md), [3.4](lessons/03-04-the-joint-declaration-merit-and-reward.md))*
- **"The Annex repealed can. 32" or "Trent defined condign merit."** Can. 32 is rung 1 and unrevoked; "condign" is rung 4; the Annex has no rung. Annex 2E's "no claim" is neither an adoption of Calvin nor a choice of Scotus over Thomas; whether it fits can. 32 is rung 5. *([3.4](lessons/03-04-the-joint-declaration-merit-and-reward.md))*
- **"The Lutheran confessions reject James."** The Apology answers 2:24 and calls James "right", and the Formula adopts it; the 1522 preface is no confession. *([3.3](lessons/03-03-paul-and-james.md))*
- **Lutheran and Reformed readings of James differ**: the Apology's "pronounced righteous in a forensic sense" (Romans 2:13 read of believers) against Calvin's "before men" (Romans 2:13 read hypothetically). *([3.3](lessons/03-03-paul-and-james.md))*
- **"A Catholic who reads James as Calvin does has given up Trent."** Ch. 10's citation is not a definition (the exegesis is rung 5); the fence is can. 24 (rung 1). Promoting the citation and demoting can. 24 are the twin errors. *([3.3](lessons/03-03-paul-and-james.md))*
- **"Justified before men" has a basis in the Greek** (Luke 7:29; Romans 3:4); and "James refutes Paul" is nobody's confessional position. The crux is before God or before men, not whether James means Romans 4:5's act. *([3.3](lessons/03-03-paul-and-james.md))*
- **"The New Perspective proves Paul agrees with Trent"**: a rung-5 hypothesis that moves the referent of works, not the imputation-inherence crux. *([3.3](lessons/03-03-paul-and-james.md))*

### Traps on the Church and orders

- **Reading *Unitatis Redintegratio* as forbidding argument** for the papacy with other Christians, or the 2007 *Doctrinal Note* as forbidding attempts to convince: it regulates means and motives and forbids no outcome. UR 3's "both sides" assigns no equal shares (*quandoque*, "at times"); no text ranks individual reception against corporate unity. *([1.2](lessons/01-02-what-the-church-says-about-other-christians.md))*
- **The rock-is-Christ, *Petros*-a-pebble reading is not "the Protestant reading"**: the *Treatise* (§25) reads the rock as the ministry of Peter's confession, the Reformed argue from the keys and Christ's headship, and Cullmann, a Lutheran, read the rock as Peter, which conceded no succession. *([4.1](lessons/04-01-the-petrine-texts-argued.md))*
- **"Vatican I defined that Matthew 16 and John 21 prove the primacy."** The canons define the primacy and the succession (rung 1); which verse carries what is chapter teaching; the rock's referent is rung 5. Nor is succession open because the exegesis stops at Peter: the ch. 2 canon is rung 1. *([4.1](lessons/04-01-the-petrine-texts-argued.md))*
- **"The gates of hell" is not "no pope can err"**: the promise is indefectibility; infallibility is defined under four conditions. Matthew 18:18 is not "only about quarrels" (LG 22); the Reformed keys are not "no church authority" (Westminster XXX commits them to officers, not to the Church immediately as the *Treatise* does). *([4.1](lessons/04-01-the-petrine-texts-argued.md))*
- **The *Treatise* and Calvin do not deny Rome any early standing** (Calvin grants "high honour"; the *Treatise*'s account is Boss 4(a)'s to state), and the *Treatise* is Melanchthon's, not Calvin's. *([4.2](lessons/04-02-what-the-patristic-record-proves.md))*
- ***Pastor Aeternus* did not define the history it tells**: Philip's sentence (the legate's, not Ephesus's) is taught, not defined; calling the defined primacy "a reading of the evidence" understates it. *([4.2](lessons/04-02-what-the-patristic-record-proves.md))*
- **Canon 28 is neither the whole Church's theory nor negligible**; Tierney's thesis concerns irreformable teaching, not the first-millennium primacy; "no appeals before the forged decretals" fails on Sardica (343 or 344). *([4.2](lessons/04-02-what-the-patristic-record-proves.md))*
- **"The Reformation churches rejected apostolic succession"** blurs three positions: Anglicans claimed it, the Apology wished to keep the bishops' grades, the Second Helvetic ordains with hands and places succession in doctrine. *([4.3](lessons/04-03-apostolic-succession-and-the-validity-of-orders.md))*
- ***Apostolicae Curae* did not turn on the chalice or Parker's consecration** (§§20-21 set both aside), and *Saepius Officio* compared the ancient forms and the East, not the Roman rites of 1550. "Two grounds" fails: the letter gives three supports. *([4.3](lessons/04-03-apostolic-succession-and-the-validity-of-orders.md))*
- ***Apostolicae Curae*'s level, both ways**: not a bull, a dogma, rung 1 or a CDF definition; not a disciplinary ruling freely disputed or reopened by ARCIC. Old Catholic participation does not confer validity automatically (can. 845 §2; the Leonard case). *([4.3](lessons/04-03-apostolic-succession-and-the-validity-of-orders.md))*
- **"The Reformers taught an invisible Church."** Westminster XXV.2-3, Calvin IV.1.4 and the Apology affirm a visible Church; the target is the claim that the Church in its truest sense is the hidden company. *Mystici Corporis* §14 aims at an invisible-bond ecumenism, not Westminster. *([4.4](lessons/04-04-the-visible-church-and-what-replaced-the-papacy.md))*
- **"Protestant ecclesiology" is not one position** (the Apology's true believers, Westminster's elect; two marks or three; the Crown), and **Constance did not condemn it**: it censured Hus's articles a century earlier. *([4.4](lessons/04-04-the-visible-church-and-what-replaced-the-papacy.md))*
- **The fragmentation argument needs no number**: the usual 33,000 counts bodies country by country and splits the Catholic Church into 242. *([4.4](lessons/04-04-the-visible-church-and-what-replaced-the-papacy.md))*
- **Numbering**: Westminster XXXI.3-4 are XXXI.2-3 in the American numbering; Schaff's Latin column labels Article XXXVII as XXXVI; Article XXI is absent from the American revision. *([4.4](lessons/04-04-the-visible-church-and-what-replaced-the-papacy.md), [7.1](lessons/07-01-the-inquisition-and-the-galileo-affair.md))*

### Traps on the Orthodox questions

- **"The Orthodox doubt that the Son is fully God."** They confess him consubstantial; the objection is that the Father alone is cause; the clause first served in Spain against Arians. *([5.1](lessons/05-01-the-filioque-two-questions-kept-apart.md))*
- **"The Orthodox position is Lossky's."** Photius, Gregory of Cyprus and Palamas, Florovsky and Zizioulas differ on the Son's role and on whether the filioque is the root of the ecclesial divide. *([5.1](lessons/05-01-the-filioque-two-questions-kept-apart.md))*
- **A Catholic may not call the addition unlawful** (Florence, DS 1302: rung 2, at least rung 3); the Greek recitation is discipline; concede the manner only. Citing DS 1302 to the Orthodox begs the question, and merging the two questions confuses both. *([5.1](lessons/05-01-the-filioque-two-questions-kept-apart.md))*
- **"Florence defined what the Greek Fathers meant", or "the Son is a cause in no sense"**: the first overstates a reading; the second contradicts DS 1301's *causa*. *([5.1](lessons/05-01-the-filioque-two-questions-kept-apart.md))*
- **"The Orthodox reject any primacy for Rome."** Ravenna §41, the 1895 encyclical and Zizioulas grant Rome a first place; they refuse a power inside other churches. "Primacy of honour" is neither the whole Orthodox account nor abandoned. *([5.2](lessons/05-02-papal-primacy-as-the-east-reads-it.md))*
- **Ravenna settled nothing** (§45 postponed); the "did not exercise canonical authority" sentence is Chieti §19's; UR 16's "solemnly declares" is rung 3; Romanides's thesis is a minority one. *([5.2](lessons/05-02-papal-primacy-as-the-east-reads-it.md))*
- **Arguing primacy from a universal Church that needs a universal head** argues from the premise eucharistic ecclesiology denies; argue inside its categories. Orthodoxy is not "a federation of sovereign national churches" (Alexandria §5.1). *([5.2](lessons/05-02-papal-primacy-as-the-east-reads-it.md))*
- **Answering the Orthodox only on infallibility** misses ch. 3's jurisdiction, but do not overcorrect: the 1895 encyclical and Alexandria §3.10 object to infallibility too. "Vatican I made the pope like God" is a caricature. *([5.3](lessons/05-03-vatican-i-read-by-orthodox-theologians.md))*
- **The 1875 declaration is rung 3**: it cannot narrow the definition and affirms papal power "at every time and in every place"; ch. 3's canon is rung 1. *([5.3](lessons/05-03-vatican-i-read-by-orthodox-theologians.md))*
- **The Orthodox and Protestant objections are not one**: Westminster XXXI.4 and Article XXI say councils may err; the Orthodox say a received council cannot. Reception is not a vote on either account. *([5.3](lessons/05-03-vatican-i-read-by-orthodox-theologians.md))*
- **Do not copy Alexandria §3.11's dating**: *Praeclara gratulationis* is an apostolic letter of 20 June 1894, not an encyclical of 1895. *([5.3](lessons/05-03-vatican-i-read-by-orthodox-theologians.md))*
- **The energies are not a second God, a go-between or a part of God**: his defenders deny all three; "lower divinity" is the opponents' best text, not the thesis. The Latins do not teach that grace is a thing God gives instead of himself. *([5.4](lessons/05-04-essence-and-energies.md))*
- **Rung-1 *Firmiter* and Florence do not make Palamism heresy**: the application is rung 5, and *Firmiter* predates Palamas by about eighty years. *Benedictus Deus* and Florence name neither him nor the distinction. *([5.4](lessons/05-04-essence-and-energies.md))*
- **The 1351 Tome is neither a papal-style definition nor a local opinion**: it binds by reception. Say "several Byzantine-rite Catholic churches keep his commemoration", not that "Byzantine Catholics" as such hold him a saint. The Scotist precedent shows only that the Latin acts do not exclude every non-relational distinction. *([5.4](lessons/05-04-essence-and-energies.md))*
- **"The Orthodox deny purgatory"** is a blur: they pray for the dead, Mark allowed a cleansing, nothing is defined; they refuse the fire, a debt paid after death and the treasury. *([5.5](lessons/05-05-marriage-divorce-and-the-state-of-the-dead.md))*
- **"Trent anathematized the Orthodox practice."** Can. 7 strikes whoever says the Church *errs* (worded at Venice's request); the mirror slip, "so remarriage after adultery stayed open", understates (at least rung 2). Waterworth's "dissolve" in cans. 3-4 renders *dirimere*, "make void". *([5.5](lessons/05-05-marriage-divorce-and-the-state-of-the-dead.md))*
- **"Florence defined the fire"** (it names none: rung 5); "Lyons II defined purgatory" (the profession defines nothing of itself); "the Greeks accepted purgatory" or "were tricked" (both outrun the record). Mark pressed his case at Ferrara (1438), not "at Florence". *([5.5](lessons/05-05-marriage-divorce-and-the-state-of-the-dead.md))*

### Traps on the distinctives

- **"The Orthodox reject the Immaculate Conception because they think Mary sinned"**: they call her All-Holy. Nor do they "deny original sin" (they object to inherited guilt), nor did 1895 condemn the dogma conciliarly (a synodical letter; Ware calls it opinion). *([6.1](lessons/06-01-mary.md))*
- **"The Reformers abolished Mary", or "Luther was a Marian Catholic whom Protestants abandoned"**: the confessions keep the mother of God and, in places, the ever-virgin; Lutherans are bound by the confessions, not Luther's sermons. Article XXII names no Marian doctrine. *([6.1](lessons/06-01-mary.md))*
- **"*Hyperdulia* is dogma"** (a school word; *latreia* for God alone is rung 1); "Trent defined original sin as privation of grace" (rung 4; transmission rung 5); "the Immaculate Conception means Mary needed no Saviour" (the merits clause, rung 1, says the opposite). *([6.1](lessons/06-01-mary.md))*
- **"Catholics deleted the second commandment"**: the Roman Catechism prints the whole image clause under the first, on Augustine's division, as Luther's catechisms do. The Apology grants that the saints pray for the Church, and Heidelberg Q.97 allows pictures of creatures. *([6.2](lessons/06-02-the-saints-the-images-the-relics.md))*
- **Trent's saints-and-images decree has no canons but closes its images section with an anathema** (DS 1824): "so invocation is optional" understates; "Trent defined invocation under anathema" overstates. *([6.2](lessons/06-02-the-saints-the-images-the-relics.md))*
- **Article XXII cannot be aimed at Trent XXV**, which met later (December 1563). Frankfurt (794) rejected a *latria* for images that Nicaea never taught, the misreading Calvin I.11.16 repeats; Heidelberg Q.98 already refuses Gregory's "books for the laity". *([6.2](lessons/06-02-the-saints-the-images-the-relics.md))*
- **Aquinas's "relative *latria*" for Christ's image is a school position**, not Nicaea II's wording; and Trent's "aid, (and) help" goes beyond "pray for us", which the Catholic reading must show holds in practice. *([6.2](lessons/06-02-the-saints-the-images-the-relics.md))*
- **"Protestants abolished confession"**: Lutherans kept private absolution and the Apology calls it a sacrament; the Anglican Ordinal gives priests John 20:23; Calvin commends confession to one's pastor. The Reformed do not deny that the forgiven suffer (Westminster XVII.3); they deny that it satisfies. *([6.3](lessons/06-03-penance-purgatory-and-indulgences.md))*
- **"Article XXII forbids prayer for the dead"**: it names purgatory and pardons; Westminster XXI.4, Reformed, is the text against it. Pressing 2 Maccabees as Scripture on a Reformed reader ignores 2.2; 1 Cor 3:15 does not prove purgatory "as all the Fathers agreed" (Augustine: "possibly"). *([6.3](lessons/06-03-penance-purgatory-and-indulgences.md))*
- **Levels on penance**: "Trent defined satisfaction as vengeance" (ch. 8, at least rung 3), "Trent XXV defined purgatory" (Florence, VI can. 30 and XXII can. 3 are the acts), "Article XXII is Anglican dogma" all overstate; "temporal punishment is a scholastic theory" understates (rung 1). The Hebrews argument is not a misunderstanding. *([6.3](lessons/06-03-penance-purgatory-and-indulgences.md))*
- **"Reformed" does not mean Zwingli's bare memorial**: Calvin, Heidelberg Q.76 and Westminster XXIX.7 teach a real feeding by the Spirit; Calvin shares Zwingli's figurative "is" (Consensus art. 22), not an empty sign. *([6.4](lessons/06-04-the-real-presence.md))*
- **Lutherans do not teach "consubstantiation", a body spread everywhere**: the word is not the Formula's, which rejects local extension; "in, with and under" is later shorthand. Do not swap the traditions on the unworthy (Lutherans: they receive the body to judgment; Westminster XXIX.8 and Article XXIX: they do not). *([6.4](lessons/06-04-the-real-presence.md))*
- **Aquinas's "after the manner of substance" is not Trent's definition** (ch. 1 says the manner can scarcely be expressed, at least rung 3), and the doctrine is not committed to Aristotle. Trent's eucharistic reading of John 6 is not defined (XXI ch. 1). Answering the Reformed with omnipotence alone, or by Christ leaving heaven, fails. Article XXVIII binds no Anglican like a confession and is not Zwinglian. *([6.4](lessons/06-04-the-real-presence.md))*
- **"The Reformers thought Catholics believe Christ dies again at each Mass"**: Calvin records the denial (IV.18.5); the objection is that any present offering for sins is a second oblation. Augsburg XXIV kept the Mass; the Apology and Westminster allow a sacrifice of praise, never an offering of Christ. *([6.5](lessons/06-05-the-sacrifice-of-the-mass.md))*
- **"Trent defined how the Mass is the same sacrifice"**: the identity formula is chapter teaching (at least rung 3) and every account of the *how* is rung 5; the mirror, "so the sacrifice is opinion", understates cans. 1-4. Trent reformed the traffic and defended private Masses (ch. 6; can. 8). *([6.5](lessons/06-05-the-sacrifice-of-the-mass.md))*
- **Justin and Irenaeus do not teach Trent's propitiatory doctrine** (anachronism), nor does *Dialogue* 117 prove a bare memorial. Quote Article XXXI whole, and Trent's "sins, punishments, satisfactions" whole. *Ephapax* is not at Heb 9:26 (*hapax*). *([6.5](lessons/06-05-the-sacrifice-of-the-mass.md))*
- **"Protestants reject all development"**: Mozley grants explanation and Westminster I.6 "good and necessary consequence"; the objection is addition and excess, and answering Mozley only with Newman's falsifiers meets a charge he did not make. *([6.6](lessons/06-06-development-or-corruption.md))*
- **"The Orthodox stopped in 787, or deny the Assumption"**: they received the Palamite councils and sing the Dormition; they object to "development" as a category and to defining the Dormition, and are not unanimous. *([6.6](lessons/06-06-development-or-corruption.md))*
- **The notes and Vincent's canon bind no one; *Dei Filius* ch. 4's limit does** (rung 1). A definition declares what the Church judges to be in the deposit; it does not make it revealed. "To be deep in history ..." is Newman's and proves only a negative. *([6.6](lessons/06-06-development-or-corruption.md))*

### Traps on the hard history

- **Calling every argument from silence fallacious**: silence counts where a record was expected; for the Assumption the record is recorded ignorance, not silence. *([1.3](lessons/01-03-arguing-from-history-without-abusing-it.md), [6.6](lessons/06-06-development-or-corruption.md))*
- **Quoting Jerome's Letter 15 or 146 alone as "Jerome's view of the papacy"**; repeating Coxe's claim that Jewel's challenge went unanswered (Harding, 1564); saying Gregory himself annulled John the Faster's synod (Pelagius II did; Gregory reports it with approval). *([1.3](lessons/01-03-arguing-from-history-without-abusing-it.md))*
- **"The 1616 decree was only disciplinary"**: it censured a doctrine as contrary to Scripture (rung 3 at its lightest while in force). The 1633 court's "declared and defined" did not make it a definition. The Congregation of the Index issued the 1616 decree; the Holy Office the 1633 sentence. *([7.1](lessons/07-01-the-inquisition-and-the-galileo-affair.md))*
- ***Exsurge Domine* did not define that heretics may be burned**, nor is its censure weightless (at least rung 3, no precise note); *Dignitatis Humanae* defined nothing (rung 3). *([7.1](lessons/07-01-the-inquisition-and-the-galileo-affair.md))*
- **"The state did the burning, so the Church is clear"**: relaxation excused no one. The definition distinction answers only the doctrinal charge, never the moral one. Give the range, not a single number, for Spanish Inquisition executions. *([7.1](lessons/07-01-the-inquisition-and-the-galileo-affair.md))*
- **The *Consilium* is not papal teaching or "the cardinals' verdict"**: it has no magisterial level; who signed it and what kind of document it is are Boss 7(c)'s to state. *([7.2](lessons/07-02-the-reformations-grievances.md))*
- **The disciplinary/doctrinal distinction was not invented at Trent**: Augsburg (1530) already separates articles of faith from abuses. Calling the treasury defined overstates it (rung 3). *([7.2](lessons/07-02-the-reformations-grievances.md))*
- **The reformers' own persecutions do not cancel the grievances**: the counter-question tests only a claim to purity, and used as a defence it is the comparative move. Cite the Augsburg abuse articles as XXII-XXVIII from the Triglot, not Schaff's I-VII. *([7.2](lessons/07-02-the-reformations-grievances.md))*
- **"It happened everywhere"**: as a comparison of rates it is unestablished almost everywhere, where France measured it the Church came out highest outside family and friends, and a true comparison would still be irrelevant. *([7.3](lessons/07-03-the-abuse-crisis-and-the-argument-from-scandal.md))*
- **Indefectibility overstated or understated**: it does not promise good governance and is not "defined"; nor is it "only an opinion" (at least rung 3, silent on conduct). *([7.1](lessons/07-01-the-inquisition-and-the-galileo-affair.md), [7.3](lessons/07-03-the-abuse-crisis-and-the-argument-from-scandal.md))*
- ***Vos estis* and the 2019 rescript are law, not doctrine or proof of holiness**: acts judged by what changes. Francis did not call clericalism "the root": the Letter says it "supports and helps to perpetuate" the evils. *([7.3](lessons/07-03-the-abuse-crisis-and-the-argument-from-scandal.md))*
- **Treating *Dei Filius* ch. 3's sign as untouched by the record**: the record is real evidence against the sign, and that is the crux. Do not call the Belgic Confession Lutheran. *([7.3](lessons/07-03-the-abuse-crisis-and-the-argument-from-scandal.md))*
